attracted by the things I dream up. Let's say that they are gifted
in that line of things. The strange thing is - here I let myself
betray their confidences - that they make progress, they tell me,
by discussing it together. Thinking is certainly not usually done
à deux. The fact is, they manage it, and for a long time they have



- 122 -

been producing things about the borromean knot which seem to
me more than interesting- indeed, an achievement. This
discovery is certainly not the crowning glory. they will make
more.

在对这种新几何进行任何想象的思考时所产生的阻力
（resistence），让我印象深刻，因为我经历过它。苏里和托
米对我的教学中出现的想象、象征和实在的结合着迷，这
当然不是纯粹的偶然，他们特别被我所幻想的事物所吸引。
他们在这方面似乎确实有天赋。
奇怪的是——在这里我感到自己背叛了他们的信心—
—他们告诉我，通过两人一起（à deux）讨论，他们取得了
进步。 思考是当然通常不会两人一起（à deux）完成的。事
实是，他们做到了，而且很长一段时间以来，他们一直在
研究有关波罗米结的东西，在我看来，这不仅很有趣，而
且是一项成就。这一发现当然不是至高无上的荣耀。不过
他们会有更多的成就。

I will not add what Soury has told me of his thoughts about
teaching. In this matter, I think that by following my example,
which I described a moment ago, he will certainly manage as
well as I am able to; that is, in the same risky fashion. But that



- 123 -

dialogue should prove fertile, especially in this field, is a
confirmation of my need of it. For the two months I spent
relentlessly looking for the fourth triple knot and the way it
could form a borromean knot with the three others. I was alone
in this search, I mean in this hopeful thinking-through. It is time
for me to talk of the significance this research has for me.

我不会补充苏里对教学的看法。在这件事上，我认为
按照我刚才描述的例子，他一定会像我一样做到；也就是
说，以同样危险的方式。但这种对话应该证明是富有成效
的，尤其是在这个领域，是我对它需要的确认。两个月来，
我一直在不懈地寻找第四个三重结，以及用这第四个结与
其他三个结结成波罗米结的方法。只有我一个人在研究这
一问题，我的意思是在这个充满希望的思考中。现在是时
候谈谈。这项研究对我的意义了。

2

This research is extremely significant to me for the
following reason. The three circles of the borromean knot are all
three, as circles, equivalent - I mean that they are constituted by
something which is reproduced across the three.



- 124 -


由于以下原因，这项研究对我来说非常重要。 波罗米
结的三个圆都是三个，作为等同的圆——我的意思是它们
是由贯穿在三个结之间重复的东西构成的。

I situate the support of consistence in the imaginary.
Likewise, I make the essential constituent of the symbolic the
hole. And I make the real the support of what I term ex-sistence,
in this sense: in its sistence outside of the imaginary and the
symbolic, it knocks up against them, its play is something
precisely in the order of limitation; the two others, from the
moment when it is tied into a borromean knot with them, offer it
resistance. In other words the real only has ex-sistence in rather
an astonishing formulation of mine -in its encounter with the
limits of the symbolic and the imaginary.

我将对一致性的支撑置于想象中。同样，我将象征的
基本组成部分设为洞（trou）。在这个意义上，我使实在成
为我所谓的外—实存的支撑：存在于想象和象征之外，实
在与它们相撞，它完全运作（joue）19在有限制的秩序中；
其他两个，从它与它们打成一个波罗米结的那一刻起，就

19 Jouer 也有玩，扮演的意思



- 125 -

对实在产生阻力（resistance）。换句话说，实在只外—实存
于它与象征和想象的界限的相遇中。



To be sure, as much should be said of the two others - for
example, it is to the extent that it ex-sists in the real that the
imaginary also encounters conflict, which is here better felt.
Why then do l place this ex-sistence precisely where it can
appear most paradoxical? Because I have to redistribute these
three modes, and it is exactly this ex-sistence which supports the
thinking of the real

可以肯定的是，对于象征和想象来说，应该说的也是
如此——例如，在想象外—实存于实在的程度上，想象也
遇到了冲突，在这里我们能更好地感受到这一点。那么我



- 126 -

为什么要把这种外—实存（ex-sistence）精确地放在它看起
来最自相矛盾的地方呢？因为我必须重新分配这三个域，
而正是这种外—实存支撑着对实在的思考。

But what is the result of this? - if not, that these three terms
should be conceived as linked to each other. If they are so
analogous, to use this term,could one not suppose that this is due
to a certain continuity? And thus we are led directly to tie the
triple knot.' starting from the inter-balancing, inter-leaving of the
three, it is not a great step to link up the points and make their
arrangement one of continuity.
But what is the result if what is of the order of subject, in as
much as the subject is always merely supposé, finds in the end
its support in this knot? Is the triple knot tied into a borromean
knot enough? My question concerns this point.

但这样做的结果是什么呢？——如果不是，这三个术
语应该被认为是相互关联的。如果它们如此相似，使用这
个术语，难道不能假设这是由于某种连续性吗？因此，我
们被直接引导去打三重结。从三者的相互平衡（inter-
balancing）、相互出离（inter-leaving）开始，将点连起来，
使它们的排列具有连续性，而这并不是什么了不起的事。



- 127 -

尽管主体永远只是假设，但是，如果属于主体秩序的
东西，最终在这个结中找到它的支撑，结果会是什么？三
重结是否系在一个波罗米结上就足够了？我的问题主要关
注这一点。

In the diagram of a borromean chain, does it not seem that
the minimum is always constituted by a quadruple knot? Pull on
the green cord and you will see that the black cord, here knotted
to the red cord, is pulled by the blue cord, giving the perceived
form of a borromean chain. It seems that the least one can
expect from the borromean chain is this relation of one to three
others. And if we suppose that the .triple knots link up with one
another in borromean knots, we will get to this - that a fourth
will always be propped upon [prendra appui] three supports,
which we will call here subjective - in other words,
personal.You remember how I introduced this fourth element.
Each of the three others are supposed to constitute something of
the personal. In respect of these three, the fourth will be what
this year I am terming the sinthome.

在图中，难道最小的波罗米结不总是要由四重结构成
吗？拉动绿圈，您会看到黑圈，这里与红圈打结，被蓝圈



- 128 -

拉动，呈现出一种可感知的链条形式。似乎从波罗米结中
至少可以看到这种一到三个他者的关系。
如果我们假设三个结在波罗米结中相互连接，我们将
得到这个——有一个第四项将始终支撑于三个支撑之上，
我们将这个第四项称之为主观的（subjectifs）——换句话说，
个体的（personnels）。你记得我是如何介绍这第四项要素
（quart élément）的。其他三个中的每一个都应该构成个人
的某些东西。这第四项将是今年我所说的圣状。



Borromean relation of 1 to 3

1 至3 的波罗米式联系



- 129 -



There is a reason why I have written in a certain order RSI,
SIR, IRS.

我按特定顺序写 RSI、SIR、IRS 是有原因的。这与我
去年的标题 RSI（实在，象征，想象）相对应



3

Likewise Soury and Thomé have highlighted, as I
mentioned last year, that in relation to the borromean knot there
are two different types from the moment when it is given an
orientation and colours.



- 130 -

What does this difference put in place [ que suppose-t-elle]?
Not that the identity of either is marked by an initial letter. The
distinction between them which has effects in their orientation
can only be traced in the way in which their difference is
marked by colour - not the difference of one from another, but,
as it were, their absolute difference, difference common to all
three. It is to the extent that something which is one marks as
such the difference between all three and not that between any
two, that the distinction between the two structures of the
borromean knot appears.

同样，正如我去年提到的，苏里和托米已经强调，从
给定方向和颜色的那一刻起，波罗米结有两种不同的类型。
这种差异是怎么实现的？并不是说两者的身份都由首
字母标记出来的。它们之间的区别对它们的方向产生了影
响，而这种区别只能通过用颜色标记它们的差异的方式来
追寻——不是彼此之间的差异，而是它们的绝对差异，是
三者共有的差异。正是在这种意义上，有种“一”标志着三者
之间的差异，而不是任何两者的差异，这才出现了波罗米
结的两个结构之间的区别。

Which is the true one? Which is true in respect of the way



- 131 -

the imaginary, the symbolic and the real are knotted together
into the support of the subject? This is the question which
should be posed. One should refer back to my preceding
comments on the duality of the borromean knot to gauge its
importance, for I have only been able to touch on it for a
moment today. What is remarkable is that the triple knot shows
no trace of this difference. There is only one kind of this triple
knot, in which the imaginary, the symbolic and the real are in
continuity, and which is thus an homogenisation of the
borromean knot. Check this yourself.

哪个是真的？关于想象、象征和实在结合在一起成为
主体性支撑的方式，哪一个是正确的？这才是应该提出的
问题。人们应该参考我之前关于波罗米结的二元性（二重
性）的评论来衡量它的重要性，因为我今天只能稍微谈谈
它。值得注意的是，三重结没有显示出这种差异的痕迹。
这种三重结只有一种形态，想象、象征和实在是连续的，
因此这是波罗米结中的同质化（homogenisation）。你可以自
己去想想。

However, it is well known that there are two triple knots,
depending on whether the knot turns to the left or to the right.



- 132 -

What, then, is the link between the two types of Borromean knot
and the two types of triple knot? Whatever it is, if the triple knot
is really the support of all kinds of subject, how can it be put in
question? How can it be put in question such that it really
concerns a subject?
There was a time when my progress took a certain route,
before l got onto that of analysis, which is witnessed by my
thesis - paranoiac psychosis and its relation, as I put it, to the
personality. If I have been opposed to the republication of this
book for so long, this is due to something simple: there is no
relation between paranoiac psychosis and the.personality.
Because it's the same thing. In so far as a subject makes a triple
knot of the imaginary, the symbolic and the real, its only support
is their continuity; all three have one and the same consistence.
And this is what paranoiac psychosis consists of.

然而，众所周知，有两个三重结，取决于结是向左还
是向右。那么，两种波罗米结和两种三重结之间的联系是
什么？不管是什么，如果三重结真的是各种主体的支撑，
那怎么可能有问题呢？怎样才能对它提出一个真正关于主
体质疑？
曾经有一段时间，我采取了一个确定的路线，然后才



- 133 -

进入分析的道路，我的论文证明了这一点《论偏执型精神
病及其与个性的关系》（De la psychose paranoïaque dans ses
rapports avec la personnalité）。如果我长期以来一直反对再
版这本书，原因很简单：偏执型精神病与个性无关。因为
这是同一件事。只要一个主体将想象、象征和实在打成三
重结，它的唯一支撑就是它们的连续性。三者都具有相同
的一致性。这就是偏执型精神病的构成。

To understand correctly what I'm proposing today, one
could deduce the following: a fourth term could be knotted as a
symptom to the paranoiac three, a term which would be situated
as personality，distinct from the three prior personalities, and
their symptom.
Is this to say that this too would be paranoiac? Nothing
indicates this when it is a matter of a borromean chain made up
of an indefinite number of triple knots. Such a chain no longer
constitutes a paranoia, if only because it is common. The
possible terminal flocculation of fourth terms in this braid, this
tress of subjectivity, allows us to suppose that there are certain
chosen points in the totality of the texture which function as the
term of this quadruple knot. And this is exactly what the
sinthome consists in - not in so far as it is personality but that in



- 134 -

relation to the three others it specifies itself as sinthome and
neurotic.

为了正确理解我今天提出的建议，可以推断出以下几
点：三种偏执可以结成症状，而第四个项将被定位为一种
个性，因为它本身将和前三种个性与它们的症状截然不同。
这是说这也是偏执的吗？当它是由无限数量的三重结
组成的波罗米结的问题时，没有任何迹象表明这一点。这
样的链条不再构成偏执，而这只是因为它很常见。这条辫
子中第四项的可能最终絮凝
20 （The possible terminal
flocculation），这主体性的长辫（tress of subjectivity），使我
们能够假设在整个结构（texture）中存在某些选定的点，它
们充当这个四重结的项。而这正是圣状所存在的位置——
不是其个性，而是在其它三个结的联系之中，它把自己指
认为圣状和神经症（neurotic）。


20 拉康的隐喻指的是主体的编织物（辫子、编结）的概念，其中某些股簇成簇
或丛；在“絮凝”下，腔室“聚集成簇状、片状或云状”。有关拉康絮凝的更多
信息，请参阅SVII，《精神分析伦理学》，同前，第102 页。



- 135 -


Two pairs in the four-component Borromean link

四组分波罗米链环中的两对

Here we are given an insight into the nature of the
unconscious. There is a term which is particularly connected to
it which has a privileged relation to the sinthome. In this set of
four, you see two couples are formed, red-green and blue-red. I
read that as a link between the sinthome and the unconscious,
and between the imaginary and the real. That is where the
sinthome emerges.

在这里，我们可以深入了解无意识的本质。有一个与
它特别相关的术语，它与圣状有着特殊的关系。在这四组
中，你看到了两对组合，红绿和蓝红。我把它看作是圣状
和无意识之间以及想象和实在之间的联系。这就是圣状显



- 136 -

现的地方。

4

These are difficult. things I wanted to propose to you today.
To complement this, I should certainly speak of why I have now
opened up the triple knot, and am not giving it the usual circular
form. I have already noted the field of J(Ⱥ) [barred A]. This is
jouissance, and it is not that of the Other, because there is no
Other of the Other; in the symbolic, site of the Other as such,
there are no oppositions.

我今天想向你提的这些东西都很难。为了进行补充，
我当然应该谈谈为什么我现在解开了三重结，而不是用平
时圆环来呈现。因为我已经注意到J(Ⱥ)的领域，这是原乐
（jouissance），不是大他者的原乐，因为没有大他者的大他
者；在大他者的象征性场所中，没有对立。




- 137 -


The three central fields of the RSI diagram

RSI 图的三个中心区域

The jouissance of the Other of the Other is impossible for
the simple reason that no such instance exists. All that remains,
then, is what is produced in the field of the intersection of the
circle of the symbolic and the circle of the imaginary: meaning.
Elsewhere there is so-called phallic jouissance, to be
distinguished from any jouissance of the penis. The jouissance
of the double, of the specular image or imaginary body, is the
support of a certain number of béances ['gaps/abeyances'],
which constitute its different objects. By contrast, the jouissance
of the phallus, J(Φ) ,is located at the conjunction of the symbolic
and the real, and is experienced as a parasite by the subject
supported by the speaking-being [parlêtre], in the sense of what



- 138 -

I designate as the unconscious. I mark it in balance over against
meaning. This is the site consciously designated as power by the
speaking-being.

大他者的大他者的原乐是不可能的，原因很简单，即
不存在这样的例子。那么，剩下的就是在象征圆环和想象
圆环的交叉领域中产生的东西：意义（sens）。
在其他地方有所谓的菲勒斯的原乐（phallic jouissance），
这与阴茎原乐（jouissance of the penis）区分开来。双重的，
镜像的或想象性的身体的原乐，是一部分断裂的支撑，它
们构成了它的不同对象（客体）。相比之下，菲勒斯原乐
（the jouissance of the phallus），J(Φ)，则位于象征和实在的
结合处。菲勒斯原乐被言说的存在[parlêtre]（也就是我所指
定的无意识意义上的存在）所支撑的主体把握为寄生性的
原乐。我将其标记为与意义之间的平衡。这是被言说的存
在有意识地指定的场域，比如，权力。






- 139 -

Meaning-jouissance diagram

意义–原乐图

The similarity is that the three rings participate in the
imaginary on the level of consistence, in the symbolic as hole,
and in the real as ex-sistent to these. The three rings imitate one
another.
The fact that they do not imitate each other in a simple way
makes this so much more difficult. In fact they make up a triple
knot. Thus, after making the discovery that triple knots can be
tied into triple borromean knots, I took care to note that if they
are kept unconstrained, a triple knot exists whose plays extends
across its full texture; a knot which is well and truly a fourth
term, and which is called sinthome.

相似之处在于，三个环在一致性的层面上参与想象，
在象征中作为洞（trou），在实在中作为外—实存。三环相
互模仿。
他们不会以简单的方式互相模仿，这一事实使得问题
变得更加困难。事实上，它们组成了一个三重结。因此，
在发现三重结可以系成三重波罗米结之后，我注意到如果



- 140 -

它们不受约束，就会存在一个三重结，外—实存着出现在
其结构中；而这就是第四项，被我称为圣状（sinthome）。

1975 年12 月6 日





- 141 -

IV
JOYCE AND THE FOX RIDDLE
乔伊斯和狐狸之谜
The real bears no meaning
Mentality and the adoration of the body
Joyce deep-rooted in the father he disowns
Making a splice between sinthome and jouissance

实在不承载着任何意义
精神和对身体的崇拜
乔伊斯深深的根植于他所断绝关系的父亲之中
在圣状和原乐之间制造一个拼接

One's responsibility only goes as far as one's savoir-faire.
What is savoir-faire?-Let's say it is what gives a remarkable
value to one's art. Why remarkable? Because there is no Other
of the Other to pass the final judgement. This means that there is
something we cannot enjoy. Let us call it the jouissance of God,
including in this the sense of sexual jouissance.

一个人的责任只限于自己的“做的知识”（savoir-faire）。



- 142 -

什么是做的知识？——比方说它给一个人的艺术以非凡的
价值。为什么非凡？因为在这里没有大他者的大他者
（Other of the Other）来宣告最终的判决。这意味着有一些
东西是我们不能享乐（enjoy）的。让我们称它为上帝的原
乐（jouissance），其中包括性的原乐的意义。

Supposing that he exists, does our image of God indicate
that he enjoys his creation? To answer that he doesn't exist is to
deal too briskly with the question, if we take on the burden of a
thought whose essence is to take its place in this reality -a first
approximation of the word real which has a different meaning in
my vocabulary -in this limited reality which testifies to the
exsistence of sex.

假设上帝外—实存，我们对上帝形象的想象是否表明
祂享受他自己的创造？而回答说祂并不外—实存着，就是
太过轻率地处理这个问题，如果我们承担了一个思想的负
担，而这个负担的本质是要在这个现实（reality）中占据一
席之地——一个近似的词，real，在我的词汇里有着不同的
意义——在这个证明了性的存在的有限的现实中。

These are the first truths I bring you this new year ...



- 143 -

这是我在新的一年里带给你们的第一个真理。

That's not such a bad thing for a new year. Not that I
haven't been labouring away over the break since we last met
some three weeks ago now. I've been working on a few things, a
sample of which you can see up here on the blackboard.

对于新的一年来说，这并不是一件坏事。并不是说自
从我们三周前最后一次见面以来，我就一直在休息。我一
直在做一些事情，你可以在黑板上看到一个例子。


New figuration of a Borromean link

波罗米链环的新外形




- 144 -

This, as you can see, is a Borromean knot. 1 It is no less of
one than the one I usually draw, and which is put together as
follows.

如你所见，这是一个波罗米结。它和我通常画的一样，
我把这个和接下来图放在一起。


Usual figuration of the Borromean link

波罗米链环的通常外形

The first only differs from the second on account of one
thing, which is by no means negligible, namely that this second
one can be stretched out in such a way that there is a ring at each
of the two extremities, with the one in the middle forming the
join.



- 145 -


第一幅图与第二幅图不同之处在于一点，这一点绝不
可忽略，即第二幅图可以以这样的方式延伸，即在两个末
端的每一个都有一个环，中间的一个形成结点。


Figuration of the middle ring

中环的构型

The difference is this. Suppose if you will that three
elements like this middle ring come together in a circular way.
You can see, I hope, how this can be made without me having to
trace it out for you on the board. This can be simplified like the
one that I traced out first, or else as follows. This is the same
knot.




- 146 -

区别在于此。假设你愿意，像这个中环这样的三个要
素可以以一种圆形的方式聚集在一起。我希望，你可以看
到，这是如何做到的，而不用我在黑板上为你寻找答案。
这可以简化为如下所示。这是同一个结。


Another figuration of a Borromean link

波罗米链环的另一种图示

Naturally, I wouldn't make do with this alone. I spent my
holidays dreaming up a good many more of these in the hope of
finding one good one that would serve as an easy support for
what I've started to tell you today by way of home truths.



- 147 -


当然，我不会单独处理这个问题。我在假期里一直想
着更多这样的事情，希望能找到一个好的例子，作为我今
天和你们讨论的大实话（home truth）21。

Well, and here's the surprising thing, this doesn't happen all
on its own. Not that I think I'm wrong to have found in the knot
the support of our consistence. Only, this is already a sign that I
can deduce this knot only from a link, namely, something that is
not at all of the same nature. A link is not the same thing as a
knot. Let's get back, though, to the ticking over of these truths
that I've called home truths.

好吧，这是一件令人惊讶的事，这件事不是完全是它
自己发生的。我并不认为我错了，在这个结中找到了我们
的一致性的支持。只是，这已经是一个迹象，表明我只能
从一个链接中推断出这个结，也就是说，某个根本不具有
相同性质的东西。链接（link）和结（knot）不是一回事。
不过，让我们回到我所谓的“大实话”进展上来。

The very first outline of what is called thought, everything

21 原文为vérité première，即首要的真相



- 148 -

which produces meaning from the moment of its first
appearance, comprises a reference to, a gravitation towards, the
sexual act -however little that act is in evidence. The very word
act implies the polarity active/passive, which is already to get
caught In a false sense. This is how knowledge is described,
with the following ambiguity that what is active is that which
we know, but because of the effort we make to know，it is
ourselves we imagine to be active.

所谓思想的第一个轮廓，所有的物自其第一次显现的
时刻便产生了意义，它们包括一个指向——性行为——无
论这个行动显然是怎样的微不足道。这个单词——行为
（act），它暗示了极端的主动（active）/被动，它已经把我
们带到了错误的意义上。这就是知识被描述的方式，且往
往伴有歧义，即那个主动的东西（what is active）是我们所
知道的，但我们却“主动的”去努力获得知识，而我们想象中
那个主动的东西正是我们自己。

So knowledge, from the outset, reveals its true nature -its
deceptiveness. This is why, from the outset, everything should
be reconsidered in terms of the opaqueness of the sexual. I use
the word 'opaqueness' because we do not see in the sexual any



- 149 -

relation, founded on anything at all. This implies，following the
movement of thought, that there is no responsibility - in the
sense where 'responsibility' means non-response side by side
with response - there is no responsibility other than sexual
responsibility, something in the end everyone feels.

因此，知识从一开始就揭示了它的真实本质——它的
欺骗性。这就是为什么从一开始，鉴于性的晦暗
（opaqueness），一切都应该被重新考虑。我使用“晦暗”这
个词，因为我们在性行为中看不到任何建立在某些东西之
上的关系。这意味着，随着思想的运动，没有责任
（responsibility）存在——在“责任”（responsibility）意味着
无回应（non-response）和回应（response）并列的意义上—
—除了性的责任之外没有任何责任，这是最终每个人都能
感受到的。

What I have termed savoir-faire goes far beyond this,
adding artifice, which we ascribe to God - absolutely
gratuitously, as Joyce insists, because this is a point which
tickled (what we describe as) his thought. God is not the author
of this thing we call the universe. What we impute to God is the
business of the artist. The first model for this, as is well known,



- 150 -

is the potter, who is said to have moulded (with what, though?)
this thing called, not by accident, the universe - which means
only one thing, that there is One.

我所称的“做的知识”远远超越了这一点，我所谓的诀窍
远远超出了我们无端地归咎于上帝的诡计——正如乔伊斯
所坚持的那样，是绝对无端的，因为这一点触动了（我们
所描述的）他的思想。上帝并不是这个我们称之为宇宙的
东西的创造者。我们归咎于上帝的东西是艺术家干的。众
所周知，这方面的第一个模范是，陶匠（the potter）22，据
说他塑造了（虽然是用什么？）的这个东西叫做宇宙，这
不是偶然的——它只意味着一件事，有这么一个“一”（Y a
d’l’un）。

There is One, but we do not know where. It is more than
unlikely that this One constitutes the universe. The real - that is,
impossible - Other of the Other, is our idea of artifice, in that it
is an activity which escapes us, in other words which far
exceeds the enjoyment [jouissance] we can have of it. This
absolutely slender jouissance is what we call spirit [l'esprit].

22 在以赛亚的祷告中，他为主宣告的一个特别的名字，the potter。原文如下：
“耶和华啊！现在祢仍是我们的父。我们是泥，祢是窑匠。我们都是祢手的作
品”（赛64:8）



- 151 -


有个“（太）一”，但我们不知道在哪里。这个“（太）
一”是不可能构成了宇宙的。实在——即，不可能性——大
他者的大他者，是我们观念的狡计，因为这是一种逃离我
们的活动，换句话说，这远远超过了我们对它所能拥有的
原乐[jouissance]。这种绝对纤细的原乐，我们称之为精神
[l'esprit]。

All of this implies a notion of the real which we must
distinguish from the symbolic and the imaginary. The only
trouble is that in this process the real is given meaning, whereas
in fact the real is founded to the extent that there is no meaning,
that it excludes meaning, or, more precisely, that it is deposited
in this exclusion.

所有这些都暗示了一个实在的概念，我们必须将它与
象征和想象区分开来。唯一的问题是，在这个过程中，实
在被赋予了意义，而实际上，实在是建立在没有意义的范
围中的，它排除了意义，或者，更准确地说，它被沉淀在
这种排除之中。

 There you are. I tell you this as I think it, for your



- 152 -

knowledge. The form most devoid of meaning, but which is
nevertheless imagined, is consistence. Bear in mind this: that
nothing forces us to imagine consistence.

就是这样。我一边想着一边向你们讲述，为了你们的
知识。最缺乏意义但仍然被想象的形式，是一致性。请记
住：没有什么能迫使我们想象一致性。

I have here a book by Robert M. Adams, called Surface and
Symbol, whose sub-title indicates what is at issue - The
consistency of James Joyce's Ulysses '. This is a kind of
presentiment of the distinction between the imaginary and the
symbolic. Thus there is a chapter which places a question-mark
after Surface or Symbol, surface or symbol.

我这里有一本罗伯特·M·亚当斯的书，叫做《表象与象
征》（Surface and Symbol），它的副标题点出了争论的焦点
——詹姆斯·乔伊斯的《尤利西斯》的一致性。这是对想象
界和象征界之间的差异的一种预感。因此，有一章《在表
象与象征、表象与象征》（Surface or Symbol, surface or
symbol）之后放置了一个问号。




- 153 -

What does consistence mean? It means what holds things
together, and this is certainly why it is symbolised here by the
surface. Our only idea of consistence, poor souls that we are, is
what makes a bag or a cloth. We even feel the body as merely
skin holding in its bag a heap of organs.

一致性是什么意思？它意味着某个把事物结合在一起
的东西，这当然就是为什么它在这里被表象所象征着的原
因。我们唯一对于一致性的想法，我们所是的那些可怜的
灵魂，是制造一个袋子或一块布的材料。我们甚至觉得身
体就像皮肤做成的包一样，这个包里装着一堆器官。

To put it in other terms, this consistence reveals the cord.
But the Imagination's powers of abstraction are so weak that It
excludes from this cord, the residue of consistence, the knot.

换句话说，这种一致性揭示了绳索。但是想象的抽象
力是如此的弱，以至于它被排除在这条绳索之外，即一致
性的剩余部分，也就是结。

It is on this point that I wish to add the only grain of salt I
would perhaps accept responsibility for - the knot is the only



- 154 -

thing which ex-sists, properly speaking, in the cord. It is not
without hidden reason that I have had to provide you with
access to this knot by starting with the chain, whose elements
are distinct. These elements have their consistence in the form of
the Cord - or rather line, which we must suppose to be infinite
so that the knot doesn't come undone, or rope-loop, in other
words a cord which is tied together, or more exactly is spliced
together. The knot does not constitute the consistence, it ex­sists
in the cord element, the consistent cord.

正是在这一点上，我想补充一些东西——结是唯一“外
—实存”（ex­sists）的东西，更准确地说，在绳结里。这并
不是没有隐藏的原因，通过从要素各不相同的链条开始，
我必须为你们提供去往这个结的路径。这些要素以绳的形
式获取一致性（或者说线），我们必须认为它是无限的，这
样结，或者说绳结，就不会解开，换句话说，绳子被绑在
一起，或者更确切地说是拼接在一起。结不构成一致性，
它外—实存（ex­sists）于绳索的要素，那个一致的绳索。

So a knot can be tied. If I have taken the path of stitching-
together the elements, this is because I thought it more didactic,
given the mentality proper to the speaking-being [parletre],



- 155 -

senti-mentality, because he feels it [iI la sent], he feels the
burden. It is also the mentality in so far as he lies [il ment]. It is
a fact that he lies. What is a fact? It is precisely he who makes it
[le fait]. There is no fact Without the fact that the speaking-
being says so. There are no facts other than those the speaking-
being recognises as such by speaking about them. There is no
fact without artifice, and it is a fact that he lies, in other words
that he accords recognition to false facts; because he has
mentality, in other words self-love [amour-propre]. This is the
principle of imagination. He adores his body. He adores it
because he thinks he possesses it. In reality he does not. But his
body is his only - mental, of course - consistence. His body is
always buggering off. It's already fairly miraculous that it
subsists for a time, for the time of this consummation which in
fact - due to the fact of its being said - is inexorable, because
nothing can be done, it cannot be reduced. It is an observed fact
even in animals that the body does not evaporate, it is consistent.
And this is what is disagreeable to the mentality, entirely
because it believes it has a body to adore. This is the root of the
imaginary. I think this -p-a-n-s-e- in other words, I make a
bandage of it, thus I clean it [l'essuie]. That's what this comes
down to. It is the sexual which lies [ment] in this due to too



- 156 -

much talk about itself, without the above-mentioned imaginary
abstraction, which boils down to consistence. What is concrete,
all that we Know, is always sexual adoration, that is to say,
misunderstanding [meprise], in other words contempt [mepris].
The object of adoration is supposed, except in the case of God,
to have no mentality at all, which is only true of the body
considered as such - I mean adored, because that is the only
relation of the speaking-being to his body; so that it is always
suspicious when he adores another body, for this entails the
same contempt, a true contempt, because it is a question of truth.
What is truth {as someone else said)? What is it, to say - as,
when I started my bullshitting, they reproached me for not
saying - the truth about truth? It is to do no more than I have
effectively done - to follow the trace of the real, the real which
only consists and ex-sists in the knot.

于是我们可以打上一个结。如果我采取了把要素都缝
合在一起的方法，这是因为我认为这更有教育意义，考虑
到言说的存在[parlêtre]特有的心态，多愁善感，因为它感觉
到了它[iI la sent]，它感到了负担。这也是它撒谎[il ment]时
的一种心态。事实（fact ）是他说谎了。什么是事实
（fact）？恰恰是它（言说的存在所说的事实）创造了它



- 157 -

（事实）[le fait]。没有言说的存在（the speaking-being）如
此说出的事实,就没有事实。除了言说的存在通过谈论他们
而认识到的事实之外，没有其他事实。没有什么事实是没
有技巧的，这个事实是他在撒谎，换句话说，他承认了虚
假的事实；因为他有某种心态，换句话说，就是自爱
[amour-propre]。这就是想象的法则。他崇拜他的身体。他
很喜欢它，因为他认为自己拥有它。实际上，他并不拥有。
但他的身体是他的唯一——当然是精神上的——的一致性。
他的身体总是正在溜走（buggering off）。相当神奇的是它能
存在一段时间, 因为这一完成的时候（the time of this
consummation），事实上——由于它被说出的事实——是不
可阻挡的，因为没有什么可以做，它不能减少。即使在动
物身上也可以观察到，身体不会突然消失（evaporate），它
是一致的。而这是这种心态所厌恶的，完全是因为它相信
自己有一个值得崇拜的身体。这是想象界的根源。我认为
这个-p-a-n-s-e-23，换句话说，我给它包扎了个绷带，这样
我就把它擦掉[l'essuie]了。这就是这个问题的症结所在。性
是由于太多的讨论它自己，而没有上述想象的抽象，它可
以归结为一致性，性就存在于谎言之中（lies[ment] in ment
有说谎的意思）。具体的，我们所知道的一切，总是性崇拜
（sexual adoration），也就是说，误认[meprise]，换句话说，

23 拉康这里玩的是Penser（思考）和panser（包扎）的双关



- 158 -

蔑视[mepris]。崇拜的对象，除了上帝，都被认为是没有精
神的，这只适用于被认为如此的身体——我的意思是，被
崇拜的（身体），因为这是言说的存在与他自己的身体之间
唯一的联系；所以当他崇拜另一个人的身体时，他总是怀
疑的，因为这牵涉到同样的蔑视，一种真正的蔑视，因为
这是一个真理的问题。（正如别人说的）什么是真理？当我
开始胡扯的时候，他们责备我没有说——关于真理的真理
（the truth about truth），那该说些什么呢？要做的不过是我
事实上已经做了的——去追随实在的痕迹，那只存在于
（consists）并且外—实存（ex-sists）于结的实在。

2

Haste has a function - I must go hastily. Naturally I won't
get to the end, although I haven't dawdled. But tying the knot
carelessly simply means going a bit fast.

匆忙（Haste）有一个作用——我必须赶快讲完这些。
当然，我不会一口气讲到最后，虽然我并没有在磨蹭。但
我在打结时不用心确实意味着我们进行得有点快。

Let us stick to the principle that there is no sexual relation,



- 159 -

which I was led to by hysteria, in so far as it is, as Freud saw,
the final perceptible reality concerning the sexual relation,
precisely the final usteron. From this Freud learned the b-a-ba,
which didn't stop him from posing the question WwdW - Was
will das Weib? This was a mistake. He thought that there was
das Weib; there is only ein Weib.
WwdW
WweW

让我们坚持不存在性关系的原则，这里我被癔症所引
导，因为正如弗洛伊德所见，癔症是关于性关系的，最终
的可感现实，确切的说是最终的usteron（希腊语，ὕστερος：
latter,coming after,有之后的意思）。从中弗洛伊德认识到了
它背后的基础，但这并没有阻止他提出这个问题WwdW—
—女性想要什么？（Was will das Weib?）这是一个错误的问
题。他认为有“女性”（das Weib 即拉康所说的La femme，
普遍的女性）；但是这里只有一个女性（ein Weib，即la
femme）。
WwdW  Was will das Weib “女性”想要什么
WweW  Was will ein Weib  一个女人想要什么

Anyway, now I'm going to give you a little bit to get your



- 160 -

teeth into. I would like to illustrate this with something which
supports it, and which is certainly what is in question. I have
already spoken at one point about enigma [,énigme, also 'riddle'],
which I write Ee. It's a matter of énonciation and énoncé. An
enigma is an énonciation whose énoncé cannot be located.

不管怎样，现在我要给你们一点点空间，好让你们能
明白。我想用一些例子来说明这一点，当然是讨论中的例
子。我曾经说过谜，[énigme（谜），或riddle 谜]，我把它写
作Ee。这是一个话（陈诉）（énonciation）与说（énoncé）
之间的问题。谜是一种说（énoncé）无法定位的话（陈述）
（énonciation）。

You will find in R·M·Adams' book something of value -
that, in the opening chapters of Ulysses, when he goes to teach
at Trinity College in front of that diminutive nation which makes
up a class, Stephen - in other words Joyce as he imagines
himself (and who, since he's not a fool, he's not in love with - on
the contrary, he need only mention Stephen and he starts
giggling, which is not very far from my position when I talk, at
least about what I'm chattering on about to you ... ) ...




- 161 -

你会在R·M·亚当斯的书中发现一些有价值的东西——
在《尤利西斯》的开头章节中，当他去三一学院任教时，
面对的是组成了一个班级的小国家，斯蒂芬——换句话说
乔伊斯他想象着自己（或者想象着某人，因为他不是一个
傻瓜，他没有爱上某人——相反，只需要提到斯蒂芬他就
开始咯咯笑，跟我说话时的情景也差不多，至少和现在我
对你们喋喋不休的场景差不多……）。

What does the enigma consist in? In an art which I would
term between­the-lines, to refer to the cord. It is not clear why
lines which are written should not be knotted by a second cord.

这个谜是怎么构成的？在我称之为线之间的技术中，
涉及到绳索。目前还不清楚为什么被写下的线不应该用第
二根绳索来打结。

Writing: it's an interest of mine. A certain Fevrier has done
a history of it, another, Guelb, has theorised It. For my part, I
only think historically - it through little bits of writing that we
have entered into the real - that is, ceased to imagine. The
writing of little letters, little mathematical letters, is what
supports the real.



- 162 -


写作：这是我自己的一个爱好。某个叫弗利埃（Fevrier）
的人研究过它的历史，另一位叫盖尔布（Guelb），则把它理
论化了。就我而言，我只历史性地思考——我们通过一点
点写作进入了实在（the real）——也就是说，停止了想象。
小字母，小数学字母（little mathematical letters）的书写，
支撑着实在。

But how on earth does it do this? , I wondered. Truly, I said
to myself, it must be that writing always has something to do
with the way we write the knot.

但它到底是怎么做到这些的呢？我想要知道。说真的，
我对自己说，书写一定总与我们写出结的方式有关。

A knot is commonly written like this. [reference to fig]

一个结通常是这样写的。[参照图]




- 163 -


Beauty according to Hogarth

霍加斯所说的美

This already gives us an S, in other words something which
has a considerable relation to the instance of the letter as I
maintain it. And this gives us a likely basis for beauty - if
Hogarth is to be believed, beauty was always a matter of this
double Inflection. Bullshit, of course, but in the end, at least this
would imply that beauty is connected to something other than
the obscene, in other words the real. There would be, in sum,
nothing but beautiful writing - why not?

这已经给了我们一个S，换句话说，它与我所坚称的字



- 164 -

母的例子有相当大的关系。这给了我们一个美的基础——
如果霍加斯（Hogarth）所说的话能相信的话，美总是一个
关于这种双重转变（this double Inflection）的问题。当然，
这是胡扯，但最后，这至少意味着美与某些并非淫秽
（obscene）的东西有关，换句话说，某些并非实在（the
real）的东西。总而言之，这样只会有美丽的书写——但为
什么不呢？

3

Let's get back to Stephen, who also starts with an S.
Stephen is Joyce as he solves his own riddle[il dechiffre sa
propre enigme]. He doesn’t get very far because he believes in
all his symptoms. This is very striking. He begins with -well, he
began long before that, he scribbled some little bits, even some
poems. His poems are not what he did best. He believes in the
uncreated conscience of my race - that is the ending of A Portrait
of the Artist as a Young Man, and it's clear that it doesn't go very
far. The end is better - Old father, old artificer, stand me now
and ever in good stead. It is to his father that he addresses this
prayer -his father whose precise characteristic is to be an
unworthy, lacking father, and who Stephen is to search for



- 165 -

throughout Ulysses, in settings where he has no chance of
finding him. There is clearly a father somewhere - Bloom, a
father in search of a son, but Stephen places one in opposition to
him - but not much of one, I would say, having had so much
father I was sick of him. It is peculiar that, throughout the novel,
there is a gravitational attraction between the thoughts of Bloom
and Stephen. Adams, whose name sounds more Jewish than
Bloom, is very struck by the way Joyce attributes to Bloom a
knowledge of Shakespeare which is manifestly unlikely, and
which, moreover, is not necessarily favourable, like claiming
that Shakespeare had relations with a certain herbalist who lived
near him in London. For such a thing to occur to Bloom goes
beyond what can rightly he imputed to him. This knowledge
belongs to Stephen. The entire Surface or Symbol chapter talks
of nothing but this. It culminates at the point in the text of
Ulysses where Blephen and Stoom meet, which shows that they
are not only made of the same signifier, but truly of the same
material.

让我们回到斯蒂芬（Stephen）身上，他也以一个S 开
头。斯蒂芬是乔伊斯，因为他解开了他自己的谜［il
dechiffre sa propre enigme 解开自己的谜团］。他没有走得太



- 166 -

远，因为他相信自己所有的症状。这是非常惊人的。他的
起点——嗯，他早在那之前就开始了，他草草写了一些小
事，甚至一些诗。他的诗并不是他最擅长的。他相信我的
种族的未被锻造的良心——这是《一位青年艺术家的肖像》
的结尾，显然，这并不遥远。这样的结尾很好——“老父亲，
老工匠，现在和以后，请助我一臂之力吧”（Old father, old
artificer, stand me now and ever in good stead）（注：这句祷告
是对希腊传说中的巧匠迪达勒斯所发，又会让人想起迪达
勒斯之子在起飞之前对他说的话）。他是对他的父亲祈祷的
——他父亲的确切特征是一个不配的、匮乏的父亲，斯蒂
芬要在整部《尤利西斯》中寻找他，在他没有机会找到他
的环境中寻找。很明显，在某个地方有一位父亲——布鲁
姆（Bloom），一个寻找儿子的父亲，但斯蒂芬把他与自己
对立了起来——我想说的是，那个已经有了那么多父亲的
人，我对他感到厌倦。奇怪的是，在整部小说中，布鲁姆
和斯蒂芬的思想之间存在着一种引力。亚当斯（Adams），
他的名字听起来比布鲁姆更犹太，亚当斯惊讶于乔伊斯认
为布鲁姆了解关于莎士比亚的知识，但这显然是不可能的，
而且也不一定是有什么用处的，这就好像声称莎士比亚和
伦敦的某位住在他附近的草药医生有关系一样。对于在布
鲁姆身上的发生的这样的事，已超出了他可以恰当地归咎
于他自己的范围。这个知识属于斯蒂芬。在《表象和象征》



- 167 -

的所有章节中，只提到了这一点。它在《尤利西斯》的文
本中达到高潮，那里布莱芬（Blephen）和斯托姆（Stoom）
相遇，这表明他们不是仅由相同的能指组成的，而是确实
由相同的材料构成的。

Ulysses bears Witness to the way Joyce remains rooted in
his father, even as he denies him - and this is exactly his
symptom. I have said that Joyce was himself the symptom. His
whole oeuvre is one long testimony to this. Exiles is an
approach to what is for him the central symptom, that of the lack
of the sexual relation. The form taken by this lack is not
arbitrary, but it must take one - for Joyce, this is what binds him
to his wife, the so-called Nora, during whose reign he dreams up
Exiles. There could be no better term to express non­relation
than exile. Non-relation means that there is no reason why he
should take a woman, among others, to be his. A woman among
others is also one who has a relation to some other man, and this
'some other man' is the character he imagines, and for whom he
opens the choice of 'a woman' in question, Who as it happens is
none other than Nora.

《尤利西斯》见证了乔伊斯仍然植根于他的父亲的方



- 168 -

式，即使他否认他——而这正是他的症状（symptom）。我
曾说过，乔伊斯的症状就是他自己。他的全部作品都是对
此的冗长的证词。流亡是为了解决他的核心症状，即性关
系的缺失。这种缺失所采取的形式不是任意的，但必须采
取一种——对乔伊斯来说，这就是把他与他的妻子，即所
谓的诺拉，系在一起的东西。在他在世的时候，他梦想着
流亡（逃离）（Exiles）。没有比流亡更好的术语来表达非—
关系(non­relation)了。非—关系意味着他没有理由让“在他
者之间的一个女人”（« Une femme entre autres »）变成某个
人的女人。“在他者之间的一个女人”也就是和任何其他男人
有关系的人，这个“任何其他男人”是他想象的角色，他为其
打开了“一个女人”（Une femme）的选择问题，她不是别人，
恰恰正是诺拉。

Concerning the uncreated conscience of my race he
invokes the supreme artificer who would be his father. But it is
he who is the artificer, he's the one who knows what he has to do.
However, he believes that there is a race which has an uncreated
conscience, which is a great illusion. He also believes that there
is a book of himself - the idea of making oneself into a book!
The idea could only occur to a damn stunted poet. Why doesn't
he say, instead, that he's a knot?



- 169 -


关于“ 我的种族未被创造的意识” （the uncreated
conscience of my race），他援引了最杰出的工匠，也就是他
的父亲。但他才是工匠，他才是知道自己该做什么的人。
然而，他相信，有一个“未被创造的种族的意识”，这是一个
巨大的幻觉。他还相信有一本关于他自己的书——让自己
成为一本书的想法！这个想法只能出现在一个蛇皮的发育
迟缓的诗人身上。他怎么不说他是个结？

Let's get to Ulysses. A certain Chechner, who because he
has read lots of books about analysis thinks that he's an analyst -
it's an illusion that is fairly widespread, especially amongst
analysts - has wished to analyse Ulysses. The effect this creates
is absolutely terrifying, it truly gives the idea that the novelist's
imagination, I mean the imagination which reigns over Ulysses,
is to be thrown in the dustbin. Unlike Surface and Symbol, this
analysis of Ulysses Wishes to be exhaustive - naturally, because
one can't stop when one analyses a book. Freud, all the same,
only wrote some articles of this type, and moreover, he didn't,
properly speaking, analyse the novel, with the exception of
Dostoyevsky. He made nothing but a little allusion concerning
Ibsen. He restrained himself!



- 170 -


让我们来看看《尤利西斯》。某位叫切赫的人
（Checher），因为他读过很多关于分析的书，于是认为他是
一名分析师——这是一种相当普遍的错觉，尤其是在分析
师中——他们希望分析《尤利西斯》。这所产生的效果绝对
是可怕的，它确实产生这样一种想法：即小说家的想象
（imagination），我的意思是，支配着《尤利西斯》的想象，
将被扔进垃圾箱。与《表象与象征》不同的是，这种对尤
利西斯的分析希望是详尽的——很自然，因为当一个人分
析一本书时，根本停不下来。尽管如此，弗洛伊德只是写
了一些这类的文章，而他并不，准确的说，并不分析长篇
小说（the novel），陀思妥耶夫斯基除外。关于易卜生，他
也只是略作影射。他克制住了自己！

Here is what our beloved Joyce, In the guise of Stephen,
proposes to his pupils as a riddle [enigme]. It's an enonciation:

这是我们心爱的乔伊斯，以斯蒂芬的伪装，向他的学
生们提出的一个谜题。他的话（énonciation）是这样说的：

The cock crew
The sky was blue



- 171 -

The bells in heaven Were striking eleven
'Tis time for this poor soul
To go to heaven. (U, 22)

公鸡打了鸣
天色一片蓝
天堂那些钟
敲了十一点
可怜的灵魂
该升天堂啦（U,22）

I'll tell you what the answer was. After the whole class
gives up, Joyce provides it - The fox burying his grandmother
under the bush. This seems to amount to nothing, but beside the
coherence of the énonciation - note that it's in verse, it's a poem,
a consistent artefact - next to this, this little fox burying his
grandmother under a bush is truly a wretched thing. What echo
can that have for - I won't say for everyone in this room, but for
those who are analysts? Analysis is that - the answer to a riddle;
and an answer, it must be said, which is quite exceptionally
stupid .




- 172 -

我来告诉你谜底是什么。在全班都放弃了思考后，乔
伊斯给出了答案——狐狸在冬青树下埋葬它的奶奶。这似
乎毫无意义，除了陈诉的连贯性——注意它是一首诗，是
一首诗，一件始终如一的艺术品——此外，这只小狐狸把
它的祖母葬在冬青树下真的是一件不幸的事情。这会产生
什么回响——我不是说房间里的每个人，但对那些分析师
呢？分析是一个谜语的答案，而一个答案，它必须被道出，
这实在是太愚蠢了。

This is why one must hold on to the cord. If one has no
notion of where the cord ends up - that is, in the knot of the
sexual non-relation - one runs the risk of talking rubbish.
Meaning is the result of an intersection between the imaginary
and the symbolic. And if we hold that there is no Other of the
Other, at least no jouissance of the Other of the Other, we must
make a stitch [suture] or splice [epissure] between the imaginary
and the symbolic: unconscious knowledge. All this to obtain a
meaning - which is the object of the analyst's response to what
the analysand reveals over time about his symptom.

这就是为什么一个人必须紧紧抓住绳子。如果一个人
不知道绳子的终点在哪里——也就是说，在性的非—关系



- 173 -

（the sexual non-relation）的结中——他就会有说废话的风
险。意义是由想象和象征之间的交集的结果。如果我们认
为没有大他者的大他者，或者至少没有大他者的大他者的
原乐（jouissance of the Other of the Other），我们必须在想象
和象征之间进行缝合[suture]或拼接[epissure]：无意识的知
识。所有这些都是为了获得一个意义——这就是分析师对
于被分析者随着时间推移而被揭示出的症状作出反应的目
的。

In making this splice, at the same time we make another
between what constitutes the symptom and the real. In same
manner, we teach the analysand to splice together his symptom
and the parasitic real of jouissance. This is what characterises
our procedure. To render this jouissance possible is the same
thing as what I would write as j’ouïs-sens ['I hear sense'], to hear
a meaning. Analysis is a matter of suture and splicing.




- 174 -

在制造这个拼接的同时，我们在那构成症状和实在的
东西之间做了另一个拼接。以同样的方式，我们教被分析
者缝合他的症状和原乐的寄生性实在（the parasitic real of
jouissance）。这是我们精神分析过程的特点。为了使这种原
乐(jouissance)成为可能，正如我写作“我听到了意义“ (J'ouis
sens) ['I hear sense']（注：在法文中，我听到j’ouïs-sens 与原
乐jouissance 同音异义。而sens 有方向/意义两种意思），听
到一种意义。分析是一个缝合和拼接（épissure）的事情。


Splicing the link into a knot (open form of the trefoil knot)

将链环捻接成一个结（三叶结的开放形式）




- 175 -

But it must be said that we ought to consider the orders
[instances] as in reality separate. Imaginary, symbolic and real
do not intermingle. Finding a meaning entails a knowledge of
the knot, and sewing it up with artifice. Tying a knot, with what
I would term a borromean chain-knot. is this not an abuse?
Leaving that question hanging, I must leave you.

但必须要说的是，我们应该把这些秩序性的东西
[instances]实际上分开考虑。想象、象征和实在并不混杂在
一起。找到一个意义需要了解结，然后用技巧将它缝合起
来。打一个结，打一个波罗米链结（borromean chain-knot）。
这不是一种虐待吗？让这个问题悬而未决，我就必须得离
开你们了。

I have not left any time for dear Jacques Aubert, who I had
planned to give the floor for the rest of the session, to speak to
you now; but next time he will talk to you about the Bloom in
question - that is, about someone who is not badly qualified to
get the hang of analysis, since he's a Jew - about Bloom and how
he feels suspended between the sexes, which makes him wonder
if he's a father or a mother. Regarding his wife, he feels maternal,
he thinks he carries her in his belly. This really is the worst



- 176 -

aberration one can experience vis-...-vis somebody one loves.
But why not? Love must be explained, and explaining it as a
kind of madness is the first thing which comes within reach.

我现在没有时间留给亲爱的雅克·奥伯特了，我本来打
算在本次会议剩下的时间里请他发言，让他来与你们交流；
但下次他会跟你们谈论布鲁姆的问题——就是，关于一些
人，他们不是没有资格得到分析的诀窍，因为他是一个犹
太人——关于布鲁姆和他是怎样感到悬挂于两性之间的，
这让他怀疑自己是一个父亲还是一个母亲。对于他的妻子，
他感到了母性，他总想把她抱在肚子上。这真的是一个人
能经历到的最怪的事了…——但是对你所爱的人。为什么
不试着这样做呢？爱必须被解释，而把它解释为一种疯狂
则是最简单的事情。

1971 年1 月13 日





- 177 -

PRESENTATION AT LACAN'S SEMINAR
在拉康研讨班上的讲话

You must be thinking that, when it comes to Joyce, I'm a
fish out of water [lit 'like a fish with an apple']. This is obviously
linked to my lack of practice, to my inexperience of the
language he writes in. It is not that I'm totally ignorant of
English, but rather that he writes with such peculiar subtlety in
English, that he disarticulates it. You would be wrong to think
this only begins with Finnegans Wake. Long before Finnegans
Wake, he has a peculiar way of breaking up phrases, notably in
Ulysses; indeed, it’s a process aiming to give language another
use - and one which is far from being ordinary. This is part of
his savoir-faire. And on this question, I have already quoted
Sollers' article, whose pertinence you would do well to sample.

拉康：你们一定在想，当谈到乔伊斯时，我就如同一
条失去水的鱼（陷入窘境）[原话：像一条遇到苹果的鱼]。
这显然与我疏于实践有关，与我对他所写的语言缺乏经验
有关。这并非是说我完全不懂英语，而是他用如此独特精
妙的方式来用英语写作，他使得英语脱节了。如果你们认



- 178 -

为这是从《芬尼根的守灵夜》才开始的，那就错了。早在
《芬尼根的守灵夜》之前，他就有一种分解词组的独特方
式，特别是在《尤利西斯》中；事实上，这是一个旨在赋
予语言以另一种使用的过程——而且是一种远非普通的使
用。这是他“做的知识”（savoir-faire）的一部分。关于这个
问题，我已经引用了索莱尔（Philippe Sollers）的文章，你
们最好对他的相关内容有所了解。

So this morning I'm going to allow someone to speak
whose practical experience, not only of the English language,
but of Joyce in particular, is far superior to mine. This is Jacques
Aubert, and I'm going to let him speak straight away, since he
has kindly offered to take over from me. I will listen to him with
the full knowledge I have gathered of his experience of Joyce; I
will listen to him, and hope that my comments - brief as they
will be, I don't ask him to abridge things, far from it - the brief
comments I will add will be made with all the respect I owe him
for having introduced me to what I have called Joyce-the-
symptom.

因此，今天上午我将把发言权交给一位实践经验远超
于我的人，他不仅对英语语言，尤其对乔伊斯也有所研究。



- 179 -

这就是雅克·奥伯特，我打算让他直接发言，因为他已经同
意接替我的工作。我将带着我从他对乔伊斯的经验中得到
的全部知识来听他的讲话；我将倾听他讲话，并希望我的
评论——当然它们将是简短的，我绝非是要求他删减任何
东西——我将以我对他的尊敬来补充我的简评，因为他向
我介绍了我称之为乔伊斯–症状（Joyce-the-symptom）的东
西。

Jacques Aubert’s intervention
雅克·奥伯特的发言

Jacques Aubert - Last June, Dr Lacan announced that Joyce
was to feature on the path of his work. My presence here today
in no way indicates that I feature on that royal road. Let me say
immediately that I am, rather, on its verges; and you know why
these are generally sign-posted: so what you are going to hear
will be a roadmender's words! (la cantinnier) [punning
untranslatably on Lacan's name]

雅克·奥伯特：去年六月，拉康博士宣布乔伊斯将在他
的研究中发挥作用。我今天在这里，绝非表明我在那条康
庄大道上能发挥什么作用。让我直说的话，我倒是在这条



- 180 -

路的边缘；在通常情况下你们知道为什么这些边缘处设有
路标：而你们听到的将是一个修路者的话！(la cantinnier)
［lacannier（拉康派）的双关］

I must thank Jacques Lacan for inviting me to produce
some work; it is shoddy, not at all well tied-up, and not very
articulate concerning knots. From another perspective, I would
like to indicate that what I am going to say starts out from a
sense I had of something that was threading through the text,
certain of Joyce's texts at certain points - something woven in by
Joyce. And this awareness of the thread leads me, precisely, to
insist that this is not, as it might otherwise have been, a
definitive statement.

我必须感谢雅克·拉康邀请我一起进行一些工作；这些
工作做得很粗糙，并不扎实，而且关于扭结的阐述也不太
清楚。从另一个角度来看，我想表明，我要说的东西始于
我对某种——由乔伊斯编织入——并贯穿于他的特定的位
于某些点上的文本。准确地说，对这种脉络的认识使我坚
持认为，这这些文本并非是如与其相反的那般——并非是
一种明确的陈述。




- 181 -

To situate what happened to be my point of departure. I
should specify - putting it in a very didactic way - that it was a
little bit of 'Circe', part of an exchange from the episode in
Ulysses which was given the name 'Circe' a posteriori, and
described as the hallucinatory episode, whose art would be
magic and whose category hallucination (according to a schema
drawn up by Joyce for some friends).

为了确定我的出发点是什么。我应该以一种非常说教
的方式来明确说明——它是“喀耳刻”（Circe）章节的一小
部分，是《尤利西斯》中的一段对话的一部分，它在之后
被命名为“Circe”，并被描写为幻觉的章节，其艺术是魔法
般的，（根据乔伊斯为一些朋友草拟的概要）其类别是幻觉。

Elements from preceding chapters, whose status cannot yet
be determined, re-appear – real or fictional characters, objects,
or signifiers. But what is Interesting too, is the manner of this
return, the way it is manifestly connected with speech [la parole],
with a speaking. From the beginning one is made aware of this –
since the first two characters, one might say, are the Call and the
Answer, clearly Indicating a dimension developed In the form of
the chapter, in the ostensibly dramatic style – the dimension of



- 182 -

speech [la parole], and of the kind of Institutions/locations
which generate it [instaurations de lieux d’ou ça parle].

那些无法确定其地位的来自于前几章的要素再次出现
了——即真实或虚构的人物、对象或符号。但有趣的是，
这种回归的方式，它明显地与言语[la parole]，与一种言说
有关。从一开始，便可以意识到这一点——因为我们可以
说，前两个人物，是呼唤与回应，明显标志着一个由表面
上的戏剧性写作风格在章节形式上所发展起来的维度——
言语[la parole] 的维度，以及产生它的某种制度/ 场所
[instaurations de lieux d’ou ça parle]。

What is important is that it speaks [ça parle], in all possible
senses [sens], and that everything can be impersonated there (to
take up a term that we will encounter shortly); everything can
personate in this text, give rise to voice-effects across a mask.

重要的是它会在任何可能的意义[sens]上言说[ça parle]，
而且一切都可以是（用一个我们很快会遇到的术语来说）
“无人称的（impersonné）”；在这个文本中，一切都可以进
行人格化，产生跨越面具的语音效果。




- 183 -

I think I’ve isolated one of these functions – a detail of one
of these functions or its mode of functioning – at the beginning
of the chapter, in an exchange between Bloom and Rudolph
(who is supposed to be his father and has been dead for eighteen
years). I will read you the brief exchange in question.

我想我已经在这一章的开头，在布鲁姆和鲁道尔夫
（被布鲁姆误认为是鲁道尔夫‘即他的父亲’，事实上鲁道尔
夫已经过世18 年了）的交流中分离出这些功能中的——一
个细节或其运作模式。让我来给你们读一段文中进行的交
流。

Rudolph first appears as an elder of Zion. He has the
appearance, according to the stage-directions, of an elder of
Zion. And after various reproaches to his son, he says this:
‘What you making down this place? Have you no soul? (a native of
Hungary, he is supposed not to be able to manage English) (with feeble
vulture talons he feels the silent face of Bloom) Are you not my son
Leopold, the grandson of Leopold? Are you not my dear son Leopold who
left the house of his father and left the god of his fathers Abraham and
Jacob?’ (U, 357-8).




- 184 -

鲁道夫如同锡安长老般首次出现。根据舞台指示，他
有着锡安长老的样貌。在对他的儿子进行了一番责备之后，
他说道：
‘你在这儿干些什么名堂啊？你没有灵魂吗？（他是
匈牙利人，应该不会说英语）（他伸出虚弱的秃鹫爪子，
抚摩着布鲁姆那沉默的脸。）你不是我的儿子利奥波德
吗？不是利奥波德的孙子吗？你不是我那亲爱的儿子
利奥波德吗？就是那个离开父亲的家，也离开祖先亚
伯拉罕和雅各的上帝的利奥波德吗？’ (U, 357-8)

At first glance, what Is happening here, the reader of
Ulysses thinks, is something described several times by Bloom
himself with the expression retrospective arrangement; the term
recurs often enough in Bloom’s thoughts throughout the text.
The reader cannot fail to be aware of this retrospective
arrangement, nor that it is arranged in relation to a favourite
quotation of the father, from a literacy text which it would seem
had a certain effect on him. This text is on page 62 in the Gabler
edition:

对《尤利西斯》的读者来说，这里一目了然的是布鲁
姆本人多次以“回顾性安排”（retrospective arrangement）的



- 185 -

说法描述的一种现象；这种说法在布鲁姆的想法中经常出
现，贯穿全文。读者不可能不知道这种回顾性安排，也不
可能不知道这种安排与父亲最喜欢的一段话有关，这段话
来自一篇识字课文，显然对他产生了一些影响。这段文字
位于Gabler 版的第62 页：

‘Nathan's voice! His son's voice! I hear the voice of Nathan who left his
father to die of grief and misery in my arms, who left the house of his
father and left the God of his father.’

‘拿单的声音！他儿子的声音！我听到了拿单的声音，
他离开了自己的父亲，任他悲惨忧伤地死在我的怀抱
里。他就这样离开了父亲的家，并且离开了父亲的上
帝。’

One sees here that what returns does so with a subtle
difference. But before outlining that difference, I would like to
point to the effects this return-with-difference has on Bloom.
What is his reply, In the Circe episode? This:
‘Bloom (with precaution): I suppose so, father. Mosenthal. All that’s left
of him.’




- 186 -

可以看到，回归之物是以一种微妙的差异回归。但在
论述这种差异之前，我想指出这种有差异的回归对布鲁姆
的影响。在“喀耳刻”这一章节中，他的回答是什么？如下：
‘布鲁姆（惶恐地）：大概是的，父亲。莫森索尔。但
所剩无几了。’

Precaution - this is certainly a quality of Bloom, who is
described as prudent throughout Ulysses. The prudent one is an
aspect of Ulysses (but Ulysses is not merely that). He is
described several times, In slightly Masonic terms, as the
prudent member. The prudent member says I suppose so, I
suppose so, I suppose something to reply to the question 'Are
you not my son?'; 'I sub-pose something of the kind', which in
principle connects back to what the father said, but which
suddenly, as soon as one is following the text, appears as ,
another figure, for we immediately have this moment of arrest
marked by what the Anglo-Saxons call a period, something
which marks finality, a point not of suspension but of closure,
and beyond which Mosenthal appears, a new sentence, once
again marked by a period.

小心谨慎（Precaution）——这当然是布鲁姆的一个品



- 187 -

质，在整个《尤利西斯》中，他被描写为一个谨慎的人。
为人谨慎是尤利西斯的一个方面（但尤利西斯不仅如此）。
他被多次用略带共济会意味的字眼描述为“谨慎的成员”。谨
慎的成员说，我想是的，我想是的，我想是一些什么东西
以回答‘你不是我的儿子吗’这一问题；‘我置于之下（sub-
pose）是这样的’，这在原则上指向了父亲所说的话，但当
我们跟随文本时，突然间，出现了另一个人物，因为我们
立即有了这个以盎格鲁—撒克逊人称之为句号的这一标志
作为最终时刻的标点，一个并非（示意）暂停而是结束的
点，在这之后，莫森索尔出现了，一个新的句子，然后再
一次被标上句号。

Around this proper name something is articulated and
disarticulated at the same time based on the announced sub-
position. What, then, is this suppôt, this 'henchman', this sous-
pot function, a 'pot saucer' function (or a sous-peau function, an
'under the skin' function) that Mosenthal holds?

恰恰是围绕着这个专有名词，一些东西在宣称的次要
位置（sous-position）的基础上同时被衔接和拆分。那么，
莫森索尔所含的这个帮凶（suppôt ），这个“ 追随者”
（henchman），这个潜于器皿之下（sous-pot）的作用，一



- 188 -

个“壶–托”（pot saucer）的作用（或是一个潜于表皮之下
（sous-peau）的作用，一个“皮之下”（under the skin）的作
用）24是什么？

Here, in this context, the function of this signifier is to refer
the father's words back to the author of a text, the text which has
just been evoked by the father. But in its abruptness the signifier
obscures more than it illuminates, and the reader is led to isolate
it, search for the thoughts it connects back to, the displacements
it is implicated in.

在这里，在这一语境中，该能指的功能是把父亲的话
引回到一个文本的作者那里，这个文本刚刚被父亲唤起。
但是，这个能指的突显（abruptness）更多的是遮掩、晦涩
（obscures），而非启发（illuminates），读者被引导去区分它，
寻找它所连接的思想，寻找它所牵涉到的移置
（displacement）。

One of these displacements is obvious: in the first text,
from the 'Lotus Eaters' (U, 62) the name in question, that of the

24 ‘我放了这么多的谜团和谜语，这够让这些教授们忙活几个世纪了，争论我
在这里写的究竟是什么意思，而这也是保证一个人不朽的唯一方法。’；Ellman,
James Joyce, op.cit., p.521。



- 189 -

author, features before the quotation; here, it is in the position of
signature, and also in the position of a response. It's very
charming, particularly so because it is about Moses. But if one
calls to mind - as one always does, because one spends one's
time re-reading - the place Mosenthal held in the first text, one
realizes that there it was a displaced response to a question
about the existence of the true name; a question which itself
could only be formulated with eloquent vacillation. I must note
here another phrase which is precisely the question to which
Mosenthal is supposed to be the answer:
What is this the right name is? By Mosenthal it is, Rachel
is it? No.

其中的一个移置是显而易见的：第一个如此的文本，
来自于章节“吃忘忧果的种族”（Lotus Eaters）(U, 62)中的相
关名字，即这个作者的名字，出现在引文之前；在这里，
它处于签名的位置，同时也处于回应的位置。这是非常有
趣的，尤其是因为它是关于摩西的。但是，如果人们想起
——就像人们总是这样，因为人们会花时间重读——莫森
索尔在第一个文本中所处的位置，就会意识到，在那里它
是对一个关于真名的存在问题的移置性回应；这个问题本
身只能用有说服力的摇摆不定来表述。我必须在此写上另



- 190 -

一句话，这正是‘莫森索尔’应该作为其答案的问题：
这个剧目的名字是什么来着？作者是莫森索尔，还是
瑞切尔（Rachel）？我不确定。

For good measure, I’ve included the next part, which might
hold some interest.

为了慎重起见，我把下一部分包括在内，这可能会引
起某些人的兴趣。

Even though a German speaker who knows the slang might
hear something else, especially if it carried a diaeresis,
Mosenthal is the name of the author of a theatre play whose
original German title Bloom is trying to retrieve and retranslate.
In fact the title is a woman’s name, a female Jewish name,
which wasn’t kept in English. It’s a curious idea. It’s a
melodrama that had the title Deborah in German, which was
translated into English under the name Leah, and this is what
Bloom is trying to find. So, he’s trying to translate the original
title (which is a woman’s name) and this takes the form of this
search. Clearly, we can see the game of hide-and-seek between
the author’s name and the creature’s name at the level of art,



- 191 -

which brings into play both Being, with insistence – the ‘is’
insists – and the sexual problematic of a patronymic that comes
in the stead of a daughter’s name.

尽管了解俚语的德语使用者可能会听成别的什么东西，
特别是如果它带有分音符的话，但莫森索尔（Mosenthal）
是一部戏剧作者的名字，布鲁姆正试图回忆并重译这个戏
剧的德语原标题。事实上，这个标题是一个女人的名字，
一个女性犹太人的名字，而这在英文翻译中未被保留。这
是很古怪的。这是一部情节剧，德文标原题为底波拉
（Deborah），被翻译成的英文名为利亚（Leah），而这正是
布鲁姆试图寻找的。因此，他试图重新翻译回原标题（一
个女人的名字），并采取了这种搜寻的形式。很明显，我们
可以看到作者的名字与其作品的名字之间在艺术层面上的
捉迷藏游戏，这使得同时游弋于执着的“存在者”（Being）
——对于“存在”（is）的坚持，和对取代女儿的名字的父性
之名（patronymic）的与性相关的问题意识的坚持。

At this point the reader, whose notice nothing in Ulysses
escapes, of course, says that this rings a bell, which bears some
relation with Bloom himself.




- 192 -

在这里，读者（当然，读者不会错过《尤利西斯》中
的任何内容）说这看起来有些眼熟，与布鲁姆本人有关。

I'll give you the first passage again (and I apologize for
doing this in bits and pieces but I'm simply following the path I
took), along with its context:
Mr Bloom stood at the corner, his eyes wandering over the multicoloured
hoardings. Cantrell and Cochrane's Ginger Ale (Aromatic). Clery's
summer sale. No, he's going on straight [This refers to someone he's just
been speaking to; Bloom wonders whether he might be watching him].
Hello. Leah tonight [The play in question]: Mrs Bandman Palmer. Like to
see her in that again. Hamlet she played last night. Male impersonator.

我再给大家讲一下第一段话（很抱歉讲的这么零碎，
我只是要按照我的节奏来讲），以及它的上下文：

布鲁姆站在街角，眼光掠过五颜六色的广告牌。坎特
雷尔与科克伦公司的姜汁牌酒（芳香型）。克列利公司
夏季大甩卖。不，他一直走了［这是指他刚刚与其说
话的一个人；布鲁姆想知道那个人是否在监视他］。唷，
今晚是《利亚》（Leah）［相关的剧目］。班德曼·帕尔默
夫人。我愿意再看她演这一出。昨晚她演哈姆雷特。



- 193 -

女扮男装（Male impersonator）。

Here begins a short passage on the problematic of the sexes.
The English expression 'male impersonator' refers to an actress
who has taken on the male persona, his mask, but on the other
hand this can apply as much to the one play, Hamlet, as to the
other, Leah. Everything will revolve around this.
Male impersonator. Perhaps he was a woman. Why Ophelia
committed suicide?

从这里便开始了关于性别问题的一小段文字。英语中
的"女扮男装"指的是扮演男性角色的女演员，男性的面具，
但另一方面，这也同样适用于《哈姆雷特》这部剧，以及
《利亚》这部剧。一切都将围绕这个问题展开。
女扮男装。说不定他本来就是个女的。而这就是奥菲
利娅自杀的原因吗？（Why Ophelia committed suicide?）

So, at a certain level there is the fact that the role of Hamlet
was very often played by women. It turns out that one
Anglophone critic had the fanciful idea of analysing Hamlet
precisely in terms of transvestism, taking the impersonator
seriously in some sense and saying: Ophelia committed suicide



- 194 -

because she realized that Hamlet was in fact a woman. I’m not
mentioning this critic for the sake of it, in the name of my
Shakespearian and Joycean knowledge, but simply because this
implication appears elsewhere in Ulysses.

因此，在某种程度上，存在着这样一个事实：哈姆雷
特的角色经常由女性扮演。结果，一位盎格鲁—撒克逊评
论家有了一个遐想，即，从异装癖的角度来分析哈姆雷特，
在某种意义上认真看待女扮男装这一形式，并说：奥菲利
娅自杀是因为她意识到哈姆雷特实际上是一个女人。我不
是为了展现我对莎士比亚和乔伊斯的了解而提到这位批评
家，而是因为这种暗指（implication）出现在《尤利西斯》
的其他地方。

The statement 'Why Ophelia committed suicide?' is
equivocal. It's both Why did Ophelia commit suicide? and Was
this the reason she committed suicide? Clearly this doesn't make
it through in the French translation, and it's worthwhile pointing
that out.

“Why Ophelia committed suicide?”这一表述是模棱两可
的。这既可以被理解为“奥菲利娅为什么自杀？”也可以是



- 195 -

“这是她自杀的原因吗？”需要指出的是，显然，这一点在法
译本中没有体现出来。

What do we read next?
Poor papa! How he used to talk of Kate Bateman in that! Outside the
Adelphi in London waited all the afternoon to get in. Year before I was
born that was: sixtyfive. And Ristori in Vienna. [And this is where the
question about the title comes in.] What is this the right name is? By
Mosenthal it is. Rachel, is it? No. The scene he was always talking about
where the old blind Abraham recognizes the voice and puts his fingers on
his face.
- Nathan’s voice! His son’s voice! I hear the voice of Nathan who left his
father to die of grief and misery in my arms, who left the house of his
father and left the God of his father.
Every word is so deep, Leopold.
Poor papa! Poor man! I’m glad I didn’t go into the room to look at his
face. That day! O, dear! O, dear! Ffoo! Well, perhaps it was best for him.

我们读到哪了？
可怜的爸爸！他常提起凯特·贝特曼（Kate Bateman）扮演
的这个角色。他在伦敦的阿德尔菲剧场外面足足等了
一个下午才进去的。那是一八六五年——我出生前一年



- 196 -

的事。还有里斯托里在维也纳的演出。剧目的名字叫
什么来着？作者是莫森索尔。是《拉结》（Rachel）吧？
不是的。他经常谈到的场景是：又老又瞎的亚伯拉罕
听出了那声音，就把手指放在他的脸上。
拿单的声音！他儿子的声音！我听到了拿单的声音，
他离开了自己的父亲，任他悲惨忧伤地死在我的怀抱
里。他就这样离开了父亲的家，并且离开了父亲的上
帝。
每句话都讲得那么深沉，利奥波德。
可怜的爸爸！可怜的人！幸而我不曾进屋去瞻仰他的
遗容。那是怎样的一天啊！哎呀，天哪！哎呀，天哪！
嗬！喏，也许这样对他最好不过。

So, in this passage a whole series of questions are at stake:
questions about existence; about Being and the name; about
existence and suicide; the question of the name - and I shall be
coming back to this point - that is in fact both the name of the
father, his father, and the name of the main character in the play;
and lastly the question about the sex that personates, which is
that which per-sonation.




- 197 -

在这段话中，涉及了一系列的问题：关于存在的问题；
关于存在和名字的问题；关于存在和自杀的问题；名字的
问题——我将回到这一点上来——实际上既是父亲（他的
父亲）的名字，也是剧中主角的名字；最后是关于角色化
了的（personates ）性的问题，即关于角色之音（per-
sonation）的问题。

Behind the question of the name stands the suicide of the
father, who possesses this further characteristic of having
changed name: this is what is indicated in another passage that
is likewise presented in a curious way.

在名字问题的背后是父亲的自杀，他还具有改变名字
这一特征：这就是另一段话中所表明的，这段话同样呈现
出一种奇怪的方式。

In a pub, a few of the barflies are talking about Bloom.
'He's a perverted jew', says one of them. (Here, 'perverted'
means 'renegade', in the same way that Joyce uses it towards the
end of A Portrait: '[. . .] are you trying to make a convert of me
or a pervert of yourself?')
He's a perverted jew [. . .] from a place in Hungary and it was he drew up



- 198 -

all the plans according to the Hungarian system [the political plans of
Sinn Fein]. [. . .] He changed it [his name] by deed poll, the father did. (p.
323)

在一家酒馆里，几个酒吧常客正在谈论布鲁姆。“他是
一个反常的（perverted）犹太人”，其中一人说。(在这里，
“反常”（perverted）的意思是“变节”（renegade），与乔伊斯
在《一位青年艺术家的肖像》结尾处的使用方式相同：
“[. . .]你现在的目的是要让我相信上帝呢，还是要让你自己
也不再相信（pervert）上帝了？”）
他是一个反常的犹太人［. . .］，从匈牙利某地来的，
所有依照匈牙利方法的计划就是由他所起草的［新芬
党的政治计划］。［. . .］他父亲凭着一纸平边契据就把
他的姓改了。(p. 323)

So, it seems that the father changed his name. Moreover, he
changed it in a way that is rather interesting, using a legal form
called a deed poll. A ‘deed’ is an act (in every sense of the term),
but ‘poll’ evokes or describes in some way the act from the point
of view of the document: it’s a document that has been
‘cropped’. This ‘poll’, which describes the cropping, actually
refers to what has been lopped off or affected by pollarding (a



- 199 -

tree that has had its upper part removed is referred to as a
‘pollard’). In fact, ‘poll’ refers to the top of the head. The deed
poll has the characteristic of only comprising one part, the lower
part. This is why in the French translation it says par décret.
This is distinct from indenture where the act is torn in two,
precisely by indentation, in order to be entrusted to the two
parties. Therefore, so Joyce tells us, the father changed name by
deed poll, by a procedure that is different from a symbolic one.
But what name did he change?

所以，看来是父亲改了他的名字。而且，他改名的方
式相当有趣，他使用了一种叫做“平边契据”（deed poll）的
法律形式。“契约”（deed）是一种行为（在任何意义上），
而“修剪”（poll）以文档的角度唤起或描述该行为：这是一
份被“裁剪”的文件。这个描述裁剪的‘poll’实际上指的是被
截砍的东西，或是受截砍所影响的东西（一棵树的树梢被
砍掉，关联到的是“被截断”（pollard））。事实上，‘poll’指的
是顶端。平边契据的特点是只包括一方，即较低的一方。
这就是为什么在法语中将其翻译为“依法令办事”（par
décret）。这与合同（indenture）不同，在合同中，行为被一
分为二，正是通过将其裁开，被交付给双方当事人。因此，



- 200 -

乔伊斯告诉我们，父亲是通过平边契据改名的，通过不同
于象征秩序的方式。但他改的是什么名字？

— Isn’t he a cousin of Bloom the dentist? says Jack Power.
— Not at all, says Martin. Only namesakes. His name was Virag. The
father’s name that poisoned himself.

他不是牙医布鲁姆的堂兄弟吗？杰克·鲍尔说。
根本不是，马丁说。不过是同姓而已。他原来姓维拉
格，是他那个服毒自杀的父亲的姓。

In this we can hear a play on the genitive and on the
position of the name of the father, which allows one to
understand that it was the name that poisoned itself.

在此，我们可以看到对所有格和父之名位置的玩弄，
这让人理解为是这个名字毒害了自己。

'Virag' appears again. It is mentioned in several places in
Ulysses. It reappears in 'Circe', where first it is a virago,
designated as such: VIRAGO. Here we may remind ourselves
just what 'virago' is, namely the name that in the Vulgate, in



- 201 -

Saint Jerome's translation of the Bible, serves to designate
woman from Adam's point of view. In Genesis, man is led to
name woman: thou shalt be called woman [Virago]. She is a
little bit male (vir), while still being a woman.

“维拉格”（Virag）再次出现。它在《尤利西斯》中被
多次提及。它在章节“喀耳刻”（Circe）中再次出现，首先
以有着阳刚美德的女人（virago）在文本中出现，被称作：
VIRAGO。这里，我们要了解一下什么是“virago”，即在由
圣杰罗姆所译的《圣经武加大译本》中，由亚当起的女人
的名字（译者注：即夏娃）。在《创世纪》中，男人受引领
为女人命名：汝将被称为女人[Virago]。她有点像男性
（vir），同时仍是一个女人。

Having come to this point in my lucubration and my
groping between the lines of Ulysses, I should like to single out
what gives the impression of being a hole in this interlacing.
Indeed, it’s tempting to use, with a view to interpretation, a
schema that brings into play the suicide, the change of name,
and Bloom’s refusal to see the face of his dead father. It would
be very apt if all that were to reappear in ‘Circe’, in what is
deemed to be a hallucination. But this is nowhere near enough,



- 202 -

even if there is some truth to it, to make the text function, to
account, for example, for the passage, ‘Poor Papa! Poor man!’ in
the first extract (after ‘Every word is so deep Leopold’). These
words report the father’s commentary on the play, and then:
‘Poor Papa! Poor man!’, which perhaps was not so kind about
the father’s comments. ‘I’m glad. I didn’t go into the room to
look at his face. That day! O dear! [. . .] Well, perhaps it was the
best for him.’ In short, there is a whole set of items that need to
be accounted for, and above all the effects produced in the
dramatic redistribution that ‘Circe’ comprises. For this holds
together, it functions, and things happen precisely alongside
something that gives the impression of being a hole. Precisely,
Joyce has a knack, among other things, of displacing, if I may
say so, the area of the hole in a manner that allows for certain
effects.

在我对《尤利西斯》字里行间的理解和摸索走到这一
步后，我想指出这个交错中使人感觉到存在一个空缺（洞）
（hole）的是什么。事实上，为了解释，我们会想使用一个
图示将自杀、改名以及布鲁姆拒绝看到他亡父遗容的行为
纳入其中。如果所有这些都在章节“喀耳刻”中得以重现，且
在其中被认为出现了一种幻觉，那么这将是非常恰当的。



- 203 -

但这是远远不够的，即使其中有一些真相，也仍不足以使
文本有如此的功能，例如，解释第一段摘录的第一节中的
“可怜的爸爸！可怜的人！”（在“每句话都讲得那么深沉，
利奥波德。”之后）。这些话是父亲对戏剧的评论，然后是：
“可怜的爸爸！可怜的人！”，这也许对父亲的评论不那么和
善。“幸而我不曾进屋去瞻仰他的遗容。那是怎样的一天啊！
哎呀，天哪！[. . .]喏，也许这样对他最好不过。”简而言之，
有一系列需要解释的东西，尤其是“喀耳刻”所包含的戏剧性
的再分配中所产生的效果。因为所有的这些结合在一起，
才产生了作用，事情恰恰与给人感觉存在一个空缺的东西
相伴而生。确切地说，除此之外，乔伊斯有一种本领，如
果我可以这么说的话，就是以这样一种方式使空缺之处发
生移置，从而达到某种效果。

For example, in the given quotation the son’s voice is not
mentioned, no more than the father’s death. On the other hand,
an effect is produced by the son’s voice, which is displaced in a
reply, but a son’s voice that bears a certain savoir-faire about the
signifier. This precaution, this ability that is to be supposed, to
be sub-posed, can be seen growing in accordance with an
altogether eloquent logic. I spoke of the rhetorical eloquence of
Mosenthal, within periods, articulated in the style of j’en ai



- 204 -

marre, marabout … : Mosenthal … All that … all that’s left of
him. ‘All that’s left of him’ is all that remains of him. But it’s
also all that’s to the left of him, on his left. If one thinks of what
the Creed stipulates on the respective places of the Father and
the Son on high, this says a great deal about their relations. All
that’s left of him, a name, an author’s name; all that’s to the left
of him, something therefore that either way is not a true son.
Let’s stop there.

例如，在所给的引文中，儿子的声音未被提及，就像
父亲的死亡一样。另一方面，儿子的声音在回答中被移置，
但这个儿子的声音带有某种关于能指的“做的知识” （savoir-
faire），从而产生了一种效果。这种预防措施，这种被设想
的能力，以及被置之于下（be sub-posed）25的能力，可以被
看作是按照一种极具说服力的逻辑在发展。我谈到了修辞
学上莫森索尔的说服力，在各个完整的句子中（within
periods ），以“ 我受够了，Marabout...”[j'en ai marre,
marabout...]26的方式衔接：莫森索尔...但所剩...所剩无几了。

25 字面意思是“下–置”（under-put）；Aubert 通过引入sous 作为其第一个音节，
对supposer 进行双关。然后（见下文）扩展为“帮凶”（suppôt ‘underling’）和
“潜于器皿/表皮之下”（sous-pot(-peau?) ‘under-pot/-skin’）间的文字游戏。
26 j’en ai marre, marabout ...来自于一个名为Marabout de ficelle 的儿童校园游戏，
这个游戏改编自一首著名的童谣《三只小猫》（Trois petits chats）。它采用一种
密集的叠音形式，以音素（而非全词）为基础，形成一种首尾相接的形式。它
经常被两两对唱，并伴有拍手游戏。（译者注：词语接龙+你拍一我拍一）



- 205 -

“但所剩无几了。”（All that's left of him）意思是他所留下的
所有。但也有在他左边的一切事物，在他左边的意思。如
果考虑到信经（the Creed）中对圣父和圣子在上面各自位置
的规定（译者注：圣子坐在圣父之右），这便充分说明了他
们的关系。他所剩下的所有，一个名字，一个作者的名字；
他左边的所有东西，所以，无论如何都不是一个真正的儿
子。我们就止步于此吧。

What's quite sure is that this gives pleasure to Bloom and
that this had been understood. And how do we see this? It's
because the father is not at all happy. The following retort
begins:
Rudolph (Severely.) - One night they bring you home drunk [. . .].

可以肯定的是，这让布鲁姆爽到了，而且这是我们已
经知道了的。而我们是如何得出这一结论的呢？这是因为
父亲一点也不高兴。接下来父亲便怼了回来：
鲁道尔夫（严厉地）– 有天晚上，他们送你回家，你醉
得像死狗［. . .］




- 206 -

If you please, no out-of-place humour, let's speak instead
about your transgressions. Bloom's jubilation, Bloom who has
prudently said what he had to say, and which pleases everyone.

请你别这么没眼色的乱开玩笑，让我们谈谈你干过的
蠢事吧。布鲁姆带来了欢快的气息，布鲁姆审慎地说出了
他想表达的意思，这让所有人都感到高兴。

However, within this series of effects, some of which I’ve
just isolated, there is another sort of cascade: another effect
develops, which is in some respect structural in relation to the
previous effects, a sort of result of the previous effects. This
game in relation to the father seems to slide to the side of the
mother. The father, who is contested in various ways, leads to a
mother on the side of the imaginary.

然而，在我刚刚所指出的这一系列的效果中，其中的
一些之间存在一种叠续（cascade）：另一种效果的发展，在
某种程度上与之前的效果有结构上的关联，是之前效果的
某种结果。这个与父亲有关的游戏似乎滑向了母亲的一边。
以各种方式受到挑战的父亲，导致了想象（imaginary）中
的母亲的出现。



- 207 -


So, Rudolph has mentioned his son's transgression, when
he came home drunk, having spent all his money, and also when
he came home covered in mud. 'Nice spectacles for your poor
mother!' He wasn't the one who was displeased, it was her!

所以，鲁道尔夫细数他儿子干过的蠢事，当他花光了
钱醉醺醺地回家时，还有当他满身是泥地回家时。“你那可
怜的母亲要是看见了该怎么说！”生气的不是他，而是她!

But the way this comes about, the way it is passed on to the
mother by means of the mud, is rather funny: those of you
who've read A Portrait of the Artist in English may have noticed
that 'mud' is also a familiar term for 'mother'. This gets
associated with a pantomime (p. 67 in the Viking edition). It's a
little sketch, of the epiphany type (I'm using the term with a
little provocation): in one of the first chapters of A Portrait Joyce
placed a series of sketches where the child Stephen, young
Stephen, is finding his way around in Dublin, based on a certain
number of points, scenes, sites, and houses. He is seated in a
house (generally the scenes start like that), on a chair, in the
kitchen of his aunt who is reading the evening paper and



- 208 -

admiring 'the beautiful Mabel Hunter', a beautiful actress. A
young girl with ringletted hair approaches
on tiptoe to peer at the picture and said softly:
 — What is she in, mud?
 — In a pantomime, love.

但这种方式，通过泥巴传给母亲的方式，相当有趣：
读过《一位青年艺术家的肖像》英文版的人可能会注意到，
“泥巴”（mud）也是“母亲”的一个常见称谓。这与一个童话
剧27有关（维京版p. 67）。这是一个简短的情节，读懂它需
要有慧根（epiphany，我使用这个词带有些许挑衅意味）：
在《一位青年艺术家的肖像》的第一章中，乔伊斯放置了
一系列简短的情节，在其中，小斯蒂芬，年轻的斯蒂芬，
正在通过一些位置、场景、地点和房子，在都柏林寻找他
的道路。他坐在一间房子里（通常场景都是这样开始的），
坐在他姑姑的厨房里的椅子上，姑姑正在看晚报，欣赏着 "
美丽的梅布尔·亨特"，一个美丽的女演员。一个满头鬈发的
小姑娘走过来
踮着脚走过来，偷看那张图片，她柔和地说：
— 她站在什么地方，泥里面？

27 译者注：起源于英格兰后传播于英语国家的一种戏剧形式，多于圣诞节期间
演出。



- 209 -

— 她在演一出童话剧，小乖乖。

Now, it so happens that the passage from ‘Circe’ that I was
speaking about just now slides into the mud because this
signifier, ‘mud’, comes back three or four times in the passage,
sliding from mud to the emergence of the mother: ‘Nice
spectacles for your poor mother!’, says Rudolph, and Bloom
says, ‘Mamma!’, because she is appearing at that very instant.
(In ‘Circe’, as soon as certain words, certain signifiers, are
introduced, the object, as it were, comes to the surface.) And in
what way? ‘In pantomime dame’s stringed mobcap, widow
Twankey’s crinoline and bustle, blouse with muttonleg sleeves’,
following the logic of English pantomime, that is to say, a man
disguised as a woman (these pantomime shows are played in
particular around Christmas, and involve generalized cross-
dressing: panto-mime).

现在，恰好我刚才讲的“喀耳刻”中的这段话滑动到了泥
（mud），因为“泥”这个能指在这段话中出现了三四次，从
泥滑动到了母亲的出现：“你那可怜的母亲要是看见了该怎
么说！”鲁道尔夫说，而布鲁姆说：“妈妈！”，因为她就在
那一刻出现了。（在“喀耳刻”中，只要某些词，某些能指被



- 210 -

引入，这一对象就会浮现出来。）那么是以何种方式出现的
呢？“头戴圣诞童话剧中贵妇人的系带式室内女帽，身穿寡
妇吐安基式带硬布衬垫加后撑架的裙子，背后扣扣的羊腿
袖女式衬衫”，按英国童话剧的逻辑，也就是说男扮女装
（这种童话剧表演主要在圣诞节前后上演，普遍涉及异性
装扮：泛–女性（panto-mime））。

So, women's dress. But something else resonates here,
because at the very start of Ulysses the mother has been
mentioned in relation to pantomime (pp. 9-10). Indeed, after
having mentioned his dead mother, Stephen says:
Where now?
Her secrets: old featherfans, tasselled dancecards, powdered with musk,
a gaud of amber beads in her locked drawer. A birdcage hung in the
sunny window of her house when she was a girl. She heard old Royce sing
in the pantomime of Turko the Terrible and laughed with others when he
sang:
I am the boy
That can enjoy
Invisibility.
Phantasmal mirth, folded away: muskperfumed.




- 211 -

就是如此，女性的服装。但这里还有其他的共鸣，因
为在《尤利西斯》一开始就已经提到了母亲与童话剧的关
系(pp. 9-10)。事实上，在提及他已故的母亲后，斯蒂芬说：
而今在何处？
她的秘藏：她那上了锁的抽屉里有几把陈旧的羽毛扇、
麝香熏过的带穗子的舞会请帖和一串廉价的琥珀珠子。
少女时代，她家那浴满阳光的窗户上挂着一只鸟笼。
她曾听过老罗伊斯在童话剧《可怕的土耳克》中演唱，
而当他这么唱的时候，她就跟旁人一起笑了：
我就是那男孩
I am the boy
能够领略随心所欲的
That can enjoy
隐身的愉快。
Invisibility.
幻影般的欢乐被贮存起来了：用麝香熏过的。

There appears here a phantasmatic whole that is linked to
the mother, through Stephen's stumbling, with a radical
ambiguity: what was she laughing at? At old Royce singing? At
what he was saying? At the sound of his voice? Goodness
knows what else.



- 212 -


经由斯蒂芬磕磕巴巴的诉说，这里出现了一整套与母
亲相联系的幻影，带有一种根本的模糊性：她在笑什么？
在笑老罗伊斯的歌声？在笑他说的话？在笑他的声音？天
知道还有些什么别的。

It turns out that this mother, this problematic mother, is
dressed precisely as the mother in the Aladdin pantomime,
Widow Twankey, is dressed. The Widow Twankey blouse is the
blouse worn by Aladdin's mother in the pantomimes, a mother
who clearly understood nothing of what he was up to, apart from
the fact that in rubbing the lamp the genie within was being
made to talk.

事实证明，这位母亲，这位令人疑惑的母亲，其穿着
正是阿拉丁童话剧中的母亲寡妇吐安基的穿着。寡妇吐安
基式衬衫正是童话剧中阿拉丁的母亲所穿的衬衫，这位母
亲显然对他所做的事情一无所知，只知道擦一擦神灯就能
让里面的灯神说话。

I shall leave this point here in order to move onto another
aspectof the functioning of the text.



- 213 -


对于这一点我将暂且搁置，转向到文本运作的另一个
方面。

Ellen Bloom, who has just emerged, is not at all like the
father on the side of the elders of Zion. Rather she sounds like
she is on the side of Catholic religion, apostolic and Roman, for
what does she say when she sees him all covered in mud?
O blessed Redeemer, what have they done to him!
[. . .] Sacred Heart of Mary, where were you at all at all?

刚刚提到的艾琳·布鲁姆（母亲）完全不像父亲那样，
处于锡安长老的一面。相反，她听起来像是处于天主教、
使徒教义和罗马教廷的一面，因为当她看到他（布鲁姆）
浑身是泥的时候，她是怎么说的？
噢，神圣的救世主，把这孩子给糟践成什么样子啦！
［. . .］圣母圣心啊，你到底在哪儿呢，在哪儿呢？

This is rather curious because one would have expected the
Sacred Heart of Jesus instead. In a certain way, this betrays her
narcissistic relation to religion: she is very clearly Catholic, in a
way that it was possible to be in the nineteenth century, and this



- 214 -

is a dimension that deserves to be noted when one speaks about
Joyce, even if one has to go looking in the more benign texts,
Stephen Hero and Dubliners: an imaginary relation to religion is
ascertained in the mother.

这是相当奇怪的，因为人们本会期盼耶稣的圣心而非
圣母的。在某种程度上，这透露了她与宗教的自恋关系：
一个值得注意的方面是，显而易见她是天主教徒，以一种
在19 世纪身为天主教徒所可能的方式，当人们谈论乔伊斯
时，即使人们更多地需要在良性文本中寻得，《斯蒂芬英雄》
和《都柏林人》：在母亲身上可以观察到一种想象中的宗教
关系。

I would like to indicate this first of all in relation to
epiphany. What is known as 'epiphany' signifies many things
that are fairly diverse. Joyce defined epiphany once only, in
Stephen Hero, and of course what he said has been slightly
twisted. Here is the definition:
By an epiphany he meant a sudden spiritual manifestation, whether in the
vulgarity of speech or of gesture or in a memorable phase of the mind
itself.




- 215 -

我想首先指出这与顿悟有关。所谓的“顿悟”（epiphany）
有很多不同含义。乔伊斯只对顿悟有过一次定义，是在
《斯蒂芬英雄》中，当然他所说的内容被稍稍曲解了。以
下是定义：
一场顿悟，无论是在粗俗的语言、手势里，还是在头
脑中难忘的片段，于他而言都是种突然的灵魂显现。

This is a definition that is polished, didactic, and redolent
of Aquinas. But it is slotted into a text that over two pages takes
us from a dialogue with his mother in which she reproaches
Stephen for his unbelief - she is constantly mentioning the
'priests' - to Stephen breaking away from her on this issue and,
at another level, circumventing the problem. In his discourse he
slips from the woman/priest relationship to the beloved. Then,
suddenly, he says that he started wandering round the streets,
and a Dublin incident that was 'keen enough to afflict his
sensitiveness' effectively 'set him composing some ardent verses'.
There is no further mention of the poem, but he reports the
dialogue he heard, a colloquy between a young lady and a young
gentleman. One of the few words that appears is the word
'chapel', a word that in Ireland designates a Catholic church (the
churches strictly speaking were occupied by the Anglican



- 216 -

church): apart from this word there is precious little but
suspension points in the dialogue.

这是一个有涵养的、说教式的、阿奎那式的定义。但
它被塞进了一段文字中，在这两页文字中，我们看到的是
他与母亲的对话，母亲责备斯蒂芬的不信任–她不断地提到
“神父”–而斯蒂芬在这个问题上与她决裂，另一方面又绕开
这个问题。在他的论述中，他从女人/神父的关系滑到了心
爱之人。然后，突然间，他说他开始在街上游荡，一件“尖
锐地刺激着他的敏感神经”的都柏林事件足以“让他写几行热
情的诗句”。并未详细提及诗的内容，但他提到了他所听到
的对话，一位年轻女士和一位年轻绅士之间的对话（译者
注：断断续续的对话）。出现的几个词中有一个是“小教堂”
（chapel），这个词在爱尔兰指的是天主教堂（严格意义上
的教堂是由圣公会所占有的）：除此之外，对话中除了省略
号外，几乎没有其他内容。

So, this dialogue which, from one angle, doesn’t present
anything, leads him to write a poem and, from the other angle,
he baptizes it and defines it over the following lines, in learned
fashion, as an ‘epiphany’. He adds that what he wanted to do
was to record these scenes, these realist and exceedingly



- 217 -

suggestive sketches. Therefore, we have a sort of duplication of
experience (let’s say, in order to simplify things, a realist side
and an in some sense poetic side), and a kind of elimination or
suppression of the poetic in the text of Stephen Hero. Now, the
elided poem was called ‘Vilanelle of the Temptress’, and it
emerged precisely within a certain discourse that involved his
mother, his mother in relation to the priests.

因此，这段没有呈现出任何东西的对话，一方面，使
他写了一首诗，而从另一个角度看，他在接下来的文字中，
以学术性的方式，将其洗礼并定义为“顿悟”。他补充说，他
想做的是记录这些场景，这些现实主义和极富暗示性的素
描。因此，我们有一种经验的双重性（简而言之，一个现
实主义的一面和一个在某种意义上的诗意的一面），以及一
种《斯蒂芬英雄》文本中诗意的消除或压制。现在，这首
被省略的诗被称为《魅魔维拉内拉》（Vilanelle of the
Temptress），它正是在某种话语中生成的，此种话语涉及他
母亲以及母亲与神父的关系。

This relationship, which I have roughly defined as an
imaginary relation to religion, is to be found again in different
ways in A Portrait of the Artist, for example with the sermons on



- 218 -

hell, which are interminable (both Kantian and very Sadean) and
which aim to present in detail the horrible tortures of hell, to
give in praesentia an idea of what hell is. Or in another way with
the figure of the confessor, who listens but also replies. What
does he reply? What does he say? It is precisely around all of
this that Stephen’s Easter duty revolves, the confession of his
turpitude, and also the function of the artist.

这种关系，我粗略地定义为与宗教的想象关系，在
《一位青年艺术家的肖像》中以不同的方式再次出现，例
如关于地狱的布道，这些布道是无休止的（既是康德式的，
也是非常萨德式的），其目的是详细介绍地狱的可怕折磨，
让人预先了解地狱是什么。或者以另一种方式，以忏悔者
的形象，他倾听，但也回应着。他的回应是什么？他说了
什么？斯蒂芬的复活节义务正是围绕着这一切展开的，对
自己罪行的忏悔，以及艺术家的功能。

Here I shall indicate two passages, from two texts, one that
is to be found at the start of Stephen Hero, where Stephen says
that in writing verse he was able to ‘combine the offices of
penitent and confessor’; the other towards the end of A Portrait
of the Artist when, mortified at seeing his beloved lending an ear



- 219 -

and smiling to a young well-scrubbed priest, he says that he has
renounced the priesthood, that the matter is settled, and that he
doesn’t stand on that side. He adds in so many words that it’s
fellows like the young priest, whom women confide in, who in
turn tell them everything in the half-light, and he says that he
would like to be there before she engenders someone of their
race, and that the effect of this word should even improve this
blasted race a little.

在这里，我将提到两段文字，一段是在《斯蒂芬英雄》
的开头，斯蒂芬说，在写诗时，他能够“将忏悔室和忏悔者
结合起来”；另一段是在《一位青年艺术家的肖像》的结尾，
当看到他所爱之人对一个白净俊朗的年轻神父洗耳倾听并
微笑时感到没面儿，他说，他已经放弃了从事神职，这以
成定局，那不适合他。他又补充说，女人向年轻的神父倾
诉，他们反过来对她们一切诉说都是在昏暗的隔间中进行
的，他说，他希望能在女人生下他们这一种族新的成员之
前加以干预，认为他的话将使这个该死的种族有所改善。

This is perhaps related to the famous ‘uncreated conscience’
that he speaks about on the last page. It goes by way of the ear,



- 220 -

the famous conception through the ear, which moreover we meet
in ‘Circe’.

这也许与他在最后一页谈到的著名的“尚未被创造的良
知”（uncreated conscience）有关。它以通过耳朵的方式生
效，即著名的通过耳朵怀孕（译者注：天主教会的传统信
念是，圣母玛利亚是通过将圣灵的气息引入她的耳朵怀上
耶稣的），此外，我们也在章节“喀耳刻”见到它。

Jacques Lacan - And which Jones insisted on a great deal.
Jones, Freud's pupil.

雅克·拉康：这也正是琼斯所极力坚持的。琼斯，弗洛
伊德的学生。

Another essential point concerning this imaginary
dimension of religion is thrown into relief in the famous passage
in Ulysses where there is an opposition between the Trinitarian
and problematic conception of theology and an ‘Italian’
Madonnaizing conception that plugs all the holes with an image
of Mary. He says that, indeed, the Catholic Church did rather
well in founding everything ‘upon the void’, ‘upon incertitude’.



- 221 -

It seems to me that in these texts the names of the father are
playing out at various different levels.

雅克·奥伯特：是的，在《尤利西斯》的著名段落中，
关于宗教的这一想象层面的另一个要点被加以强调，在这
段文本中，三位一体的、问题性的神学概念与“意大利式”的
圣母化（Madonnaizing）概念之间存在着对立，后者用玛丽
亚的形象来填补所有的空缺。他说，的确，天主教教会将
一切都建立在“虚空”和“不确定”之上，并在这一点上做的相
当好。在我看来，在这些文本中，父之名在多个层面上发
挥着作用。

In 'Circe', however, and in Ulysses as a whole, what sets
things in motion, what produces the artifice, is the game of hide-
and-seek with the names of the father, that is to say, alongside
everything that gives the impression of being a hole there are
displacements of the hole and there are displacements of the
name of the father.

然而，在“喀耳刻”以及整个《尤利西斯》中，使事情发
生变化的、制造把戏的，是与父之名捉迷藏的游戏，也就



- 222 -

是说，在一切给人以空缺之感的存在的旁边，有着空缺的
移置，父之名的移置。

In passing, we have seen, rather haphazardly, Abraham,
Jacob, Moses, Virag, and Dedalus too, and another who is rather
funny. In one of the central episodes, where there's one eye,
'Cyclops', we meet a certain J. J. whom, you will recall, if you
have some memory of it, we met in a previous episode under the
name J. J. O'Molloy. The 'O' means 'descended from'. J. J., son
of Molloy. His position is rather curious. He's a man of law, but
a man of law - I won't say altogether diminished, but on the
wane. We are told (and here the English words are interesting)
that his clientele is declining, 'practice dwindling', and this is
because of his 'gambling'. His gambling has in some way
superseded his practice.

我们随意地顺带提到了，亚伯拉罕、雅各布、摩西、
维拉格和迪达勒斯，还有一个相当有趣的人。在其中的一
个中心情节，一只眼“独眼巨人”（Cyclops）的章节中，我
们见到了某个J. J.，如果你还记得的话，我们在之前的一个
