情节中见过他，名叫杰·杰·奥莫洛伊（J. J. O’Molloy）。这个
“O”是“后代”的意思。J. J.，Molloy 的儿子。他的职业相当



- 223 -

古怪。他是一个律师，但，是一个——我不会说是已然完
全衰颓了的，但正在衰颓的路上的——律师。我们被告知
（这里的英语用词很有意思），他的客户在减少，“业务在缩
减”，而这是因为他的“投机”。投机在某种程度上已经取代
了他的业务。

Clearly something would have to be developed from this.
What I would simply like to indicate is the function of this
perfectly false father who bears the initials of both James Joyce
and John Joyce, Joyce’s father. What’s more, it’s remarkable that
the words of this J. J. O’Molloy bear on other fathers. In a
passage that links up with the riddle that Dr Lacan quoted last
week (the ‘Aeolus’ episode, which is set in a newspaper office),
it is he who turns to Stephen to give him a fine piece of rhetoric,
which also holds some interest. We have learnt that O’Molloy,
after having taken up gambling, started doing ‘some literary
work’ for the press. We should note in passing that this also
refers back to ‘The Dead’, the last of the short stories in
Dubliners, where Gabriel Conroy, the main character, writes ‘a
literary column’ for newspapers, but we don’t know much about
it (this appears in yet another manner in Exiles). What kind of
literature? Is it the kind of literature that deserves to live?



- 224 -

Gabriel asks himself the question, and we shall see that he isn’t
the only one.

显然，这其中有很多东西需要展开来讲。我仅仅想指
出的是这个完全虚假父亲的功能，他同时带有詹姆斯·乔伊
斯和约翰·乔伊斯（乔伊斯的父亲）的大写的名字的首字母。
更重要的是，这个杰·杰·奥莫洛伊谈及他者的父亲的话是显
著的。在一段与拉康博士上周引用的谜语相联系的段落中
（“风神”（Aeolus）的章节，它发生在报社编辑室），是他
转向斯蒂芬，给他讲了一段精美的修辞，这也跟有趣。我
们已经了解到，奥莫洛伊（O’Molloy）在从事投机之后，
开始为报社做“一些攥文工作”。顺便一提，这也重新提及了
《都柏林人》中最后一篇短篇小说《往生者》（The Dead），
在其中主人公加贾伯瑞·康罗伊为报纸的“文学专栏”写文章，
但我们对其了解的并不多（这在《流亡》（Exiles）中以另
一种方式呈现）。什么样的文学？它是那种值得留存下来的
文学吗？贾伯瑞问自己这个问题，我们将看到，他并非是
唯一这么做的人。

So, we're told that J. J. O’Molloy turns to Stephen and
presents him with a fine specimen of legal eloquence (pp. 134-
5):



- 225 -


因此，我们了解到，杰·杰·奥莫洛伊转向斯蒂芬，向他
展示了一个精练的司法雄辩的范例(pp. 134-5)：

J. J. O’Molloy turned to Stephen and said quietly and slowly:
 — One of the most polished periods I think I ever listened to in my life
fell from the lips of Seymour Bushe. It was in that case of fratricide, the
Childs murder case. Bushe defended him.
[Then comes a little Shakespearian aside:] And in the porches of mine
ear did pour. [Hamlet]
By the way how did he find that out? He died in his sleep. Or the other
story, beast with two backs? [This is Stephen's cogitation.]
 — What was that? the professor asked.
ITALIA, MAGISTRA ARTIUM
 [One of the titles that punctuate the episode in the newspaper office.]
— He spoke on the law of evidence, J. J. O’Molloy said, of Roman justice
as contrasted with the earlier Mosaic code, the lex talionis. And he cited
the Moses of Michelangelo in the vatican.
 — Ha.
 — A few wellchosen words, Lenehan prefaced. Silence!
[. . .]
J. J. O’Molloy resumed, moulding his words:



- 226 -

 — He said of it: that stony effigy in frozen music, horned and terrible, of
the human form divine, that eternal symbol of wisdom and of prophecy
which, if aught that the imagination or the hand of sculptor has wrought
in marble of soultransfigured and of soultransfiguring deserves to live,
deserves to live.

杰·杰·奥莫洛伊转向斯蒂芬，安详而慢腾腾地说：
— 在我听到过的申辩演说中，最精彩的正是出自西
摩·布什之口。那是在审理杀兄事件——蔡尔兹凶杀案。
布什替他辩护来着。
［然后是一段莎士比亚的旁白：］注入我的耳腔之内。
28［哈姆雷特］
顺便问一下，他是怎样发觉的呢？他是正在睡着的时
候死的呀。还有另外那个双背禽兽29的故事呢？
— 演说的内容是什么？教授问。
意大利，艺术女神（ITALIA, MAGISTRA ARTIUM）
［报社情节中的其中一个标题。］
— 他谈的是《罗马法》的证据法，杰·杰·奥莫洛伊说，
把它拿来跟古老的《摩西法典》——也就是说，跟

28 译者注：这是哈姆雷特王子之父的亡灵对他说的话。亡灵说，自己的兄弟怎
样把毒药注入他的耳腔，害死他后娶了王后，见《哈姆雷特》第1 幕第5 场。
29 译者注：“双背禽兽”暗喻男女交媾（见《奥赛罗》第1 幕第1 场）。在《哈
姆雷特》第1 幕第5 场中，亡灵对哈姆雷特王子说，克劳狄斯是个“奸淫的畜
生”，而王后只是“外表上装得非常贞淑”。



- 227 -

《同态复仇法》（lex talionis）30——相对照。于是，他
就举出安置于罗马教廷的米开朗琪罗的雕塑“摩西”做例
证。
— 嗬。
— 讲几句恰当的话，利内翰做了开场白。肃静！
[. . .]
— 杰·杰·奥莫洛伊字斟句酌地说下去：
— 他是这么说的：那座堪称为冻结的音乐31的石像，
那个长了犄角32的可怕的半神半人的形象，那智慧与预
言的永恒象征。倘若雕刻家凭着想象力和技艺，用大
理石雕成的那些净化了的灵魂和正在净化着的灵魂的
化身，就作为艺术品所拥有的永垂不朽的价值而言，
它是当之无愧的。

Thus, having begun to turn himself into a sounding board
of legal knowledge, having specified the law of evidence in
relation to other sorts of law, O'Molloy makes Bushe speak,
makes the Bush speak, giving voice to a rhetorical testimony on

30 译者注：原文为拉丁文。指惩罚暴行要以命偿命，以牙还牙。见《出埃及记》
第21 章第23 至25 节。下文中提到的“摩西”，指米开朗琪罗于一五一三年至
一五一六年间所雕的石像。都柏林法院的门廊里也有一座“摩西”石像。
31 译者注：德国哲学家弗里德里希·谢林（1775—1854）在《艺术哲学》中说：
建筑乃是“空间的音乐，犹如冻结的音乐”。
32 译者注：由于《圣经》翻译中的谬误，中古时期人们都以为摩西头上长角，
米开朗琪罗及其他艺术家均按此塑造其形象。



- 228 -

art as founding the right to existence ('deserves to live') and the
right to existence of the work of art. One can grasp the
resonance that this carries in relation to newspaper literature: art
founds, in law, the bearer of the Law, Moses, since he shall
remain as the Vatican Moses (this is how he is designated, 'the
Moses [. . .] in the vatican'): which is not uninteresting when one
bears in mind what the Vatican represents in Ulysses, and
furthermore, when one bears in mind that the statue in question
is actually in San Pietro in Vincoli, the church of Saint Peter in
Chains.

因此，在开始把自己变成法律知识的传声筒之后，在
具体指明证据法与之相关的其他种类的法律之后，奥莫洛
伊让布什说话，让布什说话，发出了关于艺术的申辩证词，
作为生存权（值得存活‘deserves to live’）和艺术作品的生存
权的基础。人们可以把握这一点与报纸文学的共鸣：艺术
在法律上创立了法律的承载者摩西，因为他将继续作为梵
蒂冈的摩西（他就是被这样称呼的，“梵蒂冈的摩西”）：当
人们记起梵蒂冈在《尤利西斯》中所代表的是什么时，这
不无意义，更重要的是，当人们记起相关的雕像实际上位
于圣彼得锁链堂（San Pietro in Vincoli）之中时，这也不无
意义。



- 229 -


This ‘deserves to live’, which insists (through the rhetorical
‘deserves to live, deserves to live’), bears the stamp, is
countersigned, by its effects on the one for whom the ‘period’
was destined, namely Stephen. J. J. O’Molloy turns towards him
and it transpires that, ‘his blood wooed by grace of language and
gesture, [he] blushed.’ Curiously enough, Stephen’s blushing
forms a series with other texts by Joyce. I’m thinking in
particular of the passage from A Portrait that you might have
noticed: during a trip to Cork, Stephen goes with his father to
the anatomy theatre in the medical school where his father had
spent some time; very little time, it would seem. The father is in
search of his carved initials. No mention is made of the fact that
clearly these initials are also his (Simon Dedalus carries the
initials S. D., like Stephen Dedalus). But Stephen comes across
the word ‘FOETUS’ (also carved into the bench), which causes
a great effect on him. He blushes, then pales. Thus we meet
again, in relation to the initials, but in another relation, the
‘deserves to live’. I’ll add that this series can be extended with
another passage from Dubliners, again in ‘The Dead’, to which I
alluded earlier. The aforenamed Gabriel Conroy is about to give
a speech, the customary speech for a family gathering. He’s



- 230 -

always there when you need him to write in the newspaper or
make a little speech of this kind. And they’ve just been speaking,
precisely, about artists whose names have been forgotten, those
who’ve left nothing behind save a name that is altogether
problematic.

这种所坚持的“值得存活”，（通过能言善辩确立的“值得
存活，值得活下去”），带有印记，被连署，因为它对那段
“辩词”（period）注定的接收人，即对斯蒂芬产生了影响。
杰·杰·奥莫洛伊转向他，结果发现，“那些辞藻和手势的优
美使得斯蒂芬受到感染，他涨红了脸。”奇怪的是，斯蒂芬
的脸红已在乔伊斯的文本中形成了一个系列。我特别想到
了《一位青年艺术家的肖像》中的一段话，你们可能已经
注意到了：在去科克的旅行中，斯蒂芬和他的父亲一起去
了医学院的解剖剧场，他的父亲在那里待过一段时间；似
乎是很短的时间。父亲在寻找他镌刻的名字的缩写。没有
提到的是，显然这些也是他（斯蒂芬）的名字的首字母的
缩写（西蒙·迪达勒斯（斯蒂芬的父亲（Simon Dedalus）与
斯蒂芬·迪达勒斯（Stephen Dedalus）的首字母缩写都是S.
D.）。但是斯蒂芬看到了 “FOETUS”（胎儿）这个词（也刻
在工作台上），这对他造成了强烈的影响。他脸红了，然后
脸色苍白。因此，我们再次发现，与首字母有关，但却是



- 231 -

另一种关系，即“值得存活”（deserves to live）。我要补充的
是，这一系列关联可以通过《都柏林人》中的另一段话展
开来讲，还是在《往生者》（The Dead）中，我在前面提到
过。之前提到的贾伯瑞·康罗伊（Gabriel Conroy）即将发表
的讲话，这是一个传统的家庭聚会的讲话。在你们需要他
在报纸上写文章或做这种小演讲的时候他总会出现。而他
们刚才的谈话，准确地说，关于那些名字已经被遗忘的艺
术家，那些除了一个完全无法确定的名字之外什么都没留
下的艺术家。

“His name,” said Aunt Kate, “was Parkinson. I heard him when he was
in his prime and I think he had then the purest tenor voice that was ever
put into a man’s throat.”

“他名叫帕金森，”凯特姨妈说，“我在他鼎盛时期听
过他演唱，我认为那时他的嗓音是最纯净的男高音。”

This gives him pause for thought, and he takes off from
there, concluding one of the first 'periods' of his speech with two
things: an echo of a song called 'Love's Old Sweet Song', which
evokes the lost paradise in its opening line; and a quotation from
Milton (though not Paradise Lost) which says the following, 'I



- 232 -

hope that I may leave something so written to aftertimes, that
they shall not willingly let it die.'

这给了他思考的机会，他以此为出发点，用两件事结
束了他讲话中的第一段“演讲”：对一首名为《Love's Old
Sweet Song》的歌曲的呼应，这首歌的开场白唤起了失落的
天堂；以及引用弥尔顿（但不是《失乐园》中的）的一段
话：“我希望我可以为后世写下一些东西，使他们不愿意让
它消逝。”

So it is that the question of the right to existence, the
question of the right to creation, the question of validity, and the
question of certitude, too, find themselves knotted into Joyce's
discourse.

因此，存在权的问题、创作权的问题、正当性的问题
和确定性的问题，也在乔伊斯的话语中找到了他们自己交
织的结点。

Another thing concerning the bush: the eloquent Bushe, in
speaking of Moses, is also speaking about a Holy Bush, the
Holy Bush in the Bible. The Lord says to Moses that 'the place



- 233 -

whereon thou standest is holy ground', the ground before the
burning bush. The holy bush turns out to have a certain
relationship with the 'fox'. For when J. J. O'Molloy reappears in
Circe, he has a 'foxy moustache' and something of barrister
Bushe about him. This is a fox that we have seen more than
once in A Portrait, where of course it appears because Fox is one
of the nicknames for Parnell, associated with his fault. But it is
also very precisely a kind of signifier of dissimulation: 'He was
not foxing', says the young Stephen when he is in the infirmary
and afraid that he will be accused of skiving. And then, a little
later, when he has just renounced joining the orders, right after
picturing to himself his imaginary calling card, 'The Reverend
Stephen Dedalus, S. J.', he wonders what kind of face he would
have, and one of the things that comes to his mind is 'one of the
Jesuits whom some [. . .] called Lantern Jaws and others Foxy
Cambell'.

关于布什的另一件事：雄辩的布什在说到摩西时，也
是在说一个神圣的荆棘，即《圣经》中的神圣的荆棘。主
对摩西说：'你所站的地方就是圣地'，就是燃烧的荆棘前的
地面。神圣的荆棘原来与“狐狸”有某种关系。因为当
杰·杰·奥莫洛伊在章节“喀耳刻”中再次出现时，他留着“狐



- 234 -

狸般的胡子”，身上有一些大律师布什的特征。这是一个我
们在《一位青年艺术家的肖像》中不止一次见到的狐狸，
当然它出现在那里是因为狐狸是帕内尔的绰号之一，与他
的过失有关。但它也是一种非常确切的虚伪的能指：“他不
是装病”，年轻的斯蒂芬在医务室里害怕被指责为溜号时这
样说道。然后，在片刻之后，当他刚刚放弃加圣职时，在
向自己描绘了他想象中的名片“耶稣教会神父斯蒂芬·迪德勒
斯”之后，他想知道自己会有一张什么样的脸，他想到的其
中之一是“曾经有一个耶稣会的神父，有些孩子叫他灯笼下
巴颏儿，另一些孩子又叫他狐狸将军”。

So, we have this bush / fox series. But there is also some
wordplay, which functions for ‘Molloy’ / ‘Molly’ too, articulated
on ‘holy’. We had ‘holly’ / ‘holy’, ‘Molly’ / ‘Molloy’, and
another word that doesn’t appear in Ulysses but of which Joyce
says – I’m tugging this by the sleeve, or rather by the letters, but
after all, he wrote those letters – he reveals to us the name of
something that is supposed to enter the functioning of 'Circe',
namely the plant, the golden garlic, which Hermes gave to
Ulysses so that he would be able to resist Circe, and which is
called ‘moly’. The curious thing is that between the two,
between ‘moly’ and ‘Molly’, there is a difference which belongs



- 235 -

to the realm ‘f pho’ation. What is ’phonized’ in Ulysses is
‘Molly’, with a single vowel, and the ‘moly’ he speaks about is a
diphthong, a ditongue as it used to be written. The ditongue (di-
tongue?) transforms into a consonant at the same time as the
diphthong transforms into a single vowel. There is consonantal
duplication, a duplication of consonance, and this consonance is
what appears in Ulysses in the shape of ‘Molly’.

因此，我们有这个荆棘/狐狸系列。但还有一个文字游
戏，作用于‘Molloy’ / ‘Molly’，衔接于‘holy’上。我们有‘冬
青’（holly）/‘神圣’（holy），‘Molloy’ / ‘Molly’，以及另一
个在《尤利西斯》中没有出现的词，但乔伊斯说–我是从袖
子里扯出来的，或者说从字母中来扯出来的，但毕竟是他
写的这些字母–他向我们揭示了应该进入章节“喀耳刻”发挥
作用的东西的名字，即赫尔墨斯给尤利西斯的植物，金蒜，
这样他就能抵抗喀耳刻，它被称为‘moly’。奇怪的是，在这
两者之间，在‘moly’和‘Molly’之间，存在着发音上的差异。
在“尤利西斯”中被“音化”（phonation）的是‘Molly’，有一个
元音，而他所说的‘moly’是一个双元音，即过去所写的
ditongue。在双元音转变为单元音的同时，双重元音/双重–
发音（ditongue/di-tongue?）也转变为辅音。有辅音重复，



- 236 -

辅音的一种重复，这种辅音在《尤利西斯》中是以‘Molly’
的形式出现的。

He says two or three curious things about 'moly'. ’r Lacan
is going to analyse one of them, I believe; I’ll make do with
pointing out one other. It is a gift from Hermes, god of public
thoroughfares, and ‘the invisible influence (prayer, chance,
agility, presence of mind, power of recuperation) which saves in
case of accident.’ Thus, this is something that confirms Bloom
in his role as the prudent one. In the end, he is one who
corresponds fairly well to the definition that can be found in the
entry in the Lalande philosophical dictionary on this question of
prudence (which is rather disappointing, probably because it is
above all Saint Thomas who speaks about it). There is a short
authorless note that says the following: ‘Prudence. The ability,
in choosing one’s means, to obtain for oneself the greatest well-
being.’ Bloom seems to be saying that this is precisely how one
gets by.

他说了两三件关于‘moly’的奇怪事情。我相信拉康博士
会分析其中一件；我只想指出另外一件。它是来自边界及
穿越边界的旅行者之神赫尔墨斯的礼物，是“无形的作用



- 237 -

（祈祷、机会、敏捷、心灵之所在、恢复之力），在发生意
外时可以救人”。因此，这一点对布鲁姆身为谨慎者的角色
进行了证实。最后，他是一个相当符合Lalande 这一哲学术
语中对于审慎这一问题条目的定义的人（这相当令人失望，
可能是因为主要是圣托马斯在谈论它）。有一个简短的佚名
注释，说到：“审慎。在选择自己的手段时，为自己获得最
大福祉的能力。”布鲁姆似乎是在说，这正是一个人应对生
活的方式。

The second thing that I would like to stress is that there is
an ongoing question about certainty and how to ground it.
This certainty reappears precisely with respect to the
famous Virag. I didn't tell you everything. I came to a halt in the
famous quotation where O’Molloy is telling the others about
Virag:

我想强调的第二件事是，有一个一直存在着的关于确
实性（certainty）的问题，以及如何使其确立的问题。
这种确实性的再次出现正是与著名的维拉格（Virag）
相关。关于他，我未曾对你们交底。我在谈论奥莫洛伊
（O’Molloy）向其他人讲述维拉格的那段著名引文时戛然
而止：



- 238 -


His name was Virag, the father’s name that poisoned himself. He changed
it by deed poll, the father did.
 — That’s the new Messiah for Ireland! says the citizen. Island of saints
and sages!
 — Well, they’re still waiting for their redeemer, says Martin. For that
matter so are we.
 — Yes, says J. J., and every male that’s born they think it may be their
Messiah. And every jew is in a tall state of excitement, I believe, till he
knows if he’s a father or a mother.

他原来姓维拉格，是他那个服毒自杀的父亲的姓。他
父亲凭着一纸平边契据就把他的姓改了。
— 这正是爱尔兰的新救世主！“市民”说。圣者和贤人
的岛屿！
— 喏，他们至今还在等待着救世主，马丁说。就这一
点而论，咱们何尝不是这样。
— 是呀，杰·杰说。每生一个男孩儿，他们就认为那
可能是他们的弥赛亚。而且我相信，每一个犹太人都
总是处于高度亢奋状态，直到他晓得那是个父亲还是
母亲。




- 239 -

I’ll simply stress what is perhaps appearing over and above
the humour that constitutes part of the functioning of the text of
‘Cyclops’. It’s barroom humour, but it’s very much present.
Moreover, it’s a brand of humour that would have to be linked to
other problems that bear on anti-Semitism in Joyce, but I won’t
have time to tackle that now. There is an imaginary
identification that situates another question: the problematic of
the Messiah and, through this, the problematic of succession.
The problem of the king’s word that grounds legitimation, a
word that enables one, even if the mother’s belly has been
mendacious, to land back on one’s feet through legitimation.
Legitimation, that is to say, the possibility of bearing the stamp
of the king, the crown, στέφανος (Stéphanos), or else to bear
this other stamp that appears in ‘Circe’ with Virag, the
grandfather, who ‘chutes rapidly down through the chimneyflue’,
bearing the label basilicogrammate, ‘the king’s grammate’. This
problematic of legitimacy that turns out to be problematic
legitimation is perhaps assuming here the figure of the
imaginary dimension and its recuperation.

我只想强调可能出现在以及超越了幽默的，且构成
《独眼巨人》章节功能的一部分的东西。这是一种酒吧式



- 240 -

幽默，但它是绝对存在的。此外，这种幽默还可以与其他
乔伊斯作品中与反犹主义相关的问题联系起来，但我现在
没有时间来这么做。这里有一种想象中的认同，将另一个
问题置于其中：关于弥赛亚的问题，以及通过这个问题所
引出的继承权的问题。国王的话语是承认为正统嫡出的基
础，这个话语使得即使母亲的肚子里有奸情，也能通过合
法化（legitimation）为嫡出而重获正统之位。合法化为嫡出，
也就是说，有可能获得国王、王冠、胜利之冠（希腊语：
στέφανος/Stéphanos）的印记，或者获得“喀耳刻”中出现的
祖父维拉格的印记，他“迅速地顺着壁炉烟道滑下”，带有
“ 皇家文书” （拉丁语：basilicogrammate [the king's
grammate]）的标签。这个正统性的问题，原来是有问题的
正统，在这里也许是假定了想象维度及其所恢复的形式。

It seems to me that Joyce uses certainty and brings it onto
the stage in its relationships with the effects of the voice. Even if
a word, a paternal word, is challenged at the level of what it says,
it seems to suggest that something of it passes into the
personation, into what lies behind the personation, into what lies
on the side of phonation, perhaps, on the side of something that
is also something that 'deserves to live' in melody; perhaps
precisely because of this something that in spite of everything



- 241 -

has effects on the mother through melody. The mother's
'phantasmal mirth', which is mentioned at the beginning of
Ulysses bears very precisely on pantomime and old Royce (Roi-
Joyce), who used to sing in it. Something passes through the
melody, and perhaps not merely sentimentality. Of course, Irish
culture at the turn of the century was altogether pervaded by
melody, especially the melodies of Thomas Moore, which in
Finnegans Wake Joyce calls 'Moore's maladies'. Moreover, it
was in this that Joyce's father, John Joyce, exulted. But in this
art of the voice, of phonation, perhaps just enough of it was
passed on to the son.

在我看来，乔伊斯使用了确实性，并使其同与之相关
的发音效果上演对手戏。一个父系的词语，即使在它所言
说的层面上是有争议的，但它似乎暗示着它的某些东西进
入了角色化，进入了角色化背后的东西，进入了语音的一
边，也许，在某些东西的一边，这些事物也是“值得存活”
（deserves to live）的旋律；或许正因为如此，某种东西无
论如何都会通过旋律对母亲产生影响。在《尤利西斯》的
开头提到的母亲的“幻影般的欢乐”，正是与童话剧和曾在其
中唱歌的老罗伊斯（Roi-Joyce：巨子–乔伊斯）有关。有
些东西在旋律中传递，也许不仅仅是情感上的旋律。当然，



- 242 -

世纪之交的爱尔兰文化完全被旋律所充斥，尤其是托马
斯·摩尔的旋律，乔伊斯在《芬尼根的守灵夜》33中称之为
“摩尔病”（Moore's maladies）。此外，也正是在这里乔伊斯
的父亲约翰·乔伊斯因之而欢欣鼓舞。34但在这种声音的艺
术中，发音的艺术，也许有些什么恰好足够传递给儿子。

In short, while this certainty with respect to what he makes
always has something to do with the mirror, with these mirror
effects that would have to be enumerated, this also has to do
with the voice-effects of the signifier. I would like to remind you
that 'The Dead', with which Joyce concluded Dubliners, at a
crucial moment in his poetic production, at a time when things
became unblocked in some respect, the guiding idea of 'The
Dead' occurred to him when his brother told him about a
particular interpretation of one of Moore's melodies that staged a
dialogue between revenants and the living. Stanislaus said to
him that the fellow who sang it did so in an interesting way, in a
way that said something. Then, as if by chance, Joyce set to
writing 'The Dead'. And one of the centrepieces of this short

33 译者注：据传拉康仅读到该书的15 页就没读下去了。
34 译者注：《芬尼根的守灵夜》的主人公伊厄威克（Humphrey Chimpden
Earwicker）的创造原型是乔伊斯的父亲约翰·乔伊斯。据称，约翰·乔伊斯是
一位出色的业余男高音。



- 243 -

story is the moment when the protagonist's wife is mesmerized,
frozen into stone like the Moses statue, listening to a hoarse-
voiced singer singing this famous melody. What effect does this
have on the protagonist? It turns his wife into a symbol.

简而言之，虽然这种关于他所做之事的确定性总是与
镜子有关，与这些所需列举的镜子的效果有关，但这也与
能指的声音效果有关。我想提醒你们这篇乔伊斯以其作为
《都柏林人》收尾的《往生者》（The Dead），在他诗歌创作
的一个关键时刻，在事情在某些方面变得畅通无阻的时刻，
他的兄弟告诉他一种涉及亡灵与生者之间对话的，在对摩
尔的一首旋律的特殊阐释时，他有了《往生者》的指导思
想。Stanislaus（乔伊斯的弟弟）对他说，唱这首歌的人以
一种有趣的方式，以一种在说些什么的方式来歌唱。然后，
巧合般地，乔伊斯开始写《往生者》。这个短篇小说中最有
趣的内容之一是当主人公的妻子听着一个嗓音嘶哑的歌手
唱出这首著名的旋律时，她被迷住了，像摩西雕像一般地
僵住了。这对主人公产生了什么影响？这把他的妻子变成
了一个象征。

A woman was standing near the top of the first flight, in the shadow [. . .].
He asked himself what is a woman standing on the stairs in the shadow



- 244 -

[. . .] a symbol of.

在一楼上方楼梯口附近，一个女人站在阴影里。［. . .］
他在心中暗自揣摩，一个女人站在楼梯的阴影里［. . .］
象征着什么呢？

He describes her in vaguely realist terms, but at the same
time he asks what does that symbolizes. This symbolizes a
certain way of listening, among other things.

他用模糊的现实主义术语描述她，但同时他问：这象
征着什么。这象征着除了其他之外的某种倾听的方式。

Joyce wanted to set out the rules for this certainty, for the
problems of certainty and its grounding in relation to the voice’s
effects on the signifier, in an aesthetic science, but fairly quickly
he saw that this was not so firmly linked to science, and that it
was precisely a savoir-faire tethered by a practice of the signifier.
What is foremost in my mind, which imposes itself upon me
through and beyond what Aristotle said about praxis in his
Poetics (which gave Joyce pause for thought), is Lacan’s
definition: ‘a concerted action on the part of man [. . .] that puts



- 245 -

him en mésure, in a position, to deal with the real through the
symbolic.’ It is precisely in ‘Circe’ that we perceive this
question of mésure, when Bloom, entering the brothel, is seen
by Stephen as he turns. And this evocation of mésure is, as if by
chance, also a quotation from Revelation. I should doubtless
stop here before my talk becomes overly apocalyptic.

乔伊斯想在美学科学中为这种确定性，为确定性的问
题及其与声音对能指的影响相关的基础列出规则，但很快
他就意识到，这与科学的联系并没有那么牢固，它恰恰是
一种被能指的实践联系起来的做的知识（savoir-faire）。在
我的脑海中，最重要的，通过并超越了亚里士多德在他的
《诗学》中关于实践（praxis）的说法（这让乔伊斯暂停思
考），强加给我的是拉康的定义：“一种成为人的一部分的行
动[. . .]，使他有措施（en mésure）处于一个通过象征来处
理现实的位置。”正是在“喀耳刻”中，当布鲁姆进入妓院被
斯蒂芬转身看到时我们感知到了这个“措施”（mésure）的问
题。仿佛是巧合一般，这种对“措施”（mésure）的唤起，是
对《启示录》的引用。毫无疑问，我应该在这里停下，以
免我的讲话变得过于默示化（apocalyptic）。

I thank Jacques Aubert for having got his feet wet.



- 246 -


拉康：非常感谢雅克·奥伯特所做的工作。

The author of Surface and Symbol, whose name I gave you
last time, labels Joyce's art one of inconceivably private jokes.
In the same text there appears a word I had to look up in the
dictionary, eftsoon. I don't know if this word is a common one.
You don't know it? It doesn't ring a bell? It refers to things that
are put off till later, after soon. There's nothing but that in Joyce.
Not only are the effects put off till later, but more often than not
they throw you off track.

我上次告诉过你们他的名字，《表面与象征》（Surface
and Symbol）的作者，给乔伊斯的艺术贴上了一个“不可思
议的圈内笑话”（inconceivably private jokes）的标签。在同
一篇文章中，出现了一个我不得不在字典中查找的词，
eftsoon。我不知道这是否是一个常见的词。你们不知道它吗？
没有一点印象吗？它指的是的事情被推迟到以后、不久以
后。这就是它的意思，但在乔伊斯那里。不仅是指事情的
发生被推迟，而更多的时候是指使你们迷失方向。




- 247 -

Well, that's the art of Jacques Aubert too. He got you to
follow one of his threads with bated breath. All of that obviously
gives some grounding to what I'm trying to give consistency to
in the knot.

嗯，这也是雅克·奥伯特的艺术。他让你们屏气凝神地
跟随他的思路。所有这些显然给了我在结中试图赋予一致
性的东西一定的基础。

I realized that I was already referencing Joyce's slipping
and sliding in my Seminar Encore, which rather stunned me. I
asked Jacques Aubert whether this wasn't what led him to invite
me to speak about Joyce, but he replied that the Seminar hadn't
yet been published at the time. So, it can't have been this that
incited him to present me with this hole into which I shan't be
venturing, no doubt out of some prudence, prudence such as he
has just defined it.

我意识到，我在《研讨班二十:Encore》中已经提到了
乔伊斯的滑行和滑动，这让我相当惊愕。我问雅克·奥伯特，
这是不是他邀请我谈论乔伊斯的原因，但他回答说当时这
期研讨班还没有出版。所以，不可能是这个原因促使他向



- 248 -

我提出这个我不会进入其中探险的洞穴，无疑是出于某种
谨慎，刚刚他所定义的谨慎。

The hole of the knot poses me no less of a question.

结上的空洞使我产生了更多的问题。

One is able to ascertain the duplicity of the Borromean knot
which actually is not a knot but a link - I mean, to ascertain that
there are two of them, only when the circles, the rings of string,
are coloured. I am indebted to Soury and Thomé for mentioning
that, which I surely did notice myself, of course.

人们能够发现波罗米结的欺骗性，它实际上不是一个
结，而是一个链——我的意思是，只有当圆圈、线环被染
上颜色时，才可以发现有两个结。感谢Soury 和Thomé 提
到了这一点，当然，我自己肯定也注意到了这一点。

When the coloured quality distinguishes any one of these
rings from the two others, we can, with the aid of this daubing,
make it so that there are two knots. If the knots are not coloured,
if nothing sets the rings apart, then nor will there be anything to



- 249 -

set one knot apart from the other. Both statements will be
equivalent.

当色彩的特性将这些环中的任何一个与其他两个区分
开来时，我们就可以借助于这一上色的过程，而得出有两
个结的结论。如果这些结没有颜色，如果没有任何东西将
这些环区分开来，那么也不会有任何东西可以将一个结与
另一个结区分开来。这两种说法将是等同的。

You will tell me that in the planar diagram there is one that
is levorotary and another that is dextrorotary. Here, however,
lies the whole crux of this calling into question of the planar
diagram. The planar diagram implies a specified viewpoint, and
doubtless it is no accident that the notion of left and right cannot
be translated into the symbolic in any way whatsoever.

你会告诉我，在平面图中，一个是左旋的，一个是右
旋的。然而，这正是对平面图有所质疑的关键之处。平面
图意味着一个特定的视角，毫无疑问，左和右的概念无论
如何都无法转化入象征之中，这并非偶然。

For the knot, this only starts to ex-sist upwards of the three-



- 250 -

component relation. How can it be that the three-component
relation enjoys this privilege? Precisely here lies that with which
I should like to endeavour to solve the question. There must be
something here, which cannot be unrelated to the way that
Jacques Aubert singled out for us just now the function of
phonation, precisely in what is at issue in supporting the
signifier.

对于结来说，只在超越三元（three-component）关系之
上才得以存在。三元关系为何会享有这种特权呢？这正是
我想努力解决的问题。这里一定有什么东西，而它一定与
雅克·奥伯特刚才为我们特别指出的发音的功能有关，而这
正是在其对能指的支撑方面。

The keen point, on which I shall remain, is that of knowing
where significance qua written starts to be distinct from mere
effects of phonation. Phonation is what transmits the proper
function of the name, and it is from the proper name that we
shall be setting off again, so I hope, next time we meet.

我接下来将关注的重点是，我想知道，就书面形式的
意指而言，其究竟是如何开始区别于单纯的发音效果的。



- 251 -

正是发音传达了名字其恰当的功能（the proper function of
the name），所以我希望我们下次见面时，将从的恰当的名
字（专名）（the proper name, propre noun）再次开始。

1976 年1 月20 日





- 252 -

V
WAS JOYCE MAD?
乔伊斯疯了吗？
The jouissance of the real
Redemption or castration
The real within the entanglements of the true
Compensating for a de facto Verwerfung
The value of the proper noun

实在的原乐
救赎或阉割
在真理纠缠之中的实在
补偿事实上的除权
专名的价值

It is not going well, and I’ll tell you why, I spend my time
trying to soak up the enormous literature - for all Joyce's
loathing of this term, it’s nevertheless what he brought about,
and willingly so - the enormous blather around his work.

事情进展并不顺利，我会告诉你们原因，我花费时间



- 253 -

试图吸收（soak up）大量的文学作品——虽然乔伊斯厌恶
这个术语，不过这正是他所带来的东西，而且他自愿如此
——围绕着他的作品喋喋不休。

How does this come about? Jacques Aubert, who is down
there in the front row, from time to time sends me from Lyons a
list of supplementary authors to read. He is not innocent in this
matter (but who is innocent?), having himself produced things
on Joyce. Why, then, am I engaged in this work of soaking-up?
Because I began it, for sure. But I am trying, as one does for all
reflection, to ask myself why I began.

这是怎样产生的？雅克·奥伯特，他现在坐在这里的
第一排，而且在平时会时不时从里昂发来一份追加的作者
名单，供我来阅读。在这件事情上他不是无辜的（但谁是
无辜的呢？）他基于乔伊斯的作品创作了一些东西。那么，
为什么我要投身于这个工作呢？当然，因为我是开启了这
项工作。但是我始终在尝试，正如一个人进行反思所做的
——问我自己我为什么开始（这项工作）。

1




- 254 -

The question is worth asking - at what point is one mad?
Was Joyce mad? Not being able to give an answer today does
not prevent me from beginning to orient myself according to the
distinction I have proposed to you, between the true and the real.
In Freud - it is clear this is how he organized things - the true is
what gives pleasure, and this is just what distinguishes it from
the real, which does not necessarily give pleasure.

这个问题是值得问的——什么时候一个人疯了？乔伊
斯疯了吗？今天，无法给出答案并不妨碍我根据向你们提
出的真理与实在的区别来定位自己。在弗洛伊德那里——
这些都是很明确的——真理是给人快乐的东西，这就是它
区别于实在的地方，实在并不一定会给予快乐。

This is obviously a point where I am twisting something in
Freud. I make the observation that enjoyment [jouissance] is the
real. This leads me into tremendous difficulties. Why? Firstly,
because jouissance which is real comprises masochism.
Masochism is the major form of enjoyment given by the real.
Freud discovered this, he did not immediately expect it. Once
you have entered it, you are dragged into this route.




- 255 -

显然我正在扭曲弗洛伊德的某些理论。我观察到原乐
[jouissance]就是实在。这将我引向了巨大的困难。为什么？
首先，因为作为实在的原乐构成了受虐狂。受虐狂是实在
给予的享乐的主要形式。弗洛伊德发现了这点，但他并没
有立刻预料到。一旦你踏足于此，你就被拖进了这条通路。

For my part, I began by writing Ecrits inspirés, which is
why I should not be too astonished to find myself confronting
Joyce, and this is why I dare to ask the question - was Joyce
mad? What was it that inspired his writings?

对于我来说，我开始于进行“灵感写作”（Ecrits
inspirés），这就是为什么我不太惊异于发现自己正与乔伊
斯对峙，并且这也是我敢于问出这个问题的原因——乔伊
斯疯了吗？是什么启发了他的写作？

Joyce left an enormous quantity of notes, scribblings,
scribbledehobble - this is the title given to one of Joyce's
manuscripts, published by a certain Connolly, whom I knew
once (is he still alive?).

乔伊斯留下了大量的笔记，涂鸦，《scribbledehobble》



- 256 -

——这是乔伊斯一份手稿的标题，由一位名叫康诺利的人
出版，我曾经认识他（他现在还活着吗？）

It is no accident that he left his notes and drafts in this state,
scribbled, he had to watch over them, and even encouraged
those known as researchers to look through them. He wrote a
vast number of letters. There are three great thick volumes.
Amongst these letters, there are some verging on the
unpublishable - which as you know, does not in the end stop
someone publishing them. The priceless Richard Ellmann has
brought out a last edition, Selected Letters, containing a certain
number of letters which had been thought unpublishable in the
first volume.

他把笔记和草稿留在这种状态，潦草的状态，他必须
保护它们，甚至鼓励那些被称为研究人员的人翻阅它们，
这绝非偶然。他写了大量的信件，有三本厚厚的书之多。
在这些信件之中，有些接近无法出版的程度——正如你们
所知，最终不会有人阻止出版它们。有趣的是，理查
德·埃尔曼出版的最新版的，《书信选》，包含了一部分，
曾在第一卷中被认为是不可出版的信。




- 257 -

I confess, I can't find my way round this jumble. I get hold
of a few little threads of course. I get a certain idea of his affair
with Nora, based on my practice, I mean on the confidences I
receive, since I have dealings with people for whom I arrange
things so that speaking the truth gives them pleasure. Everyone
says - well, everyone(!)- Freud says that if I can do this, it is
because they love me, and they love me thanks to what I have
tried to pinpoint as the transference, that is, that they think I
know. It is obvious that I don't know everything, and in
particular, I don't know, when I read Joyce - for that's what’s
frightful, I am reduced to having to read him! - what he believed
about himself. It is absolutely sure that I haven't analysed him -
and I regret it. But anyway, he was clearly not very disposed to
it. The names Tweedledum and Tweedledee, came naturally to
his pen to designate Freud and Jung respectively - which does
not show that he was inclined to analysis.

我承认，围绕着这些乱七八糟的的东西，我迷失了方
向。当然我把握到了些许小线索。根据我的实践经验，我
知道了关于乔伊斯与诺拉关系的事，我是说根据我收到的
小秘密，由于我与那些我为他们安排事情的人打交道，因
此说出真理使他们快乐。大家都这么说——是的，所有人



- 258 -

（！）——弗洛伊德说如果我可以这样做，是因为他们爱
我，并且他们爱我是因为我所试图指出的移情，那就是，
他们认为我知道。但很明显，我不知道任何事，尤其是当
我阅读乔伊斯时，我不知道——因为这才是最可怕的，我
沦落到不得不读他的书！——他对于自己的看法。毫无疑
问，我没有分析过他——对此我感到遗憾。但是无论如何，
他显然不太愿意接受分析。特威德·莱登（Tweedledum）
和特威德·莱迪（Tweedledee）的名字，自然而然地出现在
他的笔下，分别用来指代弗洛伊德和荣格——这并不表明
他倾向于精神分析。

Read, in A Portrait of the Artist as a Young Man, the chat
he reports he has with a certain Cranly, his friend. He does not
dare to state what it is he is entering Into, Cranly presses him,
harasses him, pesters him, to know if he will draw any
conclusion from the fact that he says he has lost his faith, the
faith in the teachings of the church which have been his
education. It is clear that he does not dare to free himself from
those teachings, because they are quite simply the armature of
his thought. He plainly will not take the step of affirming that he
no longer believes, he recoils from the cascade of consequences
which would follow the act of rejecting the whole huge



- 259 -

apparatus which remains his support. Cranly invokes him, urges
him to take the step, and Stephen does not take it.

阅读《一位青年艺术家的肖像》中他记述的，与某位
名为克兰利的朋友的一段谈话。他不敢说他正在步入什么，
克兰利逼迫他，侵扰他，纠缠他，想知道是否能从他说他
已经失去了他的信仰、对上帝教义的信仰这一事实中得出
任何结论，他受到的教育是对教义教会的信仰。很明显，
他不敢将自己从这些教导中释放出来，因为它们确实是他
思想的盔甲。他显然不会断言他不再信仰了，他对拒绝支
持整个庞大机构的行为所带来的一连串后果感到畏缩。克
兰利唤起了他，敦促他采取行动，但史蒂芬并没有。

The question is the following: he writes that, and what he
writes is the consequence of what he is - but to what point does
this go? How far did this go, whose tricks - silence, exile and
cunning - he gives, a means of navigation. I put the question to
Jacques Aubert - is there not some trace in his writings of his
making himself what he calls in his language a redeemer? Does
he go so far as to substitute himself for what he manifestly has
faith in - this redeemer, the true redeemer whom the priests tell
him about in their twaddle (which in my opinion is what it is).



- 260 -

Did he think he was the redeemer? I don't see why I shouldn't
ask Jacques Aubert, his feeling for these things is as good as
mine, since we're reduced to feeling [sentiment] - we are
because Joyce has not spoken to us, he has written, and when
one writes one can access the real, but never the true. Well,
Jacques Aubert?

这个问题如下：他写了那个，并且他所写的就是他所
是的结论——但这会发展到什么地步呢？他给出的，航行
的手段，他的把戏——沉默，流亡，诡诈——能走多远？
我向雅克·奥伯特提出了一个问题——在他的作品中，难
道没有任何迹象（trace）表明他使自己成为了他语言中的救
赎者吗？他是否甚至用自己替代了他所明白地信仰的东西
——这个救赎者，那些神父在他们的胡言乱语中（在我看
来就是这样）告诉他的，真正的救赎者呢？他认为他是救
赎者吗？我不明白为什么我不问问雅克·奥伯特，对于这
些事他的感觉和我一样好，因为我们都沦落到只能靠感觉
[sentiment]了——因为乔伊斯没有和我们交谈过，他写下了
这些，而当一个人写作时他可以抵达实在，但并不是真理。
奥伯特你怎么看？




- 261 -

Aubert-There are traces, yes.
Lacan - That's why I'm asking the question, because there
are traces.
Aubert - In Stephen Hero, for Instance, there are very clear
traces of that.
Lacan - The annoying thing is, it's never clear. In the
Portrait of the Artist, it is not the Redeemer, but God himself,
who is artificer, artist.

奥伯特：是的，有痕迹（traces）。（表明他使自己成
为了他语言中的救赎者）
拉康：这就是我为什么问这个问题，因为这里有踪迹
（traces）
奥伯特：例如，在《斯蒂芬英雄》里，有非常清晰的
那个的痕迹（traces）
拉康：令人恼怒的是，这痕迹从来不清晰。在《一位
青年艺术家的肖像》中，不是救赎者，而是上帝自己，是
一个创造者，艺术家。

Aubert - If I remember correctly, the passages in which he
describes the appeal of a false Christ, are equally those where he
speaks of enigma of manner. This seems to correspond to the



- 262 -

well-known period when he was fascinated by Franciscanism, in
its two aspects, one concerning the imitation of Christ (we are
all near to the Son, we imitate him) and the other poetry, the
Little Flowers. And one of the texts which he looks through in
Stephen Hero is not a theological, but a poetic text, by Jacopone
da Todi.

奥伯特：如果我没记错的话，他描述假基督的呼唤
（appeal）的段落，同样是他谈到方式（manner）之谜的段
落。这似乎与他迷恋方济各会的著名时期相一致，在两个
方面，一个是关于模仿基督（我们都接近于圣子，我们模
仿他）的，另一个是关于诗歌《小花》。他在《史蒂芬英
雄》中浏览的一个文本不是神学文本，而是雅各
布·达·托迪的诗歌文本。

Lacan - Yes, exactly. But how can the extent of his belief
be determined? What kind of physics should be used? This is
where I place my hope in my knots, with which I operate, for
lack of any other recourse. I didn't get there straight away, but
they provide me with things - things which tie things up for me,
I should certainly say.




- 263 -

拉康——是的，没错。但如何确定他的信仰程度呢？
应该使用哪种物理的方式？（What kind of physics should be
used?）这就是我把我的希望寄托在我的结上的地方，我用
它来进行操作，因为我缺乏任何其他的依靠。我并没有马
上到达那里（依靠），但它们（结）为我提供了一些东西
——我应该肯定地说，这些东西为我把它们联系起来。

2

Knots have a dynamics, which has no use [ne sert a rien],
but rather binds together [serre].What can this bind together?
Something one supposes to be stuck in these knots. Only, if one
thinks that these knots are all that is real, how can there be space
for something to be bound in? That there is, is certainly
supposed by the fact that I place a point there, and it is not
unthinkable to see the reduced notation of a cord, passing in
there and out the other side.

结有一个动力学机制，它没有别的用处［ne sert a rien
（无用）］，除了使得环结合在一起。（见下图）这能把
什么结合在一起（serre 束缚）？人们认为有些东西会陷入
其中。只是，如果一个人认为这些结全是实在的，怎么会



- 264 -

有空间让某些东西被束缚？那个被束缚的东西，是由我在
那里放置了一个点所假设的，这也并不是不可想象的，看
到一条线的简化符号，从另一边传入又传出。


Link with a dot

带点的链环

Topology is based on this: that at the very least, without
counting whatever else there is, there is the torus. My good
friend Soury and Thome have resolved the relations of the
Borromean knot and the torus.the infinite line, if made to pass
through here, makes the false hole into a true hole, in other
words something representable in a diagram.




- 265 -

拓扑学就是基于此：至少，在不考虑其他任何东西的
情况下，有一个圆环。我的好朋友苏里（Soury）和托米
（Thomé）已经解决了波罗米结和圆环的关系。一根无限长
的线，如果要穿过这里，就会把假洞变成真洞，换句话说，
某种可以在图中表示的东西。


The torus

圆环

The link set into a torus



- 266 -


链接中加入了圆环

The false hole transformed into a true hole by the addition of an
infinite straight line

通过加上一条无限长的直线，伪洞变成了真洞

In fact, the diagram still poses a question. In what way is it
appropriate? All we can say is that it is demanded by knots, as
an artifice of representation, which is only a perspective, since
we must supplement this supposed continuity at the moment
when the infinite line is thought to emerge from the hole. What
is the function of this hole? That of a ring. But a ring is not, like
the line of a circle, a pure abstraction. We must give a body to
the circle, in other words give it consistence, it must be
imagined as having some kind of physical support, for it to



- 267 -

become thinkable. Effectively, nothing thinks but the body.

事实上，这个图仍然提出了一个问题：它是以什么方
式得以合理地出现的？（In what way is it appropriate?）我们
可以说的是它是被结所要求的，作为一种透视的表现手法，
因为当这个无限的线被认为是从洞中冒出时，我们必须补
充这种假定的连续性。那这个洞的功能是什么？那个环。
但是一个环并不像圆的线（line of a circle）一样，是一个纯
粹的抽象。我们必须给圆一个身体，换句话说给它以一致
性，它必须被想象成有某种物理的支持，为了使它变得可
思考。实际上，除了身体，无物思考（nothing thinks but the
body, ne se pense [panse] que le corps）。

Let us return now to the path of Joyce.

现在让我们回到乔伊斯。

What is Joyce's relation to Nora? Oddly enough. I'd say it
is a sexual relation, even though I say there's no such thing. But
it's a funny kind of sexual relation.

乔伊斯和诺拉的关系是什么？奇怪的是，我会说这是



- 268 -

一种性关系，尽管我说过根本没有性关系。但这是一种有
趣的（奇怪的）（drôle）性关系。

Something one seldom thinks of is to turn the left-hand
glove inside out, and put it on the right hand. This can be found
lying around in Kant. But anyway, who reads Kant? It is very
pertinent in Kant, but there is one thing he didn't think of,
perhaps because in his day gloves didn't have buttons, which is
that once the glove is inside out, the button is on the inside.
Apart from this, the comparison would be completely satisfying.
Well then, Joyce's inside-out glove Is Nora - that is his way of
thinking that she fits him like a glove.

人们很少想到的一件事是把左手的手套翻过来，套在
右手上。这种事可以在康德那里找到。但无论如何，谁读
康德呢？这在康德的理解里是非常恰当的，但有一件事他
没有想到，也许是因为在他那个时代手套没有纽扣，那就
是一旦手套由内而外翻，纽扣就在里面了。除此之外，这
个比较将是完全令人满意的。那么，乔伊斯的由内而外
（inside-out）手套就是诺拉——这是他认为的，她契合着
他的方式，就像一个手套一样。




- 269 -

For Joyce, there is only one woman, she is always
modelled the same, and he only puts on his glove with the most
intense revulsion. It is palpably only with the greatest of
depreciations that he makes Nora into a chosen woman. Not
only must she fit him like a glove, but she must be tight as a
glove. She has absolutely no use. And when they are in Trieste it
even gets to a point where every time she drops a sprog -I have
to use these terms - there is a scene. It had no place in the
programme. Things between Jim (as he is called when one
writes about him in a matey way, because his wife wrote to him
under this name) and Nora start going badly as soon as there is a
kid. Each time, there is a scene.

对于乔伊斯来说，只有一个女人，她总是被塑造成同
样的样子，他只是带着最强烈的厌恶戴上手套。易于觉察
的是，只有经过最大程度的贬低，他才能使诺拉成为一个
被选中的女人。她不仅要像手套一样适合他，而且要像手
套一样紧缚着他。她完全没有用处。当他们在的里雅斯特
的时候，每次她生下一个婴儿时——我必须使用这些术语
——都会有一个镜头（scene）。它在程序（programme）中
没有位置。吉姆（当大家以友好的方式写关于他的文章时，
人们这么叫他，因为他的妻子用这个名字给他写信）和劳



- 270 -

拉的关系一有孩子就开始变得不好了。每一次，都有一个
镜头（scene）在此。

The button may well have something to do with the name
of an organ. The clitoris is certainly the dark point in this
business, metaphorical or not. This has, besides, some echoes in
the behaviour which is not observed enough of what is known as
a woman. It is very curious that a woman is so interested in,
precisely, dark points. The first thing she does to her boy is to
take the dark bits off him - it's a metaphoric expression of her
wish that her own dark point should not take up so much space.
It's always the button of the inside-out glove. There are women
who sometimes have to go in for de-lousing, like monkeys, but
one shouldn't muddle things up - squidging a bug and extracting
a dark point are not the same thing.

这个纽扣很可能与某个器官的名称有关。阴蒂当然是
这个事件的暗点（dark point），无论是否是隐喻。此外，
这与我们所知的女性行为中没有得到足够观察的部分有一
些呼应。很奇怪的是，一个女人恰好对暗点如此感兴趣。
她对儿子做的第一件事就是去掉他身上的黑暗部分（dark
bits）——这是她觉得自己的黑暗点不应占那么多空间的愿



- 271 -

望的隐喻表达。它总是内—外翻的手套的纽扣。有些女性
有时不得不像猴子一样去除虱，但人们不应该把事情搞乱
套——捕捉虫子和提取暗点并不是一回事。

3

Let's carry on with our journey. Imagining oneself to be the
redeemer, at any rate in our tradition, is the prototype of what I
write as père-version. It is to the extent to which there is a
relation of the son to the father, and since a long time ago, that
the barmy idea of the redeemer has emerged.

让我们继续我们的话题。想象自身是救赎者，至少在
我们的传统中，是我写的父亲—版本（père-version，音同
perversion）的原型。正是在某种程度上，儿子和父亲之间
存在着某种关系，而且从很久以前起，救赎者的愚蠢想法
就出现了。

Freud tried to free himself from this sadomasochism, the
only point where there is a supposed relation between sadism
and masochism - sadism is for the father, masochism for the son
- although these terms have strictly no relation between each



- 272 -

other. To imagine that sadomasochism is explained by a polarity,
one must really think an infinite line penetrates the torus, and
believe in active and passive. Freud clearly saw something
which is much more ancient than this Christian mythology,
castration. The phallus is passed on from father to son, and it
comprises something which nullifies the father's phallus, before
the son has the right to carry it. It is essentially this symbolic
transmission which is referred to in the idea of castration.

弗洛伊德试图将自己从这种施虐受虐中解放出来，这
是唯一一个在施虐和受虐之间存在假定关系的点——施虐
是对于父亲的，受虐是对于儿子的——尽管这些术语彼此
之间没有严格的关系。要想象施虐受虐是由两极化来解释，
我们必须真的认为有一条无限的线穿过圆环，并相信主动
（active）和被动（passive）。弗洛伊德清楚地看到了比这
个基督教神话更古老的东西，阉割。菲勒斯是由父亲传给
儿子的，在儿子有权携带菲勒斯之前，它包含了某些废止
父亲的菲勒斯的东西。在阉割的概念中，本质上就是这种
象征的传递。



- 273 -


This is what has led me to pose the question of the relations
between the symbolic and the real. These are highly ambiguous,
at least in Freud. And this is where the question of the critique
of the true is raised. What is the true, if not the true real? And
how can one distinguish - If not by using some metaphysical
term, Heidegger's Echt - the true from the false real? For Echt is
after all on the side of the real. There Heidegger's metaphysics
comes to a stop - in this little bit about Echt he confesses, if I
can put it thus, his defeat.

这就是为什么我提出了象征与实在之间关系的问题。
至少在弗洛伊德看来，这是高度模糊的。这就是对真理进
行批判的问题所在。如果真理（the true）不是实在的
（real），那么什么是真理（the true）？如果不使用海德格
尔的真（Echt）这个形而上学术语，我们如何区分真与假？
因为真（Echt）毕竟是站在实在一边的。在那里，海德格尔
的形而上学停止了——在这一点上，他承认了（如果我可



- 274 -

以这样说的话），他的失败。

The real is situated in the entanglements of the true, and
this is what led me to the idea of the knot; which results from
this - the true is interpenetrated, due to the fact that its use
creates meaning out of everything; and this because it slides, it
is sucked up by the image of the bodily hole which emits it,
namely the sucking mouth.

实在位于真理的纠缠之中，这就是使我产生结的想法
的原因；由此得出的结果是— — 真理是互相贯通的
（interpenetrated），基于这个事实，它的使用从一切中创
造了意义；这是因为它在滑动，它被身体的洞的想象（the
image）所吸入，这个洞就是吸吮的嘴。

There is a dynamics of the gaze which is centrifugal, that is,
which starts from the seeing eye, but also a dynamics of the
blind spot - it starts from the moment of seeing, and takes it as a
support. In fact, the eye sees instantaneously - this is what is
called intuition, which redoubles what is termed space in the
image. There is no real space. It is a purely verbal construction,
which has been spelt out in three dimensions according to the



- 275 -

so- called laws of geometry, which are those of the ball or the
balloon imagined in kinaesthetics, in other words oral-anally.

这里有一种凝视的动力学，它是离心的，也就是说，
它开始于在看的眼睛，但也是盲点的动力学——它从看的
时刻开始，并将其作为支持。事实上，眼睛的看是瞬间的
——这就是所谓的直观，它加倍了在想象中被称为空间的
东西。没有真正的空间。它是一种纯粹的语言结构，它是
根据所谓的几何定律在三维空间中拼写出来的，即那些在
动觉学（kinaesthetics）中被想象出的球或气球，换句话说，
口腔-肛门（oral-anally）。

The object I have termed a is in fact one and the same
object. I have given it back the name object because it is ob, an
obstacle to the expansion of the concentric, that is, engulfing,
imaginary. Conceivable means can be grasped in the hand - it is
the notion of Begriff - like a weapon. And, to invoke some
Germans who weren't stupid, this weapon, far from being an
extension of the arm, is from the beginning a weapon to be
thrown. We didn't wait for bullets to throw the boomerang.

我称之为a 的客体实际上是同一个客体。我把它的名字



- 276 -

重新叫回object，因为它是“反”（ob），一个阻碍同心圆
扩展的障碍（obstacle），它正在，吞没，想象。可信的方
法能被把握在手中——就是Begriff（德语，意思是概念，
出自动词greifen，意思是抓取，把握）的概念——就像一
种武器。并且，用一些不愚蠢的德国人的话来说，这种武
器，根本不是手臂的延伸，而从一开始就是一种可以投掷
的武器。我们并不指望射出的子弹像扔出的回旋镖一样飞
回来。

What appears from all this is that, in sum, all that subsists
in the sexual relation is the geometry to which what we said
about the glove made reference.

从这一切中可以看出，总而言之，存在于性关系中的
一切都是我们在手套中提及的几何学。

This is all that the human species has left to support this
relation, and is why it is from the beginning involved in the
business of inflating [soufflure, lit. 'blowing up' (e.g. balloons)],
in which it has made the solid more or less fit. Nevertheless, we
should differentiate between the outline of this solid and the
solid itself. Now, what has the most consistence in this inflated,



- 277 -

concentric sphere, is the cord - the cord which makes a circle,
which turns round into a loop, unique in that it is part of the
diagram.

这就是人类为了支持这种关系所剩下的全部，也是它
从一开始就参与膨胀原因[气泡。“鼓起来”（例如气球）]，
在其中它使物体（solid）大概适合它。然而，我们应该区分
这个物体的轮廓和物体本身。现在，在这个膨胀的同心球
体中，最具一致性的是线——线形成一个圆，然后变成一
个环，独特之处在于它是图表的一部分。

What proves, after all, that the spiral is not more real than
the ring? In which case nothing indicates that to join itself up, it
has to make a knot, if it is not the falsely named Borromean knot,
that is, a chain-knot [chainoeud] which naturally produces the
trefoil knot,which derives from the splices of the noeud bo.

毕竟，是什么证明了螺旋并不比环更真实？在这种情
况下，没有任何迹象表明，要将自身连接起来，它必须打
一个结，如果它不是被错误命名为波罗米结，也就是说，
一个链结[chainoeud]，它自然会产生三叶结，它来自波罗米
结（the noeud bo）的拼接。



- 278 -



From the Borromean link to the trefoil knot

从波罗米安链到三叶草结

What is no less striking is that when it is reversed like this
(reference to fig 5), it does not produce a trefoil knot.

最引人注目的是，当它像这样倒转时，它不会产生三
叶结。


A false trefoil knot



- 279 -


假三叶草结

It was immediately shrewdly observed that if you change
something here in the passage underneath on this side of the
knot, the entire knot is undone.

可以马上注意到的是，如果你在结的这一边这样操作，
整个结就被解开了。


The unknot as a ring, then folded into a figure of eight and a
false trefoil

解结为环状，然后折成八字形和假三叶形

The question I raise at the end of this little chat, is about
whether or not Joyce was mad. Why should he not have been?
It's not a privilege. In most people, the symbolic, the imaginary



- 280 -

and the real are entangled to the point where they are continuous,
one to another, if it is true that there is no procedure to
distinguish them in the so-called Borromean knot -for the
Borromean knot is not a knot, it is a chain. Each of its loops is in
continuity with the next in a non-differentiated way, and at the
same time this is not a privilege only available to the mad.

在这次交谈结束之际，我提出的问题是关于乔伊斯是
否疯了。而是为什么他不应该如此呢？这不是一个特权
（privilege）。在大多数人身上，象征、想象和实在被纠缠
在一起，它们彼此连续着，如果在所谓的波罗米结中并没
有手段来区分它们——因为波罗米结不是一个结，而是一
条链。它的每一个环都会以无差别的方式与下一个环保持
连续，同时这并不是只有疯狂才能获得的特权。

This, purely and simply a ring when folded produces this 8.
We can provide a remedy for this schema by adding a loop to it,
thanks to which the trefoil knot will not come unravelled. Does
the case of Joyce not amount to a way of making up for this
unknotting? Is Joyce's desire to be an artist who would occupy
everyone, or anyway as many people as possible, not an exact
compensation for the fact that his father had never been a father



- 281 -

for him?

这个，一个简单的环，当它被折叠时，产生了这个8。
我们可以通过添加一个环来补救这个图示，多亏了它三叶
结才不会被解开。乔伊斯的案例难道不算是一种阻止结被
解开的方式吗？乔伊斯想成为一名能够吸引所有人，或者
说吸引尽可能多人的艺术家，这难道不是对他父亲从未是
他的父亲这一事实的确切补偿吗？




Buckle repairing the false trefoil knot




- 282 -

用圆扣修复的假三叶草结35

Not only did the latter teach him nothing, but he neglected
just about everything, except for falling back on the good Jesuit
fathers, the diplomatic church - the word diplomatic is from the
text of Stephen Hero, but in the Portrait of the Artist too, the
father speaks of the church as a very good institution, and the
word diplomatic Is likewise foregrounded.

后者不仅没有教会他什么，而且他几乎忽略了一切，
除了依靠耶稣会神父，外交教会（the diplomatic church）—
—外交一词来自史蒂芬·赫罗的文本，但在《一位青年艺术
家的肖像中》，父亲也谈到教会是一个非常好的机构，外
交一词同样是被强调的。

Was It not in compensation for this paternal abdication, this
Verwerfung ,in fact, that Joyce felt himself imperiously called-
this the very word, resulting from a mass of things in his text -
to valorise his proper name at the father's expense? It was this
name which he wished to be paid homage,the homage he had
himself refused to pay to anyone. The proper name makes every

35 L7a1 (Rolfsen 标号726)



- 283 -

effort here to be greater than the master-signifier.

难道不是为了补偿父亲的缺位，事实上，这个除权
（Verwerfung），乔伊斯觉得他自己被专横地称为的这个词
——这正是这个词，源自他文本中的大量内容——以牺牲
父亲为代价来维护他的专名（the proper name，propre nom）
36吗？他希望别人向他致敬的就是这个名字，他自己也拒绝
向任何人致敬。专名在这里竭尽全力试图超越主人能指
（S1）而走向知识（S2）。

A fiction which has been spread forever through history is
that this subject had two names which were proper to him. That
Joyce was called James too, is only followed on by the use of
the nickname Dedalus. That in this way they can be heaped up
in piles leads to only one conclusion - bringing the proper name
back to the status of the common noun [nom commun].

历史上流传至今的一个虚构故事是，对他来说有两个
专有名字。乔伊斯也被称为詹姆斯（James），只是后来使
用了绰号迪达勒斯（Dedalus）。通过这种方式，它们可以

36 Propre 在法语里面有自己的，固有的、专有的意思，nom 在法语里面既有名
字的含义，也有名词的含义。



- 284 -

被成堆地堆积起来，指向唯一的结论——将专名恢复到普
通名词[nom commun]的状态。

That's where I've got to at this point. You must have had
your fill [votre claque] even your Jaclaque, since I would also
add an han! Which would express my relief at having got
through this lot today. Thus, I reduce my proper name to the
commonest noun.

这就是我今天想说的。你们一定得到了你的收获[votre
claque（你们的掌声）]甚至是你的«Jacq’ Lac»（意思是“受
够了雅克·拉康”），既然如此，我也会补充一个《han!》
（Lahan 拉好！）来表达我对于结束今天这么多内容的宽慰。
以此，我将我的专名简化为了最普通的名字

 1976 年2 月10 日





- 285 -

VI
JOYCE AND PAROLES IMPOSEES
乔伊斯和强迫的言说
Lacan's knot
Knots and links
Slips of the knot mended by the sinthome
Sexual equivalence = non-relation
Symptomatic woman, ravaging man
Sinthome is a woman

拉康的结
结与链接
被圣状修补的结的滑动
性别对等=非—关系
症状的女人，破坏的男人
圣状是一个女人

I had put some hope - don't think this is some coquetterie or
titillation - in the fact that it was the holidays. Lots of people go
away. At least in my clientele, this is striking. But not here.




- 286 -

我曾把希望——不要以为这是一些卖弄或挑逗——寄
托在假期上。因为在假期就会有很多人走了。至少在我的
支持者中很明显有不少人走了。但是在这里却不是这样

I see the doors are still bursting as ever, despite my hopes
for a smaller audience, in return for which I was hoping to
become confidential, to speak in a slightly more intimate manner.
All the same, it would be nice if I could get some response,
some collaboration, some interest.

我看到门口还是一如既往地爆满，尽管我希望能有更
多的听众，作为回报，我希望能亲近些，以一种稍微亲密
的方式说话。同样，如果我能够得到一些回应，一些合作，
一些兴趣，那就更好了。

But it seems to me difficult to take an interest in what is
becoming a (re)search [recherche]. I mean that I am beginning
to do what the word recherche implies - to turn round and round.

但在我看来，要对某种正在成为一种研究[recherche]的
东西感兴趣是很困难的。我的意思是，我开始做recherche37

37 Recherche 在法语里面有研究，寻找，搜索的意思（译注）。



- 287 -

这个词所包含的意思——转来转去。

1

At one time, when I was a bit strident, I used to say, like
Picasso, I don't search, I find. Nowadays I have more trouble
clearing my path.

从前，当我有点直愣的（strident）时候，我常说，像
毕加索一样，我不探寻，我发现（I don't search, I find）。如
今，我在开辟自己的道路上遇到了更多的麻烦。

The Borromean knot is no such thing; it is contradicted by
its name, which like all names reflects a meaning: its meaning
allows the location of meaning somewhere within the
Borromean chain.

波罗米结不是这样的东西；它与它的名字相矛盾。它
像所有的名字一样反映了一种意义：它的意义允许在波罗
米链的某个地方找到意义。

If we term this element of the chain the imaginary, this the



- 288 -

real and that one the symbolic, meaning will be there (reference
to fig.1). We can have no hope of placing it anywhere else,
because every thought we have is imagined, in the end.

如果我们把链条上的这个要素称为想象，这个称为实
在，那个称为象征，意义就会在那里（见下图）。我们不能
希望把它放在其他地方，因为我们的每一个想法最终都是
想象出来的。

Only we don't think without words, contrary to what has
been advanced by certain psychologists, those of the school of
Wurzburg.

只是我们不能不借助词语（word，mot）思考，这与某
些心理学家，即伍兹堡学派的人所提出的观点相反。

Last time, I remarked that a single fault anywhere in the



- 289 -

triple knot was enough to reduce it to a simple ring. This docs
not happen automatically. For instance, take the quintuple knot.

上一次，我说过，在三重结中任何地方搞错都足以使
它变成一个简单的环（见下图）。这并不是自动发生的。例
如，以五重结为例。

Since there is a well-known knot with four crossings called
Listing's knot, I had the madcap idea of calling this knot with
five crossings Lacan's knot.

由于有一个著名的四联结被称为Listing's knot，我突发
奇想地给这个五重结起了名字：拉康结。




- 290 -


Listing's knot                                    Lacan's knot

  Listing 结                                           拉康结

So, with the knot of five crossing points, if you make a
mistake at either one of these two points labelled 4 and 5, the
same thing occurs as in the trefoil knot, namely, the whole thing
comes undone, and clearly that forms nothing more than a ring.
If on the other hand you make a mistake at any one of these
points here, 1, 2 or 3, you can observe that it still holds together
as a knot, that is to say, it becomes a trefoil knot.

因此，对于由五个交叉点组成的结，如果你在标记为4
和5 的这两个点中的任何一个点上犯了错误，就会出现与三
重结相同的情况，也就是说，整个结都会被解开，很明显，
这只会形成一个环。另一方面，如果你在这里的任何一个



- 291 -

点（1、2 或3）犯了错误，你可以观察到它仍然是一个结，
也就是说，它变成了一个三重结。

This is to tell you that it's not self-evident that, by making a
mistake at one point of the knot, the whole knot evaporates, if I
can put it like that.

这是为了告诉你，在结的某一点犯错误，整个结就会
消失，这并不是不言而喻的。

What I said last time is the following - I alluded to the fact
that the symptom, which this year I've been calling the sinthome,
is what enables the Borromean link to be mended, should we no
longer be making it a link, namely, should we have made what I
have been calling a mistake at two different points.

我上一次所说的是：我暗示了这样一个事实，即今年
我称之为圣状的症状，是使波罗米链得以修复的原因，如
果我们不再把它作为一个链接（link），也就是说，如果我
们在两个不同的点上犯了我所称的错误。



- 292 -



The Borromean sinthome

波罗米圣状

Should the symbolic thereby come free, as I once noted it
would, we have a way of mending it, which is to fashion what I
defined for the first time as a sinthome，This is the item that
enables the symbolic, the imaginary, and the real to go on



- 293 -

holding together, even though here none of them are actually
holding on to any of the others anymore due to the two mistakes.

如果象征环因此被解开，我们只有一种方法去修补，
那就是通过我所第一次定义的圣状，它使象征、想象和实
在能够结合在一起；尽管它们两个错误，它们中没有一个
能与另一个相结合。

What I proposed very gently last time was that Joyce has a
symptom whose origin is this: that his father was lacking,
radically lacking - he speaks of nothing but this. I centred things
around the proper name, and in my opinion it was by wanting to
make a name for himself that Joyce compensated for the lacking
father.

我上次非常温和地提出，乔伊斯有一个症状，其起源
是这样的：他的父亲是在匮乏中的，彻底地匮乏中的——
他一直所说的只是这个。我把事情集中在专名（nom propre）
上，在我看来，乔伊斯正是通过想要为自己扬名立万，来
弥补这个匮乏的父亲。

I said this, because I was unable to say better, and I will try



- 294 -

to articulate it in a clearer fashion. At any rate, Joyce's art is so
particular that the term sinthome is very fitting for it.

我这样说，是因为我无法说得更好，我将努力以更清
晰的方式来阐述它。无论如何，乔伊斯的艺术是如此特别，
以至于用圣状这个词来形容它非常合适。

2

It happens that last Friday, at my presentation of what is
generally known as a 'case', I examined a case [of madness,
certainly) which began with the sinthome paroles imposées
['imposed speech/words'].

碰巧上周五，在我介绍一般所谓的“案例”时，我检
查了一个[当然是疯狂的]案例，发现了一种圣状《paroles
imposees》（强迫的言说）。

This, at least, was the articulation given by the patient
himself, and it seems to me the most sensible kind of
articulation I could describe as Lacanian. How is it that we do
not all feel that the words on which we are dependant are, in a



- 295 -

sense, imposed on us?

这至少是病人自己给出的表述，而且在我看来，这是
最拉康式的表述。我们怎么会完全不觉得我们所依赖的文
字在某种意义上是强加给我们的呢？

It's true that a so-called 'sick' man sometimes sees more
than what we call a normal person. Language [la parole] is a
parasite; it is a veneer; it is the form of cancer which afflicts the
human being. Why does the so-called normal man not notice
this? There are some who go as far as feeling it, and Joyce gives
us a taste of it.

一个所谓的“病”人有时确实比我们所说的正常人看
到得更多。言说[la parole]是一种寄生虫；它是一种外衣；
它是困扰人类的癌症。为什么所谓的正常人没有注意到这
一点？有一些人感受到了这一点，而乔伊斯让我们体会到
了它。

Last time, I didn't mention his daughter Lucia (he gave his
children Italian names), with the aim of not getting into
storytelling. Well, she's still alive - in England, in a hospital,



- 296 -

because she is what is usually termed a schizophrenic.

上次，我没有提到他的女儿露西娅（他给孩子起了意
大利名字），目的是为了不涉及到讲故事。嗯，她还活着—
—在英国，在一家医院，因为她是通常所说的精神分裂症
患者。

The case I was presenting had suffered a deterioration.
Having had the experience, which for my part I think sensible,
of words being imposed on him, he also had the feeling that he
was affected by what he called telepathy - by which he meant,
not that he was aware of what others were thinking, but that
everybody else was aware of his thoughts, and in particular his
reflections concerning the above-mentioned imposed words.

我所介绍的案例已经出现了恶化。在经历了被强加给
他的言说之后，他有一种感觉，即他受到了他所谓的心灵
感应（telepathy）的影响——他的意思是，不是他知道别人
在想什么，但其他人都知道他的想法，特别是知道他对上
述强加的言说的想法。

For instance, he heard sale assassinat politique ['dirty



- 297 -

political assassination'] which he made the equivalent of sale
assistanat politique ['dirty political assistantship'].

例如，他听到了sale assassinat politique［‘肮脏的政治
暗杀’］，他将其等同于sale assistanat politique［‘肮脏的政治
援助’（政治交易）］。

The signifier can be clearly seen reduced here to what it is -
equivocation, a twisting of speech. But in response to sale
assistanat or sale assassinat, he said something to himself which
started with a 'but...', and which were his thoughts on this
subject.

在这里可以清楚地看到能指被简化为它所是——模棱
两可，言说的扭曲。但在对sale assistanat 或sale assassinat
反应时，他对自己说了一些以“但是……”开头的话，这
是他对这个问题的想法。

And what filled him with panic was the thought that any
reflection he made in addition to what he thought of as imposed
words, was known by everybody else.




- 298 -

而让他充满恐慌的是，除了他认为是强加的言说之外，
他的任何想法都已经被其他人知道了。

He was, then, as he put it, a telepathic emitter, in other
words he no longer had any secrets, and this was what had led
him to attempt to end it all, which was another reason for him
being there, and for my having to be concerned with him.

那么，正如他所说，他是一个心灵感应发射器，换句
话说，他不再有任何秘密，这就是导致他尝试自杀的原因，
这是他在那里的另一个原因，也是我必须关注他的原因。

My reason for speaking to you today about Joyce's
daughter Lucia is that Joyce, who fiercely defended the daughter,
labelled schizophrenic, against the control of the doctors, had
only one thing to say about her: my daughter is telepathic.

我今天向你们谈及乔伊斯女儿露西娅的原因是，乔伊
斯激烈地捍卫这个被贴上精神分裂症标签的女儿，反对医
生的控制，只有一句话：我的女儿有心灵感应。

In the letters he writes about this, he states that she is far



- 299 -

more intelligent than everyone else, that she informs him in a
way that is miraculous - this is the word he implies - about
everything that happens to a certain number of people, and that
these people have no secrets from her. It's very striking.

在他写的有关信件中，他说她比其他人聪明得多，她
以一种神奇的方式告知他——这是他的言下之意——关于
发生在一定数量的人身上的一切，而且这些人而言对她没
有秘密。这是非常引人注目的。

Not that I think that Lucia was in reality telepathic; but
Joyce attributes this quality to her on the basis of certain signs,
declarations which he understands in a special way.

并不是说我认为露西娅实际上有心灵感应；但乔伊斯
认为她有这种能力，是因为他以特殊方式去理解的某些迹
象、声明。

To defend his daughter, he attributes to her something
which is an extension of what I will call, for the moment, his
own symptom - namely that something was imposed on him at
the locus of speech [à l'endroit de la parole].



- 300 -


为了保卫他的女儿，他把一些东西归于她，这是我暂
时称之为他自己的症状的延伸——即一些东西是在言说的
地方[à l'endroit de la parole]强加给他的。

In fact, in the continuing progress of his art - namely, that
speech [parole] which comes to be written, to be broken,
dislocated, so that in the end to read him seems an encounter
with a continuing progress, from his first efforts in the critical
essays, then in the Portrait of the Artist and in Ulysses,
concluding with Finnegans Wake - it is hard not to see that a
certain relation to language [la parole] is increasingly imposed
on him, to the point where he ends up breaking or dissolving
language itself, by decomposing it, going beyond phonetic
identity.

事实上，在他的艺术的持续创作中——即那个被写出
来的言说[parole]，被打破，被错位，所以最后阅读他的作
品似乎就像是遇到了一个持续的进展，从他第一次在批评
文章中，然后在《一个艺术家的肖像》和《尤利西斯》中，
最后是在《芬尼根守灵夜》中——很难不看到某种与言说
的关系[la parole]越来越强加在他身上，以至于他最终打破



- 301 -

或消解了语言本身，通过分解它，超越了语音同一性。

Doubtless there is a reflection here concerning writing. It is
through the intermediary of writing that language [la parole]
breaks up at the moment of imposing itself as such, in a
deformation which is always ambiguous - is it a matter of
breaking free from the verbal parasite [parasite parolier], or,
rather, of being invaded by its phonernic qualities, by the
polyphony of language [la parole]?

无疑，这里有一个关于写作的思考。正是通过写作的
中介，言说[la parole]在把自己强加于人的那一刻被打破了，
这种变形（deformation）总是模棱两可的——这是一个摆脱
言说的寄生虫[parasite parolier]的问题，或者说，被它的语
音的质、言说[la parole]的多音性侵入的问题？

At all events, the fact that Joyce declares Lucia to be
telepathic seems to me, because of this patient whose case I
considered, certainly indicative of something Joyce bears
witness to at the same moment: the absence [carence] of the
father.




- 302 -

无论如何，在我看来，乔伊斯宣布露西娅有心灵感应
的事实，出于我所考虑的这个病人的情况，肯定表明了乔
伊斯在同一时刻见证的东西：父亲的缺席[carence]。

My supposition is that what I am supporting with the
sinthome - which is shown here as a loop of string - is produced
at the very place where an error occurs in the knot's layout. (fig5)

我的假设是，我所支持的圣状——这里显示为一个绳
圈——正是在结中出现错误的地方产生的。


The mistake rectified at the site where it occurs

错误在发生处得到了纠正38


38 L7al



- 303 -

3

Now, the slip [lapsus] is certainly the foundation, in part, of
the notion of the unconscious. The joke is to be put under the
same heading- it is not unthinkable that it derives from a slip.
This is at least how Freud himself constructs it, as a short-circuit,
an economy in relation to pleasure, to satisfaction.

现在，滑动（slip）（即口误，失误[lapsus]）肯定在某
种程度上是无意识概念的基础。笑话也是如此，它是由口
误引起的。这至少是弗洛伊德自己构建它的方式，把它当
做一种短路，一种与快乐和满足有关的经济学（économie）。

The fact that this should lie at the site where the knot fails,
where there is a kind of slip of the knot itself, is just what it
takes to hold our attention.

事实上，这应该发生在结失败的地方，那里有一种结
本身的滑动（结在哪里失败（rater），哪里就有结的失误
（lapsus））这正是我们需要注意的地方。

A knot is something which fails. Likewise, it is due to the



- 304 -

consistence of the unconscious that there are heaps of failures.
But is error [la faute], which is made into sin by the conscience,
of the order of a lapsus?

结就是失败的东西。同样，由于无意识的一致性，也
有成堆的失败。但是，错误[la faute]，也就是被良心造就的
罪恶，是否属于失误（lapsus）的范畴？

The word's ambiguity allows the passage from one
meaning to the other. This original sin which Joyce makes so
much of - does it contain something of the lapsus?

这个言说的模糊性允许从一个意思到另一个意思的转
变。乔伊斯大谈特谈的这种原罪——它是否包含了失误的
东西？

This is to conjure up a whole imbroglio. But we are caught
up in it, in the knot, and by the same token in an entanglement
[embrouille]. The lapsus occurs at a single point; but its
consequences can be seen at two other points. There is thus an
ambiguity concerning the way to correct it.




- 305 -

这勾勒出一个完整的难题。但我们被卷入其中，被打
上了结，同样也被卷入了一个纠缠[embrouille]。失误发生
在一个点上；但在另外两个点上结果却不想相同。因此，
在它的方法上存在着模糊不清的地方。

What is left of the triple knot's basic structure differs,
according to whether the sinthome is positioned where the
lapsus occurs, or at the other two points in the knot.

三重结的基本结构的剩余部分有所不同，这取决于圣
状是被位于失误发生的位置，还是位于结中的其他两点。

What is extraordinary is that things have something in
common in the way they form knots, which is shown here by a
certain direction or orientation - let's say by the fact that
compensation turns to the right. But it remains that the result of
compensation with the sinthome is different from what happens
here and there (...).




- 306 -

非同寻常的是，事物在形成结的方式上有一些共同点，
这在这里表现为某种方向或方位——比方说，补偿向右转
的事实。但仍然是，用圣状补偿的结果与这里和那里的情
况不同（...）。

The red 8 with the green ring is strictly equivalent to its
inverse. Take one, and you will easily get the other form. There
is thus a strict equivalence. Now, after the path I've opened
about the sexual relation, it is not hard to suggest that when
there is equivalence, there is no relation.

带绿环的红色8 与它的倒转严格等价（equivalent）。取
其一，你将很容易得到另一种形式。因此有一个严格的等
价关系。现在，在我开辟了关于性关系的道路之后，不难
看出，当存在等价关系时，就没有关系了。
If we uphold the present equivalence - the fact that in both
sexes there has been a failure, a failure of the knot - the result is
that the two sexes are equivalent.



- 307 -


如果我们坚持目前的等价性（equivalence），即在两种
性别中都有一个失败的事实，一个失败的结——结果是两
种性别是等价的（对等的）。

Nevertheless, if the error is put right at the place where it
occurred, the two sexes (here symbolised by two colours) are no
longer equivalent. For if here you see what corresponds to what
I have just termed equivalence, what then corresponds is this ,
which is far from being equivalent.

然而，如果在发生错误的地方把它纠正过来，两种性



- 308 -

别（这里用两种颜色来象征）就不再是等价的了。因为如
果你在这里看到了与我刚才所说的与等价性相对应的东西，
那么与之相对应的就是这幅图，但它远远不是等价的。

If one colour here can be replaced by the other there - there
can be no equivalence. The green cord will never be able to
cross the outer band of the red double 8.

如果这里的一种颜色可以被那里的另一种颜色所取代
——就不可能有等同性。绿线将永远无法穿过红色双8 的外
带。




- 309 -

At the level of the sinthome there is thus no equivalence in
the relations between green and red; there is no sexual
equivalence - in other words there is a relation. Effectively, if we
say that non-relation is a function of equivalence, it is to the
extent that there is no equivalence that the relation is structured.

因此，在圣状层面上，绿色和红色之间的关系没有等
价性；没有性的等价性（不存在性别对等）——换句话说，
有一种关系。实际上，如果我们说非—关系是等价的功能，
那么，正是在非—等价之上，关系才被结构起来。

There is no relation except where there is sinthome. It is the
sinthome which supports the other sex. I would go so far as to
say that the sinthome is the sex which I don't belong to, that is, a
woman.

除非有圣状，否则就没有关系。正是圣状支撑着另一
种性别。我甚至可以说，圣状就是我不属于的那个性别，
也就是一个女人（une femme）。

A woman is a sinthome for every man. Another name must
be found for whatever man is for a woman, as the sinthome is



- 310 -

characterised by non-equivalence. Man is anything you like for
a woman, an affliction worse than a sinthome, a devastation
even.

一个女人对每个男人来说都是一个圣状。无论男人对
女人来说是什么，都必须找到另一个名字，因为圣状具有
非—等价的特点。男人对女人来说是任何你喜欢的东西，
是一种比圣状更糟糕的苦恼，甚至是一种破坏
（devastation）。

That there should be no equivalence is the only basis for
what is known as the sexual relation in the human speaking-
being [parlêtre]. Is this not what we see demonstrated by what is
termed clinical work, that is, in bed? When we see human
beings in bed, and not only in hospital beds, it is there, anyway,
that we can get an idea about this oft-mentioned relation.

不应该有等价性，这是所谓的人类言说的存在[parlêtre]
中性关系的唯一基础。这难道不正是我们在所谓的临床工
作，也就是在病上看到的情况吗？当我们看到人类在床上，
而不仅仅是在医院的床上，无论如何，正是在那里，我们
可以对这种经常提到的关系有一个概念。



- 311 -


Everything I hear on another bed, the famous couch where
people tell me all about it, shows that there is a slender link, to
be defined, between the sinthome and the real of the
unconscious - if the unconscious is indeed real.

我在另一张床上听到的一切，也就是人们告诉我一切
的那张著名的沙发，表明在圣状和无意识的实在之间存在
着一种细微的联系，这种联系有待定义——如果无意识确
实是实在的。

How can we know if the unconscious is real or imaginary?
It takes part in an ambiguity between the two. But thanks to
Freud, we are involved in it, and this on the basis of sinthome.
Henceforth, we have to do with the sinthome in the sexual
relation, which Freud held to be natural (which is meaningless).

我们怎么能知道无意识是实在的还是想象的？它包含
二者之间的模糊性。但由于弗洛伊德，我们被卷入其中，
而且是在圣状的基础上。因此，我们必须与性关系中的圣
状打交道，而弗洛伊德认为这种性关系是自然的（也就是
说这毫无意义）。



- 312 -


It is on this point that I shall leave you today, since I also
have to mark in some way my disappointment at not having
found you thinner on the ground this time.

这就是今天我要说的，但我还是感到失望，因为这次
还是来了这么多人。

1976 年2 月17 日





- 313 -

VII
ON A FALLACE THAT VOUCHES FOR THE REAL
关于支撑实在的诡计（fallace）
The all and the set
Borromean duality
Orientation and colour
Syntax and equivoque
The phallus and the real

整全与集合
波罗米的二元对立
定向与颜色
句法与双关
菲勒斯与实在

Here I am, reduced to having to improvise. Not that I
haven't been working since last time, and profusely so, but as I
wasn't necessarily expecting to speak because in principle this is
a day of strike, here I am, then, reduced to setting out what I did
prepare a little after all, and even a great deal.
As usual, I was hoping there might be fewer of you.
I'm going to begin by showing you something that is not



- 314 -

necessarily what you were anticipating, but which is not
unrelated, something that I went to fetch before leaving and
which I wanted very much to think about because I had
promised as much to the person who is more than a little
concerned in this.

大家好，我必须得即兴讲一段。不是说我上次讲完之
后休息到现在，尽管还真是这样；但是我根本没想要作报
告，因为原则上今天是罢工日。现在我沦落到这地步，必
须得展示我多少还是准备了些东西的。
照常提一下，我其实希望少来点人。
我开始要讲的不一定是你们期待的东西，不过也不是
完全无关。这是我在离开之前取到的，我也非常想要思考
这个问题，因为我向稍微有些关心这个问题的人承诺过。

I would like to let you know, or to remind you, for those
who already know, that there is someone of whom I'm very fond,
whose name is Helene Cixous. It turns out that she had already
produced a brief note on Dora in the sold out issue of Litterature
in which I produced Lituraterre .. Since then, she has turned it
into a play, Portrait de Dora, which is being performed right
now at the Petit Orsay. It's not bad, in my opinion. I told Helene



- 315 -

what I thought about it - I've been on first-name terms with her
since I first met her - and I told her I would speak about it.

我想要让你们知道，或者说提醒一下那些已经知道的
人，有个我非常感兴趣的家伙叫海伦·希克索斯（Hélène
Cixous）。就是说，她已经在她的出版书Littérature 中简要
地分析过朵拉（Dora ）的案例，我在其中创作了
Lituraterre39。之后，她把这个案例改编成了一出戏剧，叫
《朵拉的肖像》，现在正在Petit Orsay 上演。我觉得那部戏
不赖。我告诉海伦我对此所做的思考——我初见她之后就
一直直呼她的名字——而且我告诉她我会在讨论班上谈到
这些的。

It's about Freud's Dora. This is precisely why I suspect that
it might interest a few of you to go and see how it's been put
together. It's been put together in a real fashion. I mean that
reality, the reality of the rehearsals, for example, is ultimately
what has dominated the actors. I don't know to what extent you
will appreciate it, but it's quite certain that there's something
quite striking in it.


39 译注：拉康在第十八期研讨班的口述文学。



- 316 -

我们说的就是弗洛伊德的那个朵拉。这正是为什么我
猜你们有人会感兴趣、去看看这些是如何缝合起来的。这
部剧实在是用相当时尚的方法把这些融合在了一起。我是
说真实性，比如说彩排的真实性，是对演员的最终极的支
配。我不知道你们会多喜欢它，但是这部剧里确实有极为
震撼的东西。

It's about hysteria, Dora's hysteria more precisely, and it so
happens that she's not the best hysteric in the cast. The woman
who is the best hysteric plays another role, but on no account
does she make a show of her hysteric's virtues. The woman who
plays the role of Dora doesn't make a bad show of it. At the very
least this is my sense of it.

这部剧是关于歇斯底里的，准确地说是朵拉的歇斯底
里的故事。颇为巧合的是，朵拉的演员并不是团队中最歇
斯底里的人。最歇斯底里的那个女演员演的是其他角色，
但是她绝不会做秀展示她的歇斯底里；而于此同时朵拉的
演员也把（朵拉的）歇斯底里演得不赖。至少我觉得是如
此的。

There is also someone in it who plays the role of Freud.



- 317 -

Naturally, he's in a right pickle. He looks rather bothered and
you can hear it in his delivery. Well, he goes at it with great
caution. It's all the less fortunate, for him at least, that he is not
an actor. He stepped forward for the role. So, he's constantly
afraid of overdoing Freud. In the end, the best thing I can tell
you to do is to go and see it. What you will see bears the mark of
the caution of the actor who plays Freud.

剧中也有人演弗洛伊德。他自然是处在一堆烂摊子里。
你能在演员的演说风格中听到，他似乎感到相当苦恼。嗯，
但他讲这些台词的时候还是相当警觉、注意的。稍微有些
不幸的是，他并不是一个演员。他主动承担了这个角色。
所以，他一直很害怕过度演绎了弗洛伊德。总之，我的建
议是你们最好去看看这部剧。你们看到的表演将让你们在
脑海中深深地印刻着这位演弗洛伊德的演员的形象

At the end of the day, the overall result of this is something
that is altogether curious. We are faced with a hysteria - I think
this will strike you, but then, perhaps you will appreciate it in a
different way - that I could call incomplete. I mean that hysteria
has always been in twos, since Freud anyway. Here we can see it
reduced in some way to what I might call a material state, and



- 318 -

this is why this is going to sit rather well with what I'm about to
explain to you. The play lacks the element that was added some
time ago - before Freud, when all is said and done - namely, the
way that hysteria has to be understood. This produces something
very striking and very instructive. It's a kind of rigid hysteria.
You'll be seeing this presently because I'm going to show you
what the word rigidity means here.
I won't speak any further about Portrait de Dora. I hope to
have some echoes about it from the people who come to see me,
for instance. Such things happen.

今天结束时，我们最终将得到绝对令人好奇的一个结
论。我们现在面临着一种歇斯底里——我想这会让你们震
惊，但同时你们会在另一层面上喜欢它的——我把它叫做"
不完整"（incomplete）。我是说，自弗洛伊德以来，歇斯底
里总是成对出现的。在此我们能看见它以某种方式降级到
我称之为物质状态的形式，这正与我将阐述给你们的东西
相符合。这部戏缺少了一些过去曾被添加进去的东西——
在弗洛伊德之前，当一切都被说出、被完成之前，（它缺少
的就是）歇斯底里被理解的方式。这生产了非常震撼的、
有教育性的东西。某种僵硬的歇斯底里。你们将在此理解
这一现象，因为我接下来将解释我刚刚所说的"僵硬"。



- 319 -

我不会再讲关于《朵拉的肖像》的东西了。不过我希
望一些来看我的人能给我一些反馈之类的。这类事情发生
过。

1

I'm going to speak to you about a link that I happen to have
offered to your attention, the Borromean link. It's no accident
that it gets called a knot, because it does slide towards the knot.
I'm going to show you this right away, but what you will see
first of all, in my first drawing, is a kind of embedding of
rigidity. It's not out of the question that the word link might
representify this for you, so to speak, because a link is, after all,
rigid.

接下来我要和你们说说我之前向你们强调过的波罗米
链环（Borromean link）。它被称为扭结是毫不意外的，因为
这个链环确实会滑向扭结。我即将展示这一过程，但是在
我接下来画的第一张图中，你们首先将看到的是一种"僵硬"
的嵌入。毫无疑问"链环"一词就能将这僵硬感展现给你们，
毕竟链环就是僵硬的。




- 320 -

The snag is, the link at issue here can be conceived of only
as something that is highly supple. It is even important to
consider it to be something that is altogether supple. I'm going to
show you this as well.
I was led to articulate this link, indeed to limn it, by
conjoining to it the symbolic, the imaginary, and the real. What
is important here is the real. After having spoken at great length
about the symbolic and the imaginary, I was led to ask myself
what the real might be in this conjunction.

障碍在于，我们这里所谈论的链环能仅被设想为某种
极度柔软的东西。把它当作是完全柔软的东西是相当重要
的，这我也会加以说明。
为了描述并勾画这一链环，我会把它联系到象征、想
象、实在。这里实在是最重要的。经历了漫长的对象征和
想象的描述过后，我最终自问：实在在这一联结
（conjunction）中可以是什么？

Of course, the real cannot be just one of those rings of

You must nevertheless have cottoned on a bit to what it is

that I have been trying to sustain the Borromean link with.
Here's what this yields. I wasn't minded to complete the drawing,

你们必然已经多少理解了我用以维持波罗米链环的东

Typical Borromean link

典型波罗米链环(VIII-1)

The fact that here I'm tricolouring the trefoil knot is a

You have seen how this can transform at the drop of a hat

Two-ring 'Olympic' link

Borromean link simulating an armillary sphere

模拟浑天仪的波罗米链环(VIII-5)

The advantage to the way I've presented the Borromean
link is that it simulates a sphere, as I once mentioned to Dali in
conversation, I no longer recall when.
There is of course a difference between the Borromean link
and what is always drawn in an armillary sphere when one tries
to circularize the sphere at three levels, which one may call
respectively transverse, sagittal, and horizontal. 2 You've never
seen an armillary sphere represented in this fashion.

这个我用来表示波罗米链环的方法的好处在于它模拟

波罗米链环和浑天仪球图里化的这个东西当然是有区



- 326 -

别的，如果你尝试把这个球在三个方向——可能叫做横截

点。你们是不可能在现实的浑天仪球上看到三个圆周这样

Since the false sphere that I've drawn here is supported by
circles, there is a way of handling it that consists in flipping it
over.
One cannot help but conceive of a sphere as being linked to
the idea of the all. The fact that one quite readily represents a
sphere by means of a circle links the idea of the all - which is
actually supported only by the sphere - to the circle. But this is a
mistake, because the idea of the all implies closure, whereas if
one is able to flip this all, the inside becomes the outside.

我画的这一“错误的”球面是由几个圆周支撑起来的，而
我们有法子把它翻转过来。43
人们总是不禁把球面想成是与“整全”的观念相联系的。

41 译注：根据英译版的注，可能的顺序是蓝绿红；当然其实并不影响。
42 译注：现实的浑天仪三个方向的圆环架子是内外分明可以分离的，不会像波

43 译注：这个方法大概是，正面看过去红色好像是“挺外面的”，但是你从右

了红绿蓝三个圆周的位置。



- 327 -

其实，人们很容易用几个圆周来表示球面的这一事实就把

但这是错误的，因为整全的观念暗示着封闭，而如果有人
能把这一“整全”翻转，那么内侧就变成了外侧。44

Once we are using circles to support the Borromean link it
becomes invertible, by virtue of the fact that the circle is in no
respect what people believe it to be - something that symbolizes
the idea of the all. Indeed, in a circle there is a hole.
It is only to the extent that beings are inert, that is to say
supported by a body, that one is able to say to someone, as was
done at the initiative of Popillius - I have drawn a ring around
thee, and thou shalt not step beyond it until thou pledgest me
this.

我们一旦使用了圆周来支撑波罗米链环，这一过程便
是可逆的，因为圆周完全不是人们所认为的那样象征着“整
全”的观念。事实上，圆周内部有一个洞。
仅仅在于存在是惰性的、由肉体支撑的这一程度上，
一个人才能够对别人说出像Popillius45的倡议那样的话——

44 译注：球面没法在不和自己相交的前提下连续变化至内外翻转的，直觉上很
好理解也有严格数学证明。
45 译注：似乎是Publius Popillius Laenus，公元前2 世纪中期罗马共和国政治家。



- 328 -

“吾划圈围住汝，汝不得跨越半步，除非汝向吾抵押某物。”

In sum, here we meet again, with respect to what I have
been calling by the name of the woman, the reason I asserted
that she is not all. This means that women merely form a set.
Indeed, over time, we have reached the point that the idea
of the all has been dissociated from the idea of a set. We have
come to think that a certain number of objects can be supported
by small letters, such that the idea of the all has been dissociated
therefrom. In other words, the circle that is supposed to group
together the objects a, b, c, and so forth, in a representation that
is altogether fragile, is exterior to them.

总之，这里我们再度遇见了，关于我之前所称的la
femme（“女人”）的，我断言她是“非全”的理由。这意味着
女人们组成仅仅一个集合。
确实，随着时间的推移，我们已经明白，“整全”的观念
已然从集合的观念中分离出来了。我们开始以为若干客体
是可以被一串字符所支撑的，这样“整全”的观念就已由此分
离出来了。换言之，下图这个被设想为可以把对象a, b, c 等
等聚集在一起而成为一个脆弱的表象的圆，却是外在于这

译者猜测后面这句话是他（反）农地改革说的话。



- 329 -

些对象的。


The set {a, b, c}

集合{a, b, c}(VIII-6)

Specifying that the woman is not-all implies a dissymmetry
between an object that one could call capital A - and we need to
find out what it is - and a set with one element. If there is a pair,
the two of them are united by being contained within a circle
that, by virtue of this fact, turns out to be distinct from them.
This is usually expressed by using braces, and is written thus -
{A, {B} }. This says that within a set there is, on the one hand,
an element, and on the other, a set with one single element.

具体说明女人是“非全”意味着一个对象（比如叫大

写字母A（大他者））——我们需要理解这是什么——和一

集合{A, {B}}(VIII-7)

2

I must, now, confess something.
Soury and Thome had spelt out to me how a three-
component Borromean link shows that it supports two different
objects on the condition that the three rings that constitute it be

coloured and oriented. These two conditions are required, and I

In a second phase, I found myself in the disagreeable
position of having imagined that simply colouring them would

现在我必须承认一些事项。
苏里和托米清楚地向我说明过，如果三个环是像我们

我认为这两个条件（指颜色和方向）是必要的。
 在接下来的阶段中我发现，想象着仅靠染色就足够区分
这两个对象的自己，多少有点讨嫌。这是因为我对他们给
我带来的结论的认同的方式多少有点肤浅了。

Indeed, should we assign the colour red to one of the three

This looks to be quite palpable. However, if we flip the sphere
over, it is the same object. Thus, we will obtain with the greatest

of ease a reverse layout.
Good heavens, I'll quickly draw this up for you. [Diagrams

我的天，我会很快地把这个过程画给你们看的。

These things are not all that easy to demonstrate. What is

的方式很正确地展现给了我。我也努力让你们看到这一变

究竟是什么在这一直接性中——或者说一种显然性

There is in some sense an idea of degeneration in

there is a decline in the monstration. All the waffle that
emanates from self-evidence merely brings about the emptying

out, on the condition that this is done in a signifying fashion.

More geometrico, which has long stood as the ideal support

This is altogether apt to remind us that geometrically speaking, a

“更多的几何语言”（More geometrico）长久地作为展
示（démonstration）的理想的支持，依赖于形式上的自明性

It's quite certain that I'm still exceedingly struck by my
mistake, which I quite rightly qualifed i as stupidity. It affected

无疑我仍被我的错误过度地打击了，而我非常恰当地

To demonstrate that there are two different Borromean

dextrorotary                                       levorotary

Then, we come to the fallowing fact. Orienting the ring,
which we are saying doesn't need to be in one particular colour,
evidently already amounts to isolating it. Not to say that it is of

different. Nevertheless, it is not inconsequential to say that the

于是，我们得到了如下的事实。给圆环定向，而这圆
环不需要染上什么特定的颜色，就已经很显然能将它区分

管如此，说它们三个环/线都要被给予定向并非是无意义的。

In the first of these two Borromean diagrams with an
oriented ring, the orientation of the ring that is here in red is,
from where we see it, dextrorotary. One should not assume that
an orientation is something that is maintained across all cases.
This is easy to prove. Specifically, by inverting the red ring -
and inverting it implies that the two infinite straight lines are



- 343 -

invertible as well - there will be an orientation that is exactly the
opposite.

在图（VIII-12）的两个波罗米链环中，左侧那个的红
色圆环的定向在我们看来是右旋的（顺时针的）。你们不应
当把定向假定成一个在所有情况下保持不变的东西。这很
好证明；具体地，如果你把整张图（前后）翻转过来（包
括红色圆环和蓝、绿直线），（红色圆环的）定向就会改变。

I said that it is enough for just one to be oriented. This is all
the more conceivable when you make these two lines infinite.
On the basis of what would we assign an orientation to them?
