存在于这个球体。但是，似乎没有人充分考虑到引入绳的
后果，线证明了袋子是不闭合的，除非它被绳捆住，并且
在每个球体中我们必须设想某种东西，当然是在球体的每



- 444 -

个点上以及它的结点处，把它变成了一个可以进去，用绳
给它打结的东西。

People write their childhood memories. This has
consequences: it is the passage from one writing to another. I
will speak to you in a moment about Joyce's childhood
memories, because I clearly have to show how this so-called
logic of sack and cord is something which can help us to
understand how Joyce functioned as a writer.

人们书写他们的童年记忆。它的结果是：它是从一个
书写到另一个书写的通道（passage）。稍后我将向大家介绍
乔伊斯的童年记忆，因为我必须清楚地展示所谓的“袋子和
绳索的逻辑”，它可以帮助我们理解乔伊斯怎样作为一个作
家的。

2

Psychoanalysis is something different. Psychoanalysis goes
through a certain number of utterances. No-one says that
psychoanalysis puts one on the path to writing. This is exactly
what I'm trying to set before you with my language: that it's



- 445 -

worth looking long and hard when someone turns up asking, in
the name of some inhibition, to be put in the position of writing.
For my part, I think carefully when I'm asked that (it happens to
me as to everyone), because it's not at all clear that it can be
achieved with psychoanalysis. This calls, properly speaking, for
an investigation into what it means to write. And what I am
going to suggest to you today concerns, very precisely, Joyce. It
came to me all at once, as a whole [dans la boule] - a boule
which here is far from being spherical as it is attached to
everything we know - that with Joyce something happened - in a
way which I think I can account for - so that what is generally
called the ego played a quite different role to the simple role it
plays for the everyday mortal. With him, the ego fulfilled a
function which I can only account for with my mode of writing.

精神分析是另一回事。精神分析涉及一定量的话语。
没有人说精神分析会让人走上写作的道路。这正是我试图
用我的语言向你们展示的：当有人以某种抑制（inhibition）
的名义要求将语言置于写作的位置时，值得花很长时间和
精力去寻找。就我而言，当我被问及这一点时，我会仔细
思考（这对我和每个人来说都是如此），因为根本不清楚它
是否可以通过精神分析来实现。恰当地说，这需要对写作



- 446 -

的意义进行详查。而我今天要向你们讲的，非常准确地说，
关于乔伊斯。我突然意识到，作为一个整体（dans la boule）
——一个球体，在这里远不是球形（spherical）的，因为它
隶属于我们所知道的一切——在乔伊斯身上发生了一些事
情——以一种我认为我可以解释的方式——因此，通常所
说的自我，扮演了一个与它在日常普通人身上所扮演的简
单角色完全不同的角色。在他身上，自我实现了一种功能，
我只能以我的写作模式来解释。

What put me on the track of this is worth indicating. It is
that writing is absolutely essential to his ego, which he
illustrated when [...] someone went to see him and asked him
something about a picture, the reproduction of a view of the
town Cork. Joyce, who knew how to catch people out, answered
that it was Cork; to which the chap said, ‘Yes, I know, it's
obviously the main square in Cork, I recognize It - but what is
that around it?’ To which Joyce replied, 'cork'. This is given as
an illustration of the fact that In Joyce, in what he writes, there's
always more - you only have to read the little schema of Ulysses
he gave to Stuart Gilbert (and a rather different one he gave to
Linati and Valéry Larbaud). Every single thing he brings
together, everything he narrates to make up the work of art that



- 447 -

is Ulysses, is in a relation of homonymy, at least, with the way it
is framed. That each chapter of Ulysses is given as support a
certain mode of framing, termed for instance 'dialectical',
'rhetorical', theological', is for him linked to the very materiality
of what he narrates. And, of course, this does not fail to suggest
my little rings, which are also the support of a certain framing.

是什么让我走上这条小径的倒是值得一提。写作对他
的自我来说是绝对本质性的（absolutely essential），当有人
去见他，问他关于一幅画的事情时，他就说明了这一点，
这幅画是科克（Cork）城风景的复制品。乔伊斯知道怎样
抓住人们，他回答说那是Cork；这家伙说，“是的，我知道，
这显然是科克城的主要广场，我认识它——但它周围是什
么？”乔伊斯回答说：‘Cork’（框住照片的软木）。这说明了
一个事实，对于乔伊斯，在他写的东西里，总是有更多的
东西——你只需要阅读他给斯图亚特·吉尔伯特的《尤利西
斯》的小纲要就能明白这点（一个相当不同于他给利纳蒂
和瓦莱里·拉波特的版本）。他把每一件事都汇集在一起，他
叙述的所有事构成了《尤利西斯》这部艺术作品，至少从
它的框架来看，处在一种同名（谐音）的关系（relation of
homonymy）中。《尤利西斯》的每一章都被赋予了某种框
架模式的支持，例如被称为“辩证的”、“修辞的”、“神学的”



- 448 -

所支持，对他来说，这与他所叙述的内容的实质性有关。
当然，这并非意谓着我的环就不是某种框架的支撑了。

In question is the following: the consequences of an error
[faute] not entirely caused by chance. For psychoanalysis
teaches us that an error is never the result of chance, that behind
every slip there Is a signifying finality - in other words that the
error aims, if there is an unconscious, to express something; not
simply something that the subject knows, because the subject is
situated in the division, the relation of one signifier to another,
that is the life of language, which is something completely
different to what Is simply called life, for what signifies death
for the somatic subject has its place in drives, which have to do
with what I have just called the 'life of language'.

问题是：一个错误[faute]的结果不完全是偶然造成的。
因为精神分析告诉我们，错误绝不是偶然的结果，每一次
失误背后都有一个象征性的目的论（signifying finality）—
—换言之，如果有无意识，错误的目的是表达某种东西；
不仅仅是主体知道的东西，因为主体处在分裂中，在一个
能指对另一个能指间的关系之中，这就是语言的生命，这
与简单的生命完全不同，因为对于躯体的主体来说，意谓



- 449 -

死亡的东西在驱力中有它的位置，这与我刚才所说的“语言
的生命”（life of language）60有关。

The drives in question have to do with the relation to the
body, and the relation to the body is not In anyone a simple
relation. Not only does the body have holes, this is even, so says
Freud, what should have put mankind on the track of these
abstract holes which concern the utterance of anything at all.
The something, briefly, which is suggested by this reference, is
what one should try to extricate from an essentially muddled
idea, that of eternity. It is an idea which is connected to nothing
but thought time, philia [φιλία], which I mentioned Just now.
One thinks, and sometimes one even talks wildly, about an
eternal love. One has truly no idea what one says! Is it the other
life, as it were, that is understood by that? You see how
everything gets Involved, and where this idea of eternity, which
nobody knows the meaning of, leads you！

所讨论的驱力与身体的联系（relation）有关，而这个
与身体的联系对任何人都不是一个简单的联系。不仅身体

60 在这个术语的第二次提及中，拉康删除了vie 中的阴性冠词，这就加强了与
vide de langage（vide 带有阳性冠词）“语言的空无”同音的可能。



- 450 -

有洞，甚至如弗洛伊德所说，是它使人类走上了这些抽象
的洞的轨迹，这些洞根本上涉及到对任何事物的言说。简
单地说，这篇文章提出的东西，是一个人应该努力从一个
本质上混乱的观念中解脱出来的东西，那就是关于永恒
（éternité）。这是一个除了思考时间之外不关乎任何事的观
念，philia [φιλία]（友情），我刚刚提到过。一个人会思考，
有时甚至会疯狂地谈论永恒的爱。他真的不知道自己在说
什么！可以说，这能被理解为来生吗？你看，一切都是如
何被牵扯进来的，没有人知道永恒的意义，以及会它会将
你引向何方！

3

As for Joyce, I'd like to read you something here - but in
fact you know it is available, you can read it in French, A
Portrait of the Artist as a Young Man.' There Joyce tells us this:
for some reason - connected with Tennyson, Byron, some poet
anyway - his friends tie him up against a fence and give him a
beating. The friend leading the gang was called Heron - not an
indifferent term, this erôn [ἐρῶν] - who beats him, helped by the
others; and afterwards Joyce wonders why, now the thing is over,
he has nothing against him. Joyce expresses himself -as should



- 451 -

be expected from him - very aptly: I mean that he metaphorizes
nothing less than his relation to his body. He observes that the
whole affair has emptied out; he expresses this by saying that it's
like a fruit being peeled. What does this tell us? It indicates
something which is so imperfect for all human beings, the
relation to the body - who is there who knows what goes on in
his body? This is clearly something extraordinarily suggestive
and it is even, for some people, what gives meaning to the
unconscious. But if there is one thing that I have carefully
articulated from the beginning, it is precisely that the
unconscious has nothing to do with the fact of one's ignorance
about many things concerning one's own body, and that what
one knows is of a quite different nature. One knows things to do
with the signifier. The ancient notion of the unconscious as
Unbekannte was precisely something supported by our
ignorance of what went on in our bodies; but the Freudian
unconscious - this is something worth stressing here - is exactly
what I have said, namely the relation that exists between a body
which is foreign to us and something which is a circle, or rather
a straight line (they are equivalent), which is the unconscious.

至于乔伊斯，我想在这里给你们读点东西——但事实



- 452 -

上，你知道它是有用的，你可以用法语读一读，《一位青年
艺术家的肖像》。在那里，乔伊斯向我们讲述：出于某种原
因——与丁尼生、拜伦或其他诗人有关——他的朋友们把
他绑在篱笆上，揍了他一顿。领头的朋友叫希荣（Heron）
——不是一个冷淡的术语，而是erôn [ἐρῶν]（希腊语，爱
人的意思）——他在别人的起哄里打了他一顿；后来乔伊
斯想知道为什么，事情结束后，他却对希荣没有什么怨言。
乔伊斯非常恰当地表达了自己——正如他所期望的那样：
我的意思是，他完全隐喻了他与自己身体的关系。他观察
到，整个事件都清空了；他表示这就像一个水果脱掉果皮
一样。这告诉我们什么？它暗示了某些对所有人来说都是
如此不完美的东西，与身体的关系——谁知道自己身体里
发生了什么呢？这显然是一件非常具有启发性的事情，对
一些人来说，这甚至是赋予无意识意义的东西。但是，如
果有一件事我从一开始就仔细阐述过的话，那正是无意识
与一个人对自己身体的许多事情的无知这一事实无关，而
一个人所知道的是一种与之完全不同的性质。一个人所知
的与能指有关。无意识作为unbekannet（未知）的古老概念，
正是由我们对身体中所发生的事情的无知所支持的；但是
弗洛伊德的无意识——这是一个值得强调的东西——正是
我所说的，即存在于一个异在于我的身体与一个圆环形
（circle）的东西之间的关系，或者说是与一条直线（它们



- 453 -

是等价的）之间的关系，这就是无意识。

What meaning can we give to that which Joyce bears
witness to, in other words not simply the relation to his body but,
as it were, the psychology of that relation (for, after all,
psychology is nothing but that - the confused image we have of
our own body). But this confused image is not without a
component - let us give it its name - of affects; in other words, if
we imagine this psychical relation, there is something in the
psyche which is affected, which reacts, which is not detached -
as Joyce testifies, having been beaten up by his 4 or 5 friends,
there is something which simply slips away, like the skin of a
fruit. This is striking, that there should be people who have no
affect when subjected to bodily violence. There is something,
moreover, which is ambiguous about it: it gave him, perhaps,
some pleasure; masochism is in no way outside the possibilities
of sexual stimulation for Joyce, as he emphasized sufficiently in
the case of Bloom. But I would say that what is more striking Is
the metaphor he uses, the detachment of something like the skin
of a fruit. This shows that he did not experience jouissance, but
rather - this is something which is valid psychologically - he had
a reaction of disgust, and this disgust relates to his own body. It



- 454 -

is like someone who expels a bad memory, puts it in parenthesis.
This is absolutely left as a possibility in the relation to his own
body as foreign [étranger]. And this is just what is expressed by
the use of the verb 'to have': one 'has' a body, one 'is' not it in
any way; and this is what leads to belief in the soul -there's no
reason to stop there, so one ends up thinking one has a soul,
which is the last straw.

我们能给乔伊斯所见证的东西赋予什么意义呢？换句
话说，不仅仅是与他的身体的关系，而是那种关系的心理
学（因为，毕竟，心理学只不过是那种——我们对自己身
体形象的混乱想象）。但是，这种混乱的形象并非没有某种
成分——让我们称之为——情感；换言之，如果我们想象
这种心理关系，在心灵中有某种东西受到影响，产生反应，
而不是独立于其之外的——正如乔伊斯证明的那样，在被
他的四五个朋友殴打之后，有某种东西只是溜走了，就像
水果脱皮一样。若是有人在遭受身体暴力时没有任何影响，
那会是令人震惊的。此外，还有一点是模棱两可的：它也
许给了他一些快乐；不排除受虐狂是乔伊斯性刺激来源的
可能性，正如他在布鲁姆（《尤利西斯》主人公姓氏）中充
分强调的那样。但我要说的是，更引人注目的是他使用的
隐喻，某物就像水果脱皮一样的分离。这表明他并没有经



- 455 -

历原乐（jouissance)，而是——这在心理上是合理的——他
有一种厌恶的反应，这种厌恶与他自己的身体有关。就像
有人驱逐了一段糟糕的记忆，把它放在括号里。这绝对是
一种可能性，自己的身体对他来说是陌生的[étranger]。这就
是动词“to have”的用法：一个人“有”（has）一个身体，而
无论如何一个人都不“是”（is）它；这就是导致人们相信灵
魂的原因——没有理由就此止步，所以人们最终会认为自
己有灵魂，这是最后的稻草。

This kind of 'dropping out ', the 'dropping out' of the
relation to the body, is highly suspicious to an analyst. This idea
of the self ,of the self as body, has something of significance.
This is what is termed the ego. If the ego is said to be
narcissistic, this is because something at a certain level supports
the body as image. But in the case of Joyce, is not the fact that
this image is not here engaged - is that not a sign that the ego
has a quite particular function on this occasion? How can that be
written in my nœud bo?

这种“放弃”，即“放弃”与身体的关系，对于分析师来说
是非常可疑的。这种自我（self）的观念，自我作为身体的
观念，具有某种意义。这就是所谓的自我（ego）。如果说自



- 456 -

我是自恋的，这是因为在某种程度上，某些东西支持想象
性的身体形象。但在乔伊斯的例子中，难道这不是一个事
实吗？即这个形象在这里并没有参与作用——这不是一种
迹象，表明自我在这个场合有一个相当特殊的功能吗？那
该怎样将这个写入我的波罗米结呢？

So now I'm going to trace, go through something which
you might not necessarily follow. How far does pere-version go,
so to speak (written, as you know, in my way)? The noeud bo
does just that: it sanctions the fact that Freud makes everything
depend on the function of the father. The knot is nothing but the
translation of the fact, which I was reminded of last night, that
love - and beyond the marketplace, love one could describe as
eternal - is what relates back to the function of the father,
addressing him in the name of this: the father is the bearer of
castration. At least, that is what Freud puts forward In Totem
and Taboo, in other words in referring to the primal horde: it is
because the sons are deprived of women that they love the father.
It is actually something quite unique, quite staggering, which
only Freud's intuition sanctions. But I am trying to give that
intuition another body, with my noeud bo which is so apt to
evoke Mount Nebo, or, as is said, the Law - the Law which has



- 457 -

absolutely nothing to do with the laws of the real world (which
are an entirely open question). The Law here is simply the law
of love, in other words perversion.

所以现在我要开始追根溯源，彻查一些你们不必跟上
的事情。可以说，转向—父亲（你们知道的，是以我的方
式书写）（Pere-version 音同perversion）发展到了怎样的地
步？波罗米结正是这样做的：它认可了弗洛伊德使一切都
依赖于父亲的功能这一事实。这个结只不过是一个事实的
翻译，我昨晚想起了这个事实，爱——在市场（marketplace）
之外，爱，人们可以称之为永恒的——是与父亲的功能相
关的，以这个名字称呼他：父亲是阉割的持有者。至少，
这是弗洛伊德在《图腾与禁忌》中所提出的，换句话说，
在提到原始部落时：正是因为儿子被剥夺了女人，他们才
爱父亲。事实上，这是一件非常独特、非常令人震惊的事
情，只有弗洛伊德的直觉才能认可这一点。但是我试图给
这种直觉另一个躯壳，用我的波罗米结，它很容易使人联
想到尼波山，或者，正如（乔伊斯）所说的，律法——法
律与现实世界的法律完全无关（这是一个完全开放的问题）。
这里的法则只是爱的法则，换句话说就是倒错（的法则）。

It is very curious that learning to write - to write my



- 458 -

Borromean knot, at any rate - has a function. And what I am
about to illustrate is this: imagine that there is somewhere, there
for instance [reference to mise à plat], a mistake, in other words
that the writing makes an error there; what will this lead to? You
will certainly not have imagined that the Borromean knot has, in
its own nature, an imaginary aspect; here, as you see, the ring ‘I’
there can simply take its leave. It slips away: exactly in the
manner of what Joyce feels after receiving his beating. It slips
away, the imaginary relation has no place. It has no place in this
instance, and this makes one wonder whether Joyce's intense
interest in perversion had perhaps a different signification.
Maybe, after all, the beating did disgust him; maybe he was not
a true pervert. Because it is quite a task to imagine the reason
why Joyce is so unreadable. If he is so unreadable, it is perhaps
because he arouses no sympathy in us. But could not something
be suggested in all this by the fact - quite visible, actually - that
he has an ego of a totally different kind than the ego which does
not function, precisely at the moment of his revulsion, which
fails to function immediately after this revulsion? For he
manages to become detached from himself - that’s for sure -but
afterwards, I'd say, he no longer recognizes having suffered that
beating.



- 459 -


很奇怪的是，学习写作——至少是写我的波罗米结—
—有一个作用。我将要说明的是：想象一下，在某个地方，
例如[参考“被摊平” (mise à plat)]，有一个错误，换句话说，
写作在那里制造了一个错误（下图）；这将会导致什么呢？
你肯定不会想到波罗米结有，就其本身而言，一个想象的
层面；正如你所见，环中的“I”处可以简单地解开。它溜走
了：和乔伊斯被打后的所感觉到的一样。它溜走了，想象
的关系没有位置。它在这个事例中没有位置，这让人怀疑
是否乔伊斯对倒错的强烈兴趣也许有着不同的意义。也许，
毕竟，殴打确实让他恶心；可能他不是一个真正的变态
（pervert）。因为确实很难想象乔伊斯为何如此难以阅读。
如果他如此难以阅读，那可能是因为他并没有引起我们的
同感。但是，这难道不能说明某些事吗？基于这个事实—
—实际上，很明显——即他有一个与普通人完全不同的自
我（ego），而这个自我在他厌恶的时候不起作用，在他厌恶
之后也不能立即起作用。因为他设法脱离了自己——这是
确实的——但后来，我要说的是，他不再意识到自己遭受
了那次殴打。




- 460 -


The botched link

错误的链环

So what I'm suggesting is this: that one supposes the
correction of that error, fault or lapsus here - I mark it on the
board there, one passing over the other. After all, nothing is
simpler to imagine: why should a knot not be Borromean, why
shouldn't it come undone? I have made thousands of mistakes
drawing it on the board. There is exactly what happens, when I
embody the ego here as the correction of the lacking relation, of
what does not knot itself in a Borromean way, as that which
knots together the real and the unconscious in the case of Joyce.

所以我的想法是（下图）：那个人假设了那个错误，过
错或差错的纠正——我把它标记在黑板上，一个越过另一



- 461 -

个。毕竟，没有什么比想象更简单了：为什么结不是波罗
米的，为什么它不应该被解开？我在黑板上画它时犯了数
千个错误。当我在这里把自我（ego）体现为对匮乏的关系
的纠正时，确实会发生什么事情，不是以波罗米的方式把
自己纽结在一起，而是就像在乔伊斯的例子中，他把实在
和无意识绑在一起一样。


The rectifying Ego

纠正的自我61

Through this artifice of writing, I would say that the
Borromean knot comes to be restored. As you see, it is not a

61 L10a169. 请注意，R（实在）和S（象征）这两个环是以霍普夫链环的方式
相互连接的（拉康通常避免这样），因此，严格来说，这既不是波罗米式链环，
也不是Brunnian 链环。



- 462 -

question of one of the faces of the Borromean knot, but of a
thread. Ordinary geometry is where we get the word 'face' from
- polyhedrons are full of faces, stops and summits - but the knot
(actually a chain here) presents us with a quite different
dimension, which I would describe - in contrast to the evident
quality of a geometrical face - as 'voided' [,vid,]. And because it
is voided, it is not 'evident'.

通过这种写作技巧，我可以说波罗米结得以恢复。正
如你们所见，这不是波罗米结其中的一个侧面，而是一条
线的问题。普通几何学是“面”这个词的来源——多面体充满
了面、止点和顶点——但结（实际上是一条链）为我们提
供了一个完全不同的维度，我将其描述为——与几何面的
明显性质相反——作为“空的”[vid]。因为它是空的，所以它
不是“明显的”（evident）。

Someone once asked me why I didn't say the truth about
the truth. I do not say the truth about the truth, because the truth
is that it is a lie. 'In- tension-al' truth (allow me to write it 'in-
tension', as distinct from 'ex-tension') can, from time to time,
touch on something of the real; but if it does, it is by chance.
One can never over-estimate the frequency of errors in writing.



- 463 -

The lapsus calami has no primacy over the lapsus linguae, but it
can be conceived as touching the real. I am quite clear that my
knot is that by which - and uniquely by which - the real is
introduced as such. There's no point In getting worked up about
that; It doesn't amount to all that much. I am not the only one
who handles it. Equally, I make use of it because it serves me in
explaining things to you. My fooling around, with the feeble
means I have, can certainly be tolerated - as that's exactly what
you do. But it is a way of articulating precisely this: that all
human sexuality is perverse, If we follow carefully what Freud
says. He never succeeded in conceiving that sexuality as other
than perverse, and that is the very reason I question what could
be termed the fecundity of psychoanalysis. You have heard me
very often declare this, that psychoanalysis couldn't even be
bothered to invent a new perversion. That's sad - because, after
all, if perversion is the essence of man, what a lack of fecundity
in this practice.

有一次有人问我为什么不说关于真理的真理。我不会
说关于真理的真理，因为真理，它是一个谎言。内涵的
（In- tension-al)［允许我把它写成in-tension（内在–矛盾）
正区别于'ex-tension'（外在–矛盾）］可以不时触及真实；



- 464 -

但如果是这样的话，那是偶然的。人并不能过高估计写作
出现错误的频率。笔误（lapsus-calami）并没有优先于口误
（lapsus Languae），但可以认为它触及了实在。我很清楚，
我的结是，通过它——并且是唯一的方式——实在被如此
引入。没有必要为此而激动；这算不上什么。我不是唯一
一个处理这件事的人。同样的，我利用它是因为它有助于
我向你们解释事情。我用我的小把戏乱搞，当然可以容忍
——因为这正是你们所做的。但这是明确表达这一点的一
种方式：如果我们仔细遵循弗洛伊德的话，那么所有人类
的性行为都是倒错性的。他从来没有成功地将性欲视为倒
错性之外的东西，这就是为何我质疑什么可以被称为精神
分析的多产（fecundity）。你们经常听到我这样宣称，精神
分析甚至懒得发明一种新的倒错。这是可悲的——因为，
毕竟，如果倒错是人类的本质，那么在这种实践中是多么
缺乏生产能力啊。

Well, I think that thanks to Joyce we are reaching
something I had not imagined. I had not immediately imagined
it, but it came to me with time - to consider Joyce's text, the way
it is made. It is made exactly like a Borromean knot, and what is
striking, also, Is that this totally escaped him, in other words
there is no trace of anything like it in his whole oeuvre. But that



- 465 -

seems to me, however, a sign of authenticity. Had I stopped
there, what I would have found striking, when reading the text
and especially the commentaries on it, is that not only is the
Joycean text teeming with enigmas, but it could be said that he
played on that, in the knowledge that there would be joyceans
for two or three hundred years. These people are occupied
uniquely with resolving the enigmas - namely, at least, why
Joyce put it in that way. Of course, they always find a reason -
he put it in that way because there's such-and-such a word right
after it. In the end it's exactly like my tales of os-bjet, mensionge
or dit-mension just now. In my case, there are reasons, I wish to
express something, I equivocate. But with Joyce, one always
loses what I could call his Latin (especially as he knew a bit of
Latin).

嗯，我认为多亏了乔伊斯，我们才达到了我想象不到
的程度。我并没有立即想到这一点，但随着时间的推移—
—我想到了乔伊斯的文本，它是如何制作的。它做得很像
波罗米结，而且引人注目的是，这完全没有被他发现，换
句话说，在他的整个作品中没有任何类似的痕迹。然而，
在我看来，这似乎是确实性的标志。如果我停在那里，当
我阅读文本，尤其是对文本的评论时，我会发现令人惊讶



- 466 -

的是，不仅乔伊斯的文本充满了谜团，而且可以说，他利
用了它，因为他知道乔伊斯迷们会存在两三百年。这些人
专注于解决谜团——也就是说，至少，为什么乔伊斯要那
样写。当然，他们总是会找到一个理由——他这样写，是
因为后面有一个这样或那样的词。最后，它其实就像刚才
我对于 os-bjet, mensionge（谎言的维度）和dit-mension
（言说的维度）的讲述一样。就我来说，有原因，我想要
表达某些东西，我含糊其辞。但是有了乔伊斯，人们总是
会失去我所称之为他的Latin（尤其是因为他懂一点拉丁语）。

The enigma, then, is something in which luckily I took
interest at one time. I wrote it Ee - it being a question of the
enunciation and the statement [énoncé] - and the enigma
consists in the relation of the E to the e, in other words why the
hell a particular statement has been pronounced. The
enunciantion is the enigma. When the enigma is taken to the
power of writing, it is something worth pausing over. Would this
not be the result of the sewing-up which is so badly done by an
ego whose function is enigmatic and reparatory?

幸运的是，我曾经对这个谜感兴趣。我把它写作Ee—
—这是一个话（énonciation）和说[énoncé]的问题 ，谜团在



- 467 -

于E 和e 的关系，换句话说，到底为什么一个特定的陈述会
被发音。发音是一个谜。当这个谜团被带入写作的力量时，
这股力量是值得停止（写作）的。这难道不是一个自我所
做的如此糟糕的缝合的结果吗？而这个自我的功能是神秘
的和修复性的。

That Joyce is the writer of the enigma par excellence is
what I urge upon you - I could have given you dozens of
examples if it wasn't so late - but I advise you to go and verify
this for yourselves. Ulysses exists in a French translation,
reprinted by Gallimard, if you don't have the old edition of
Sylvia Beach's day.

乔伊斯是《卓越之谜》的作者，这正是我所敦促你们
的——如果不是那么晚的话，我本可以给你们举几十个例
子——但我建议你们自己去验证一下。如果你没有《西尔
维亚·比奇的日子》的旧版本，《尤利西斯》有法文译本，由
加利马尔重印。

I'm going to indicate a few small things which seem to me
notable, before leaving you. You must be aware of what I've told
you about man's relations to his body, which entirely consist -



- 468 -

this is what I've told you - in the fact that man says that he has a
body, his body. To say 'his' is already to say that he possesses it,
like he possesses a piece of furniture, and this has nothing to do
with anything allowing a strict definition of the subject. The
subject can only be defined correctly as a signifier as it is
represented for another signifier.

在离开你们之前，我将指出一些我认为值得注意的小
事情。你一定知道我告诉过你们的关于人和他的身体的关
系，这完全存在于——这就是我告诉你们的——事实上，
人说他有一个身体，他的身体。说“他的”已经是说他拥有它，
就像他拥有一件家具一样，这与允许对主体进行严格定义
无关。主体只有被另一个能指表征时才能被正确定义为能
指。

I would also like to say something which might perhaps
even slow down a little the gulf opening in what we are able to
grasp of this perversion, by using the Borromean knot. There is
something which one is astonished to see is no longer of any use
to the body - not a body, but the body as such: that is dance. This
would allow me to write the term 'condansation' a little
differently...



- 469 -


我还想说一些事情，也许甚至可以减缓一点鸿沟的打
开，在其中我们能够掌握这个倒错，通过使用波罗米结。
人惊讶地看到一件事，它对身体不再有任何用处——不是
一个身体，而是身体本身：那就是舞蹈（dance）。这将允许
我用稍微不同的方式书写术语“condansation”（共融）……

Is the real straightforward [droit]? That is the question I'd
like to put to you today. I'd also like to point out that in Freud's
theory the real has nothing to do with the world. Because what
he explains, about something concerning precisely the ego,
namely the Lust-Ich, is that there is a stage of primary
narcissism, and that this stage is characterised, not by the
absence of a subject, but by the absence of a relation between
interior and exterior. I will certainly have to come back to this -
not necessarily before you, because after all I am not certain at
present that next year I’ll still have this amphitheatre.

实在是直截了当的[droit（直的）]吗？这就是我今天想
向你们提出的问题。我还想指出的是，在弗洛伊德的理论
中，实在与世界无关。因为他所解释的，关于自我的东西，
即Lust-Ich（快感自我），是有一个初级自恋的阶段，这个



- 470 -

阶段的特点，不是主体的缺席，而是内部和外部的关系的
缺席。我无疑必须要回到这里——不一定是在你们面前，
因为毕竟我现在还不确定明年我是否还会在这个教室上课。

I must say a few more words - it's something I've prepared-
about the 'epiphany', the famous Joycean epiphany, which one
encounters at every turn. Please note this, when he gives a list of
his epiphanies: that they are always distinguished by the same
thing, that they are the result of a mistake, namely that the
unconscious is linked to the real. It's an amazing thing, which
Joyce himself does not describe otherwise. It is absolutely
legible in Joyce that the epiphany is where, due to a mistake, the
unconscious and the real are knotted together.

我必须多说几句话——这是我准备好的——关于“顿悟”
（epiphany），即著名的乔伊斯式顿悟，人们在每一个转折
点都会遇到。当他列出他的顿悟时，请注意这一点：它们
总是以相同的东西来区分，它们是错误的结果，即无意识
与实在连接在一起。这是一件令人惊讶的事情，乔伊斯本
人并没有在其他方面描述。在乔伊斯身上绝对可以清楚地
看到，顿悟是由于一个错误，无意识和实在扭结在一起的
地方。



- 471 -


There is something - today I have been a bit slow, but that's
because I wanted to be understood - there is something I want to
draw for you here. If you have some idea of the meaning of a
Borromean knot, I'll show you this: that, if this is the ego as I
drew it for you just now, we are situated to see the Borromean
knot reconstitute itself in the following way: here is the real;
here is the imaginary; here is the unconscious; and here is
Joyce's ego. You can easily see on the schema that the rupture of
the ego sets the imaginary relation free. It is easy to imagine that
the imaginary will bugger off - if the unconscious allows it to,
and it incontestably does.

有一件事——今天我有点慢，但那是因为我想被理解
——我想在这里为你们描画一些东西（见图5）。如果你对
波罗米结的意义有一些概念，我会告诉你：如果这是我刚
才为你们画的自我，我们就可以看到波罗米结以以下方式
重建自己：这是实在；这是想象；这是无意识；这儿则是
乔伊斯的自我。你可以很容易地在图式上看到，自我的破
裂释放了想象的关系。很容易设想想象会流失（bugger off）
——如果无意识允许的话，它无疑会如此。




- 472 -


Reconstitution of the link

链环的重建

There you have what I wanted to point out in this last
session. One thinks against a signifier - that is the meaning I
gave to the word appensé - one leans against a signifier in order
to think. There you go, you are free.

这就是我想在最后一节中指出的。一个人思考倚赖一
个能指——这是我给予appensé（附加的，pensé 有思想的意
思）这个词的意思——一个人为了思考而倾向于一个能指。
到此为止了，你们自由了。

1976 年5 月11 日
